# CT-P3-045b — Validation preregistration (draft)

**FROZEN — CT-P3-045b preregistration, frozen by the merge of PR #664. Later changes are dated same-line notes or a follow-up registration (§14.21). Claude plan.**

Work item: CT-P3-045b (ROADMAP row 5, the 045b part). Protocol impact: `NONE`.
Release class: Class T development; `release_authorization` NONE.

This document is the October draft. It freezes nothing, creates no Founder
decision, and grants no signing, broadcast, funding or publication authority.
The freeze happens later, after the CT-P3-044c-1 exploratory measurements
(XD-1), FD-CT-05 and FD-CT-13, and before CT-P5-005 (ROADMAP row 5).

Labels, as in CT-P3-045a:

- **(source)** a rule quoted or paraphrased from the cited file and line;
- **(Founder)** a recorded Founder decision under `docs/claude-track/decisions/`;
- **(Claude plan)** a proposal made here. It binds later Claude-track work items
  as acceptance criteria once frozen; until then it may change. It is not a
  source rule or a Founder rule;
- **PENDING Qn** a value only the Founder can set (section 11). The draft never
  fills it, and a budget record carrying the placeholder is refused at freeze
  (045a §4.3, "empty" and "unlimited").
- **(Founder, FD-CT-92 row n)** a Qn the Founder has answered. FD-CT-92
  (decided 2026-10-27, B45 §1.1) answered Q2–Q7; each of their PENDING marks
  is replaced by the value, the bundle row and the record line. Q1 stays
  PENDING (section 11; added 2026-10-31). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C13): Q1 is decided by FD-CT-102 of 2026-11-02 (A1 :1175).*

Every number below is a proposal with its source, or a PENDING placeholder.

## 1. Retry, fee and abort rules (A-3)

### 1.1 Sources

- (source) Before the first mainnet candidate is frozen, the campaign MUST
  record retry limits, fee/exposure bounds, escalation and stop conditions,
  and the criteria for continuing after an exact-height miss. A blank budget
  or unlimited retry loop is not an authorized default (MBA:345-351).
- (source) Any party, including the miner of block 1,000,000, may take the
  opening gate; the project is a static bidder unless a fee ladder is
  pre-approved (AUD ADM-3, report section A-3; MBA:79-80).
- (Founder) FD-CT-03: frozen REGISTER rungs with different fees may be signed
  in advance at an agreed tip ≥ H0 − 144; their size and fee steps are
  preregistered here (B1:43-58). FD-CT-13: Claude drafts the numbers; the
  Founder decides only funds caps and residual-risk acceptance (B2:102-109).
- 045a fixes the conflict-set structure (§1.3), the counting rules (§2.2),
  the record format (§4.2), the refusals (§4.3) and the closed stop set
  (§4.4). This section fills the values 045a assigns to CT-P3-045b.

### 1.2 Proposed values (Claude plan unless marked PENDING or Founder)

| `max_gate_attempts` | 24 | One attempt per gate the project tries. With the Core clock moving 144 blocks per accepted birth (B3 §1; Core `+144`), 24 contested gates is about 24 days of retries after H0, well past the H0+144 review trigger (MBA:348). The row bound allows at most 172 (1.4). A smaller cap stops a griefer earlier at lower cost (1.5). |

### 1.3 Fee ladder (Claude plan; values filled at freeze) *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C15): filled in §14.21 :1876-1887.*

- Units: `r_k` is rung `k`'s target feerate (sat/kvB); `f_k` is its
  **absolute fee** (sat), as in 045a §1.3 item 5; `v` is the equal rung vsize
  (vB). All integer, checked `u64`.
- Rung 0: `r_0 = b`, the fee policy's initial feerate (FEE_POLICY §2, the
  TDS 8.10 maximum of four inputs, TDS:1135-1144); `f_0 = ceil(r_0 × v / 1000)`.
- Rung `k ≥ 1`: target `r_k = ceil(b × 3^k / 2^k)` (ratio 1.5), candidate fee
  `ceil(r_k × v / 1000)`, then
  `f_k = max(ceil(r_k × v / 1000), f_{k-1} + max(1, ceil(r_inc × v / 1000)))`,
  so the BIP125 step of 045a §1.3 item 5 always holds in absolute fees. The
  resulting feerate is `f_k × 1000 / v`. Example: `v = 213`, `b = 1,000`,
  `r_inc = 1,000`: `f_0 = 213`, rung 1 target 320 is raised to 426.
- With 8 rungs the top target is about `17 × b` (1.5^7 ≈ 17.1); the BIP125
  floor can only raise it. Every rung must
  also stay at or below `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb` and `max_fee_per_candidate_sat`;
  a ladder that would exceed either is cut at the last rung that fits (fewer
  rungs, never a higher cap).
- `v` is measured per frozen rung (045a §1.3 item 4). For sizing the draft
  uses 213 vB, the 1-input key-path REGISTER with change (AUD ADM-1); the
  freeze uses the measured value. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C15): measured 213 vB (A10 :1184).*
- `r_inc` is the larger `incrementalrelayfee` Node A and Node B declare. The
  draft does not assume a value; 044c-1 records it and rechecks it at
  broadcast (045a §1.3 item 5). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C4; A9 :1183): filled with 100 sat/kvB from Node A's read only, as at :931; FEE V1 :45-46 is read the same way.*
- **Retry at one agreed tip** (Claude plan; A #503 issuecomment-6393535560
  §2 and issuecomment-6394499878, A #509 issuecomment-6398354221 C2;
  2026-10-25, #514 merged 2026-10-26; corrected 2026-10-26 after B's fact
  check, #518 issuecomment-6406801105):
  - At one agreed tip (same height, hash and node pair), a candidate with one
    counted failure gets no further act at that tip (`FailedAttemptsAtTip`;
    `MAX_FAILED_ATTEMPTS_AT_TIP` = 1, `crates/bitagi-launch/src/machine.rs:85-96`
    at main `ec318eab`; #509, merge `5eaf97f9`). Counted failures are an
    execution or prebroadcast check failed with an ACTION_BLOCK fault (changed
    facts, or a node unavailable or disagreeing), a refused or non-final
    two-node admission (a node's `mempool min fee not met` refusal included)
    and a `SignedFinalRefused` for changed facts (`machine.rs:279-314`). A
    check failure that raises a CAMPAIGN_STOP, a submission no node accepted
    (counted as a hand-off), a failed read, a tip race and a transport error
    are not counted. A second bound, `MAX_HAND_OFFS_AT_TIP` = 2
    (`machine.rs:98-104`), allows two hand-offs (`SubmissionIntent`) at that
    tip, so a transport `Err` leaves one resend under the same authorization.
    This per-tip count is not a
    `max_gate_attempts` attempt (section 1.2). Recovery is the next rung of
    the ladder, or the next tip.
  - A ladder with only one rung (for example one the caps cut to one rung,
    bullet above) has no recovery at that tip.
  - For the Opening candidate at tip H0−1, the next tip does not recover the
    exact-height objective: at tip H0, block H0 is already mined, so a later
    inclusion is an exact-height miss under `exact_height_miss` (section 1.2)
    and section 5.3. At tip H0−1 the Opening's only recovery is its next
    rung, unless a same-height reorg or a changed node pair gives a new
    agreed tip. A replaced H0−1 block raises Reorg67, which refuses every act
    until a procedure file releases it; main cannot release that replacement
    (`crates/bitagi-launch/src/reorg.rs:257`), and PR #515 (open) adds the
    release. *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S1): PR #515 merged on 2026-10-26 (`5ce0f225`) and adds this release on Regtest only. The T67-2 case left over moved to #541; #556 (`84612637`) carries its code change. Outside Regtest the Reorg67 lock still has no release (wait registry W7; 1D3 Q17). Section 14.1.*
  - Rungs made one at a time as wallet replacements (`ReplacementOf`, the
    form the launch path uses today): reserving the next rung labels the
    previous one `Replaced` (`crates/bitagi-wallet/src/reservations.rs:8235`
    at main `ec318eab`). A 045a §1.3 conflict set reserved as one set marks
    no rung `Replaced` (`reservations.rs:8023-8026`), but no launch fixture
    or flow reserves set rows yet (the host's signing input accepts them,
    `crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/sign_input.rs:435-476`). *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S3): since D3a-1b (#645, `9f67faa4`) one Class T test flow reserves set rows: the L0 seat's set workers reserve one 045a §1.3 conflict set whole and charge every rung (`crates/bitagi-signer/tests/common/v2_writer.rs` `set_wallet_on`; `crates/bitagi-signer/tests/launch_host_e2e_sign.rs:198-205`; A #258 issuecomment-6552107427 Q-L1). That flow is test code under `crates/*/tests`. No product launch path reserves set rows, so the open item below stands. Section 14.1.*
  - Since #514 (merge `ec318eab`, finding #506) the launch core gives no act
    to a rung whose wallet label is `Replaced` (or any label other than
    `Pending` and `Stale`), or whose offline charge is revoked, and asks no
    human for its broadcast authorization (`LedgerHeld`). So no human is
    asked to approve the broadcast of an already replaced rung. One
    exception: a label-held rung charged offline, with an approval made for
    that charge and no hand-off yet, still goes through its signing path
    (checks, the human signing authorization, `Sign`) and waits at
    `OfflineSignaturePending`. Once its export is imported it is
    held, and the next rung waits (`ParentExportPending`) until that import.
  - **Open, to settle before the freeze (section 10 item 8):** with the
    replacement form, rungs reserved and signed in advance (FD-CT-03 item 2,
    section 1.1) leave only the last reserved rung broadcastable. The
    next-rung recovery at tip H0−1 therefore needs either 045a set rows on
    the launch path or a next rung reserved and signed after the refusal,
    which would fall inside `T` (section 5.1; Q6). *A decision 2026-11-02: section 14.15 (C2).*

### 1.4 Wallet-row bound check (045a §4.2)

`max_gate_attempts × max_rungs_per_conflict_set + beat_count + max_beat_replacements ≤ 1,536`

With the draft values: 24 × 8 + 60 + 100 = 352 ≤ 1,536. With `beat_count` 60
and `max_beat_replacements` 100 the largest allowed `max_gate_attempts` at 8
rungs is floor(1,376 / 8) = 172.

Separately from this static check, each set reservation checks the
ledger's actual rows and set count (045a §2.2 rule 6, #286), so a set that
is reserved and never released still uses one of the `max_gate_attempts`.

### 1.5 Griefer budget against `max_gate_attempts` (045a §6 item 3)

- (source) An adaptive griefer, including a miner, can outbid any finite
  static ladder; no fee policy guarantees a gate (AUD ADM-3; SSoT:2434-2452;
  TDS:1579 "no first-Heart promise").
- (Claude plan) The defence is a bounded loss, not a win. The worst case the
  project pays is bounded at release by the FD-CT-13 caps:
  - fee: at most `max_fee_exposure_sat` in total, at most one top rung per
    attempt (045a §2.2 rule 5);
  - principal: each mined losing rung locks its `vout[1]` Anchor amount (TDS
    8.5 default 10,000 sat, TDS:1043) under reservation; at most
    `max_principal_exposure_sat` (045a §3.2 item 2).
- With 24 attempts and every attempt lost after its top rung is mined (the
  worst case), the fee loss is at most 24 × top-rung fee and the locked
  principal at most 24 × Anchor amount. Q1 asks the Founder to cap both. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C13): Q1 is decided by FD-CT-102 of 2026-11-02 (A1 :1175).*
- The griefer's cost per captured gate is its own winning fee (AUD ADM-1
  arithmetic: about 213 vB per REGISTER). A miner pays nearly nothing for the
  opening block. So the cap is a statement of how much the project will
  spend before stopping, not a deterrent; `STOP_GATE_ATTEMPTS_EXHAUSTED`
  and `STOP_FEE_EXPOSURE_CAP` end the REGISTER campaign: REGISTER signing, release and offline charge (045a §4.4); a Heart already born keeps its BEATs (#266).

### 1.6 Disposition policy (pointer)

The disposition policy is 045a §3: retain every released rung in SEM-1, no
cancellation class, and stale sets replaced by the next gate's set reusing
their inputs (§3.4 Option B, FD-CT-18, B5 §1). 045b freezes it unchanged.
Residual-risk acceptance of 045a §3.2 is Q2; the Founder accepted it as drafted
(FD-CT-92 row 10 = Q2 (a), decided 2026-10-27, B45:75).

### 1.7 BEAT conflict sets (045a §6 item 4) — not defined (Claude plan)

SEM-1 defines no BEAT conflict set; 044b keeps refusing one (045a §1.3
item 1). Reasons:

- (source) A fixed presigned chain alone is not a fee-adaptive maintenance
  path; a pre-authorized mechanism must newly authorize each exact
  transaction or replacement under the current fee and input set
  (SSoT:1696).
- The REGISTER ladder exists because the opening block leaves one block
  interval to act (AUD XD-1; B1:43-58). A BEAT has no such window: the Core
  deadline is 2,016 blocks and the target interval 1,008 (B3 §1), so each
  BEAT replacement can be built, approved and signed when it is needed
  (FD-CT-01: no fixed wait for each REGISTER/BEAT, separate signing and
  broadcast records, B1:26).
- Each BEAT replacement is therefore its own frozen subject and approval, and
  counts against `max_beat_replacements` (SSoT:1798 "maximum
  replacement-attempt budget").

## 3. Independent time authentication (D-3, CR-13)

### 3.1 Sources

- (source) Before confirmatory data collection begins, the project MUST
  publish and hash-pin the exact release-evaluation policy and establish its
  prior existence through an authenticated, independently time-verifiable
  record. A hash created after observing results does not prove
  preregistration (SSoT:2843-2847).
- (source) A release verifier MUST compare the policy hash and reject
  undisclosed threshold changes (SSoT:2853; line corrected 2026-10-31).
- (audit) No task names a mechanism that makes the time independently
  verifiable; git commit time is author-controlled (AUD XD-5, report D-3).
- (Founder) The method and the public channel were FD-CT-17 questions Q4 and
  Q5. FD-CT-92 answered both as section 11 recommends (decided 2026-10-27;
  updated here 2026-10-31). Method (row 1, B45:61): OpenTimestamps as the
  primary witness, and the public GitHub server time with a signed tag as the
  required second witness; no OP_RETURN; the tag is signed with a
  registration-only SSH key. Channel (row 6, B45:72): the releases and issues
  of a separate public GitHub repository with no runner, plus the Class E
  notice, carrying all bytes and hashes (6.1).

### 3.2 Registration and receipt (Claude plan; schema and validator in 045b-2)

- **Registration** = the exact bytes of one preregistration file (for
  example the frozen CT-P5-005 evaluation policy, or this document's frozen
  successor). Its identity is SHA-256 of those bytes; nothing is
  re-serialized before hashing.
- **Receipt** = evidence from a time-authentication method that the
  registration hash existed at a time. Fields (method-neutral): `method`,
  `registration_sha256` (the hash the receipt covers), `receipt_sha256`
  (hash of the method's receipt bytes), `attested_time_utc` (integer
  seconds), and the method's verification result.
- **Observation record** = each confirmatory observation cites the
  `registration_sha256` it is evaluated under and carries its start time.
- **Refusals** (CR-13 acceptance, CRD:48), per witness: no receipt; a receipt
  for another hash; a required witness whose `attested_time_utc` is not
  strictly earlier than the first observation's start (every required
  witness must be earlier, not only the earliest; Q4); an OTS proof that is
  not Bitcoin-confirmed (it counts only when confirmed); re-hashing after
  observation (an observation that cites a hash other than the receipted
  one, or a registration whose only receipt is later than the first
  observation); bytes changed after the receipt.
- (Claude plan, P5-005) OTS binds the signed record (the tag object or a
  signed statement), not a bare hash. Its `attested_time_utc` is the time of
  the block header the proof binds. The registration is placed at least 24
  hours before the first observation.
- (Claude decision, A #258 issuecomment-6537782009 §3 and -6538196742 Q2;
  2026-10-31) For CT-P5-001 the registration pins the tool, schema and node
  versions and the run commands (T-5005,
  `docs/tasks/P5-005_PREOBSERVATION_REGISTRATION.md:13`). So the driver
  commit used for the confirmatory runs and A's judge are on main before the
  registration; a driver change after it needs a new registration. The first
  registration (~11-13) holds items 1 and 2 and the pool run (section 4.6);
  1e-2b-2a and D3a-1 must be merged by then. Items 3 and 4 go in a follow-up
  registration by default: same thresholds, citing the first registration's
  hash, with its own receipts under the rules above and one more Founder tag
  signature.
- Q4 and Q5 are answered (FD-CT-92 rows 1 and 6; updated 2026-10-31). A
  Class T `TEST_ONLY_RECEIPT` is never accepted for Class E. A Class E
  registration needs the row 1 witnesses: one tag in the separate public
  repository signed with the registration-only SSH key, and at least one
  Bitcoin-confirmed OTS proof over that signed tag object, each at least 24
  hours before the first observation (the receipt check,
  `tools/ct_preregistration_receipt.py` at main `bd89dd6c`). Every Class E
  freeze stays refused until section 10 item 7 holds.

---

## 4. ARDR: experiment design, schema and thresholds (D-2, D-1, CR-16)

### 4.1 Sources

- (source) Before a new Class E Profile is published, the publisher MUST issue
  an Admission Risk Decision Record satisfying items 1-7 of SSoT VI §1.2
  (SSoT:2469-2477; VII §1 item 9, SSoT:2736).
- (source) Item 6: quantitative withdrawal and NO-GO thresholds preregistered
  before the corresponding confirmatory experiment, with amendment and
  missing-data rules (SSoT:2476).
- (source) The security concern is measured by outcomes, not motive. The
  named metrics are repeat-winner concentration, winning-mining-pool
  concentration, private-order-flow share, first-BEAT failure rate,
  population realization, consecutive gates won by common control, and
  consecutive gates followed by zero accepted BEAT (SSoT:2440-2450).
- (source) Class E preregisters the subset relevant to its bounded scope and
  MUST NOT mark absent production evidence as satisfied (SSoT:2851).
- (audit) No task produces the experiments; the ARDR needs a closed schema
  with explicit NOT_ESTABLISHED states (AUD ADM-2, report D-2). Accept the
  capture and underfill risk explicitly for the one-Heart Class E scope and
  set thresholds such as consecutive common-control gate wins and consecutive
  zero-BEAT gates (AUD ADM-1, report D-1).
- Owners (CRD:51): 045b preregisters; CT-P5-001 (Breaker, Class T) runs the
  experiments; CT-P7-001 validates the ARDR against its schema and refuses any
  NOT_ESTABLISHED item.

### 4.2 Scope (Claude plan)

- All experiments are Class T: regtest and private Signet with multiple
  synthetic wallets, never mainnet (CRD:51; CR-16 "실험은 Class T로만").
- The Class E scope is one project Heart. The ARDR states that SEM-1 tests
  no population, carrying-capacity or fairness claim (AUD ADM-1 recommended
  action). Population metrics are measured to report them, not to claim them. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze; SSoT:2847 field 1, D4): the release evaluated is a new Class E Profile, launched as a new namespace at its own H0. It is not same-namespace recognition, and Class P recognition is not claimed (SSoT:2738, :2780). A manifest that changes H0 while claiming the same namespace is non-conforming (SSoT:2780).*
- Thresholds are fixed before the confirmatory run they evaluate; a run that
  started first cannot be evaluated under them (SSoT:2476, :2845).

### 4.3 Per-item table (CR-16)

Each row gives the experiment, the metric, the NO-GO threshold, the
missing-data rule and what makes the item NOT_ESTABLISHED. "NO-GO" means the
ARDR cannot state acceptance for Class E and the Profile is not published
(SSoT:2736). Numeric thresholds marked Q7 are the Founder's. FD-CT-92 row 8
(Q7 (a), decided 2026-10-27, B45:74) set every one to the Claude plan
candidate as written; withdrawal applies only in a live Class E namespace.

| Item (SSoT VI §1.2) | Experiment (CT-P5-001, Class T) | Metric | NO-GO threshold | Missing-data rule | NOT_ESTABLISHED when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. common-control and repeat-winner method | define clusters by declared synthetic-wallet ownership (ground truth known in Class T) and by on-chain heuristics (shared funding ancestry, Anchor key reuse); compare. Shared-ancestry boundary: the regtest funding source and the coinbase outputs common to all wallets are excluded, since they would link every wallet | link-based recall and precision: against the links actually placed on chain (ancestry inside the boundary, reused Anchor keys), counted over same-owner wallet pairs, with links joined by transitive closure; ownership-based recall (against declared ownership) and the unlinkable share are reported as a published limit (SSoT:2452); repeat-winner share | method not documented, or link-based recall or precision below 1.0 on the Class T data (Q7: the Claude candidate, decided by FD-CT-92 row 8). The data are fixed, so one missed or false link is a tooling defect | a gate without a recorded winner cluster counts as "unknown control", never as independent | no method document, or no Class T run with ground truth |
| 2. public mempool vs private order flow | multi-wallet gate competition on regtest. A separate miner process (not any bidder) builds every block with Bitcoin Core's own template assembly (`getblocktemplate`) under one preregistered miner policy. Public bidders broadcast through a relay node that feeds the miner's node. The private bidder hands its signed candidate only to the miner process, which keeps it in its own memory and never gives it to any node before the block (no `sendrawtransaction`, so nothing is relayed). At each block the miner process takes `getblocktemplate` from its node, adds the private set, and orders the union by one preregistered policy (descending ancestor feerate, ties by txid); the private candidate gets no other advantage. No bidder builds or orders a block. | win share by submission path; private-order-flow share | private submission wins more than 0.5 of contested gates while the public bidders' fees are at least the private bidder's (the Claude plan candidate, decided by FD-CT-92 row 8 = Q7 (a), B45:74); above it the item cannot state Class E acceptance without an explicit Founder statement under item 5 | runs with a failed node or missing relay log are reported as excluded, with count; exclusions above 10% of runs (Claude plan) make the item NOT_ESTABLISHED | fewer than the preregistered run count (section 4.6) completed *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S14): the 10% rule also counts item 2's gate exclusions `RELEASE_NOT_IN_TEMPLATE`, `RELEASE_AFTER_G`, `G_LATE` and `REPLACED_RUNG_NOT_IN_MEMPOOL` (#633 issuecomment-6549379243). Section 14.4.* *A decision 2026-11-02: section 14.15 (E13).* |
| 3. first-BEAT failure and population realization (adversarial) | accelerated regtest: zero-BEAT abandonment cohorts and uncontrollable-Anchor captures run past birth + 2,017 (AUD ADM-2); FD-CT-02 allows this in regtest | first-BEAT failure rate; population realization vs dense path; consecutive zero-BEAT gates | project Heart's own first BEAT fails in the Class T rehearsal (any failure), or the tooling misses or misplaces the zero-BEAT trigger report in a cohort (section 4.6 `item 3 cohorts`); the zero-BEAT threshold (section 4.4) is a withdrawal trigger in Class E | a cohort whose birth+2,017 height was not reached is censored and reported, not counted as success | no cohort reached birth + 2,017 *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, in Class E the zero-BEAT threshold becomes a review trigger, not a withdrawal trigger, and automatic withdrawal moves to W-C, W-P and W-DATA; ITEM3W's D-Z15, D-T, D-P, W-P and W-DATA reports are judged under this item; the cohort rule (the 15th-gate report) is unchanged; until then this row stands; see §14.18.* |
| 4. miner self-dealing, fee asymmetry, censorship, reorg replacement | test miner including its own REGISTER first at zero fee; fee-asymmetric bidders; a miner that excludes project transactions; 1-2 block reorgs replacing a winner | project win rate under each condition; outcome of reorg-replaced winners (045a §3.2 item 3) | any case where the project tooling mislabels a reorg-replaced or lost gate (a wallet defect, not a market outcome) | a scenario not run is listed as not run | any of the four sub-scenarios not run *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S10): "at zero fee" no longer holds. The self REGISTER pays the product floor fee, strictly below every honest rung 0 in G; a zero-fee REGISTER is a scenario not run, since the product signer does not sign it (A #258 issuecomment-6554792879 Q8, corrected by #643 issuecomment-6556082072 For A 1). Section 14.4.* |
| 5. trade-off acceptance | none (Founder statement) | — | Founder does not accept | — | no dated Founder statement (CRD:51 "5·7항 ≤01-21") |
| 6. thresholds preregistered | none (this table, frozen) | registration receipt time vs first confirmatory observation (section 3) | receipt missing or late (045b-2 refusals) | — | no receipt |
| 7. new admission law needed? | none (Founder statement, informed by 1-4) | — | Founder states a new law is required (SSoT:2477; then D4, not SEM-1) | — | no dated Founder statement |

Item 1 counting rules (Claude decision, A PR #616 issuecomment-6541483273
items 3-4 and A's integration check -6545256136 P3-a; 2026-10-31). They are
fixed in the frozen bytes, and A's judge uses the same rules:

- **Planted links `P_L`.** The links placed on chain are first restricted to
  the winning wallets, then closed transitively. A path the tool cannot see
  in on-chain REGISTERs (one that passes only through wallets that never won)
  is not in `P_L`; that share is the published limit (ownership-based recall
  and the unlinkable share).
- **Detected pairs `D`.** The tool's links are lifted to wallets, then closed.
  So wallet identity can join gates the tool did not link; 045b counts over
  wallet pairs on purpose.
- **Incomplete report or 0/0.** A cluster report marked `incomplete` is not
  PASS: the judge counts that run as an exclusion with its reason (the 10%
  rule, section 4.6) and gives no verdict for it. A 0/0 metric is neither
  1.0 nor 0: that metric is NOT_ESTABLISHED for that run. The item 1 design
  (40 wallets, 10 owners) always has same-owner pairs, so a 0/0 is an input
  defect.

Item 4 expected labels (Claude decision, A #258 issuecomment-6539879887 Q-b1
and Q-b7; 2026-10-31). No new outcome is added: the core's current reports
fill the item 4 NO-GO column ("mislabels a reorg-replaced or lost gate"), and
A's judge compares the tooling's labels with this table. P0 is the product
opening REGISTER (`CT-P3-044C1_1E_PREP_EN.md:567`).

| Case | Expected label |
|---|---|
| lost gate, P0 not mined | `Stale{target_gate_height: G, …}` with `opening_block_missed` true; not Done |
| lost gate, P0 mined later (or in the same block after the competitor) | `Done:IncludedNoEffect` |
| rung pushed out by a reorg before inclusion (E7) | `Stale` with `reorged_before_inclusion` true, then `IncludedNoEffect` |
| winner replaced by a reorg after winning (E9-d) | `Done:Born` (G) → `Stale` (G‴), `reorged_before_inclusion` false *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S11): the row gains the Reorg67 lock step and its Class T release (A #258 issuecomment-6557723699 Q4 (다); wording from #658 issuecomment-6565529813 For A 1). Section 14.4.* |
| E10-s: the miner puts a competitor in block G at a lower index than P0 | the competitor is born; P0 is `Done:IncludedNoEffect{G}` in the same block. The `opening_block_missed` value is read from the core and written in the row table |

E10-s follows the SSoT same-block rule: among several eligible candidates in
one block, the first fully valid one in canonical transaction order is
accepted (SSoT:334, :471, :970, I14 :1304). E10-s is part of item 4's entry
set; item 2's entry is unchanged, and A reviews the host reports of
same-block losses in item 2's EXPLORATORY runs after D3a-1 (same source).

### 4.4 D-1 thresholds (Founder Q7; the candidates, FD-CT-92 row 8)

Two thresholds are withdrawal or review triggers during the Class E namespace's
life. They are not Core rules and change no replay result (SSoT:2479). *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, the section has review rows (D-C4, D-C15, D-Z15, D-T, D-P) and withdrawal rows (W-C, W-P, W-DATA); until then it stands as written; see §14.18.*

| Threshold | Candidate (Claude recommendation) | Reasoning |
|---|---|---|
| consecutive gates won by one common-control cluster other than the project | 4 consecutive gates triggers a public disclosure update and a Founder review; 15 is the withdrawal trigger in a live Class E namespace: the project withdraws (stops publishing it as a Class E experiment and posts the withdrawal notice, CT-P7-004; default meaning below). In the Class T campaign, reaching 4 or 15 is not itself NO-GO, since section 4.6 plants runs past 15 on purpose; NO-GO is a report list that differs from the expected list (below) | 15 = the most attacker Hearts that can be CURRENT at once under full capture (2,016/144 + 1, AUD ADM-1). 4 is about four days of capture, early enough to disclose. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, 15 triggers a disclosure and a Founder review, not withdrawal (ECON-01: forceable for about 3,200 sat); withdrawal then moves to W-C, 15 consecutive opening-block gates of one strong-link cluster each with counted cost 30,000 sat; until then this row stands; the Class T expected-report rule is unchanged; see §14.18.* |
| consecutive gates followed by zero accepted BEAT | 15 (same reasoning), with the same consequence as the row above | at 15 the namespace holds no CURRENT Heart from those births (AUD ADM-1: population near 0). *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, the consequence becomes a disclosure and a Founder review (ECON-01 path B), the linkage-free withdrawal row is W-P, and the review rows D-T and D-P cover token-BEAT capture (CORE-01); until then this row stands; see §14.18.* |

(Founder) FD-CT-92 row 8 (Q7 (a), decided 2026-10-27, B45:74) adopted both
rows as written, with the Class T rules and the default withdrawal meaning
below; withdrawal applies only in a live Class E namespace. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, "as written" no longer holds for the two 15-counts' consequence (review, not withdrawal); the default withdrawal meaning is kept and applies to W-C, W-P and W-DATA; until then this paragraph stands; see §14.18.*

Class T and withdrawal rules (Claude plan; fixed in the frozen bytes):

- **Expected reports.** For each Class T run, the expected list of 4- and
  15-reports (cluster and gate) is computed from the frozen seed and inputs
  (section 4.6), once for ownership clusters and once for link-based clusters
  (section 4.3 item 1). NO-GO when the tooling's reports differ from it: a
  report missing, added, or at the wrong gate. Fixed gate positions are not
  used: under heuristic clustering the planted run's reported position
  varies by seed.
- **Unknown control, rule R3** (Claude decision, A PR #616
  issuecomment-6541483273 item 1, over B's options -6541059740; 2026-10-31).
  An unknown-control gate (section 4.3 item 1 missing-data rule) does not
  break a cluster's run and counts toward its length; that run needs at least
  one gate the cluster won. A stretch of unknown gates only that reaches 4 or
  15 is reported as cluster `UNKNOWN`, at the same thresholds and with no new
  number, so unseen capture is never read as healthy. The Class T campaign
  plants no unknown gate, so the expected list of `UNKNOWN` reports is empty;
  one that appears is a missing log, and that run counts under the 10% rule.
- **"Other than the project", reading (b)** (same source, item 2). Only the
  project's own gates are removed. The other gates of a cluster that holds a
  project gate still form runs, and a gate the project won breaks every run
  (known clusters and `UNKNOWN`). The tool still records
  `project_cluster_other_gates`. Reason: reading (a), which removes the whole
  cluster, would let one shared ancestor (such as an exchange withdrawal)
  hide another party's run in Class E; a false link should add a report,
  not remove one. The Class E project has one Heart, so it breaks a run at
  most once.
- The item 1 method (the link kinds: shared ancestry inside the boundary and
  reused Anchor keys; transitive closure; the shared-ancestry boundary of
  section 4.3 item 1) and the report rules above are fixed in the frozen
  bytes, not by the CT-P5-001 driver; the driver takes them as inputs, and the
  D2 cluster tool implements the method (A #258 issuecomment-6537782009 §3,
  2026-10-31).
- **Withdrawal, default meaning** (live Class E only; kept by FD-CT-92 row 8,
  Q7 (a)): support status `WITHDRAWN` (TDS:2430) and the CT-P7-004
  notice. Before birth, REGISTER broadcast stops (TDS §14.3, TDS:1641-1650).
  After birth there is no Core pause (TDS §14.4, TDS:1652-1659), and the
  project Heart's BEATs continue under FD-CT-05.
- Withdrawal is not a 045a §4.4 stop code that holds BEATs. If a code is
  added, it holds REGISTER rows only; this is settled before the freeze
  (target 11-12–11-13, section 10).

Amendment rule (source, SSoT:2851): a changed threshold is a new policy identity
with its reason, keeps results under the old one, and needs new confirmatory
testing; an old failing run is never relabelled as a pass.

### 4.5 Mandatory admission-market measurements (SSoT VI §1)

(source) Before activation, admission-market tests MUST measure the nine
quantities below (SSoT:2422-2432). Class E must not mark absent evidence as
satisfied (SSoT:2851), so every row is measured and reported; a row not
measured makes the ARDR NOT_ESTABLISHED. All run in the CT-P5-001 Class T
campaign of section 4.3 (the same multi-wallet regtest topology and miner
process as item 2), with the run counts of section 4.6.

| Measure (SSoT:2424-2432) | Campaign / row | Metric | Threshold | Missing-data rule | NOT_ESTABLISHED when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| candidates per open gate (SSoT:2424) | item 2 campaign | distinct REGISTER candidates seen per open gate (mean, p95, max), and the count the frozen release schedule (section 4.6) predicts for that gate | **FD-CT-92 row 8** (Q7 (a), the Claude plan candidate, decided 2026-10-27, B45:74): in any gate the observed count differs from the predicted count, which is an observation or release defect. In Class T the count itself is set by the preregistered schedule, so a market-size threshold on it would test the schedule, not the market; the distribution is reported | a gate without a complete mempool log is excluded and counted | not reported |
| mined losing candidates (SSoT:2425) | items 2 and 4 | count and share of mined REGISTERs that did not create a Heart | **FD-CT-92 row 8** (Q7 (a), the Claude plan candidate, decided 2026-10-27, B45:74): more than 25% of the project's attempted gates end with a mined losing project rung, aggregated over all attempts; at most one rung per attempt can be mined (045a §1.3), so the threshold is on the share of attempts, and above it `max_principal_exposure_sat` is consumed faster than 045a §2.2 assumes | as above | not reported |
| losing-candidate weight and fees (SSoT:2426) | items 2 and 4 | total and per-gate weight (WU) and fees (sat) of mined losing candidates | **FD-CT-92 row 8** (Q7 (a), the Claude plan candidate, decided 2026-10-27, B45:74): the project's measured losing fees per attempted gate above `max_fee_exposure_sat / max_gate_attempts`, i.e. the Q1 caps would be exceeded before the attempt cap | as above | not reported |
| replacement and cancellation traffic (SSoT:2427) | item 2 campaign (rung ladders, 045a §1.3) | replacements and cancellation attempts per gate; their share of candidate traffic | **FD-CT-92 row 8** (Q7 (a), the Claude plan candidate, decided 2026-10-27, B45:74): any project replacement refused by Node A/B `testmempoolaccept` despite meeting 045a §1.3 item 5, which is a ladder-design defect | as above | not reported |
| public-mempool versus private submission (SSoT:2428) | item 2 | as section 4.3 item 2 | as section 4.3 item 2 (FD-CT-92 row 8) | as section 4.3 | as section 4.3 |
| repeat-winner concentration (SSoT:2429) | item 1 | share of gates won by the top cluster; longest run | the section 4.4 thresholds (FD-CT-92 row 8). In Class T, NO-GO when the tooling's 4- and 15-reports differ from the expected list computed from the frozen seed (section 4.4); reaching 4 or 15 is not itself NO-GO. Withdrawal applies only in a live Class E namespace | as item 1 | as item 1 *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, in Class E the section 4.4 counts are review triggers and withdrawal is by W-C; ITEM3W's D-C and W-C reports are judged under this row, with 0 exclusions; until then this row stands; see §14.18.* |
| winning mining-pool concentration (SSoT:2430) | item 4 extended: three miner processes with preregistered hash shares (50/30/20) | Herfindahl index of winning-block producers over gates | **FD-CT-92 row 8** (Q7 (a), the Claude plan candidate, decided 2026-10-27, B45:74): Herfindahl index above 0.5, against the 0.38 the preregistered shares imply | a block without a recorded producer is excluded and counted | not reported |
| miner self-dealing scenarios (SSoT:2431) | item 4 | as item 4 | as item 4 | as item 4 | as item 4 |
| reorganization-replaced winners (SSoT:2432) | item 4 | count and outcome of winners replaced by 1-2 block reorgs | as item 4 | as item 4 | as item 4 |

Every row has a NO-GO threshold. Where no source gives a Class E value, the
threshold is a Claude candidate; FD-CT-92 row 8 (Q7 (a), decided 2026-10-27,
B45:74) adopted every candidate as written. The freeze refuses any PENDING
value (section 10), so no row can stay discretionary after collection.

### 4.6 Run counts and stopping rule (Claude plan; fixed at freeze)

SSoT VII §6 requires sampling and stopping rules before confirmatory
collection (SSoT:2847). Each count below is a field the freeze fills; a
frozen policy with any count unset is refused.

| Experiment | Proposed fixed count |
|---|---|
| item 1 (ground-truth clustering) | 200 contested gates |
| item 2 and section 4.5 rows on that campaign | 100 contested gates per submission condition (private present / absent) |
| item 3 (abandonment cohorts) | 30 cohorts, each run past birth + 2,017 *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, a ninth experiment, ITEM3W, adds 19 runs, one per plant, with 0 exclusions, in the item 3 and item 4 follow-up registration; the 30 item-3 cohorts are unchanged; until then this row stands; see §14.18.* |
| item 4 (each of four sub-scenarios) and the pool-concentration run | 50 runs each |

Inputs fixed with the counts (Claude plan; SSoT VII §6 requires sampling, fault intensity and stopping rules before confirmatory collection, SSoT:2847; all values in the frozen bytes):

| Input | Fixed rule |
|---|---|
| randomness | one 32-byte seed in the frozen policy. Draw `k` of choice `label` in run `r` of experiment `e` is `D = SHA-256(seed ‖ e ‖ 0x00 ‖ u32be(r) ‖ label ‖ 0x00 ‖ u32be(k))`, with `e` and `label` the exact ASCII strings of the draw table below and `k` starting at 0 for every choice. An integer uniform in `[0, n)` takes `x` = the first 8 bytes of `D` as big-endian `u64` and accepts `x mod n` only when `x < floor(2^64 / n) × n`; otherwise it retries with `k + 1` (rejection sampling, unbiased). Every distribution below uses this mapping |
| item 1 ownership clusters | 40 synthetic wallets, 10 synthetic owners; wallet-to-owner assignment uniform at random; each wallet shares funding ancestry within its owner with probability 30% |
| item 1 gate winners | the 200 contested gates are numbered 0 to 199. In gates 100 to 119 the winner is a wallet of the planted owner (the owner with the most wallets, lowest owner index on ties), with no random draw: gate `100 + j` goes to that owner's wallet at position `j mod m` in ascending wallet order, where `m` is the owner's wallet count. Since 40 wallets fall to 10 owners, `m ≥ 4`, so the run uses every one of the owner's wallets, at least 4 distinct wallets, and no two consecutive gates share a wallet: a planted 20-gate common-control run across distinct wallets, longer than the section 4.4 threshold of 15. In every other gate the winner is drawn uniformly among all 40 wallets. The winner's REGISTER is the one the test miner includes; nothing else decides a winner |
| item 1 Anchor keys | every Anchor key is a deterministic TEST_ONLY key. An owner's base key is the key of its lowest-indexed wallet's first Heart. A wallet reuses its owner's base key for every Anchor with probability 30%; otherwise each of its Hearts gets a fresh key. No key is shared across owners |
| item 1 seat | (Claude decision, A #258 issuecomment-6543158220 Q1; 2026-10-31) one public seat per gate, held by the planted or drawn winner, whose rung 0 pays feerate `b`. Item 1 measures the winner's cluster, and losing candidates are not in its ground truth. A campaign with losing candidates mixed in (whether their funding makes false links) is an EXPLORATORY variant only, not a CONFIRMATORY row |
| item 2 bidders and fees | 5 bidders per gate (one private when present); each bidder's rung-0 feerate `p_0` an integer uniform in [b, 4b] sat/kvB; condition allocation alternates private present / absent by run index |
| item 2 project seat | (Claude decision, A #258 issuecomment-6537782009 §2 Q2 and §3, -6538196742 Q2, -6543158220 Q4; 2026-10-31) default (ㄱ): one of item 2's public bidders is the L0 project bidder (the launch host `run_l0`, a new deployment per run), and the three section 4.5 rows that count project attempts and replacements (mined losing candidates, losing-candidate weight and fees, replacement traffic) are measured in item 2. Item 4's four sub-scenarios are all adversarial, so measuring these rows only there would exceed the 25% threshold of the mined-losing row by design. FD-CT-92 row 8's values and wording do not change. Fallback (ㄷ): if the 11-10 throughput measurement shows that about 200 L0 launches do not fit the confirmatory window, those three rows apply to every ladder; this changes the scope of the values the Founder accepted, so the Founder gets a one-line note before the freeze. A settles (ㄱ) or (ㄷ) on 11-10; which public seat holds L0 is set by A at the freeze, with D3a-1 *A decision 2026-11-02: section 14.15 (F2).* |
| item 2 release schedule | each bidder releases rung 0 when the G−1 block arrives (public bidders at the relay node, the private bidder to the miner process) and rung `k ≥ 1` exactly `60 × k` seconds later, while block G has not been produced. Rung fees are absolute and follow the section 1.3 formula with `p_0` in place of `b`: `f_0 = ceil(p_0 × v / 1000)`, target `r_k = ceil(p_0 × 3^k / 2^k)`, `f_k = max(ceil(r_k × v / 1000), f_(k−1) + max(1, ceil(i × v / 1000)))`, with `i` the nodes' `incrementalrelayfee` (sat/kvB) and `v` the equal measured vsize of the campaign's candidates (vB). So every rung meets the 045a §1.3 item 5 increment in absolute fees and is a conforming BIP125 replacement of the one before. A bidder's ladder ends before the first rung with `f_k × 1000 > 4b × v` (feerate above `4b`) or after rung 7, whichever comes first. The miner process produces block G exactly `t_G` seconds after the G−1 block, with `t_G` drawn (draw table). The predicted candidate count of a gate is the number of rungs whose release time is strictly before `t_G`, summed over bidders; rung 0 is always counted because `t_G ≥ 1` *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S12): rung 0 is released when the G−1 block has reached all four nodes, not only the relay node (#633 issuecomment-6549379243 For A 3). Section 14.4.* |
| item 2 clock and release check | (Claude decision, A #258 issuecomment-6543158220 Q5; 2026-10-31; the reading of the row above) one runner's monotonic clock. `t0` is the time the miner process produced the G−1 block; rung 0 is released when the G−1 block reaches the relay node, and rung `k ≥ 1` at `t0 + 60 × k`. So the released rungs (`60 × k < t_G`) and the predicted candidate count use the same clock, and a rung `k ≥ 1` precedes block G by at least 1 s (`t_G` and `60 × k` are whole seconds). The presence check reads only each bidder's latest released rung, since every rung spends the same inputs and replaces the one before it (045a §1.3 item 2), so earlier rungs cannot be in the template: a public bidder's latest released rung must be in block G's template, and the private bidder's must be in the private set the miner process adds. If one is missing, the gate is EXCLUDED (`RELEASE_NOT_IN_TEMPLATE`): it counts under the 10% rule and is left out, and counted, in the section 4.5 first row's predicted-versus-observed comparison. Earlier released rungs are observed candidates checked against the relay log, not the template (A's reading of 6543158220 Q5 "a released rung missing from the template", made precise 2026-10-31 after Codex review r4432487623) *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S12): "reaches the relay node" reads "has reached all four nodes" (#633 issuecomment-6549379243 For A 3). Section 14.4.* |
| item 3 cohorts | 30 cohorts by cohort index: even indices are zero-BEAT abandonment cohorts, odd indices are uncontrollable-Anchor capture cohorts (15 each). Each cohort covers 17 consecutive gates. The project wins the first gate and runs its first BEAT under the frozen first-BEAT operations; a synthetic winner takes each of the other 16, so every qualifying cohort has 16 consecutive gates followed by zero accepted BEAT and crosses the section 4.4 threshold of 15. In every cohort the tooling must report the zero-BEAT trigger at the 15th such gate; a missed or early report is a tooling defect and NO-GO, like the item-4 mislabel rule. In an abandonment cohort the synthetic winner never submits a BEAT; in a capture cohort it registers an Anchor whose key no participant holds (a TEST_ONLY provably unspendable output), so no BEAT is possible. A cohort qualifies when all 17 births happen at their planned gates; a cohort with a missed birth is reported and replaced by no other cohort (it counts toward the exclusion rule below) *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, each cohort also expects a W list computed from the plan, which states each synthetic winner's fee (empty at rung-0 fees while b ≤ 70,417 sat/kvB); D-T and D-P are recorded here and judged only in ITEM3W; until then this row stands; see §14.18.* |
| item 2 qualifying sample | order-constrained by construction: in the first 50 private-present runs the private fee is drawn first in [b, 2b] and every public fee in [private fee, 4b], so all 50 qualify (every public fee is at least the private fee); the other 50 private-present runs are unconstrained. Fewer than 50 qualifying runs completed makes item 2 NOT_ESTABLISHED *A decision 2026-11-02: section 14.15 (E13).* |
| item 4 fee asymmetry | the asymmetric bidder pays 10× the median honest feerate |
| item 4 censorship | the censoring miner excludes project transactions for 144 consecutive blocks per run |
| item 4 reorgs | depth 1 in half the runs, depth 2 in the other half, by run index *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S15): the rule is depth 1 for even run index `r` and depth 2 for odd `r`; a depth-d run replaces d blocks from G with d + 1 blocks (A #258 issuecomment-6554792879 Q6, -6551547851 Q5). Section 14.4.* |
| block producer (pool run) | each block's producer drawn by the seeded generator with weights 50/30/20 |
| pool run gates and seat | (Claude decision, A #258 issuecomment-6543158220 Q3; 2026-10-31) 4 gates per run (50 runs, 200 gates) and one public synthetic seat at feerate `b`. One miner node runs the three miner processes, and each marks its blocks with a producer tag in the coinbase; the judge reads the producer from the block bytes. A block with no tag or more than one is excluded and counted (section 4.5 missing-data rule). The Herfindahl index is computed once over the block-G producers of all pool gates; per-run values are descriptive only and not judged |
| unit of a run and of an exclusion | (Claude decision, A #258 issuecomment-6543158220 Q2-Q4; 2026-10-31) item 1: a gate inside its one 200-gate run; if the job dies at gate `g`, gates `g` to 199 are EXCLUDED and no run replaces them, so `g < 180` makes item 1 NOT_ESTABLISHED. Item 2: one run is one contested gate, `r` = 0 to 199; even `r` is private present, of which `r` = 0, 2, …, 98 are the 50 order-constrained runs; odd `r` gives `BID_FEE_0` … `BID_FEE_4` to five public seats; the seats are W00–W04, bidder 0 being private when present. Pool: a run of 4 gates. Items 3 and 4 keep A's default (the cohort; the run), which the freeze sets (A -6537782009 §3) *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S16): items 3 and 4 keep the cohort and the run as their units; the pool's unit is the run of 4 gates and its 10% rule counts gates; an item-1 job that dies at gate `g` counts gates `g` to 199 as missing under the 10% rule (A #258 issuecomment-6549827052 Q2, -6557890079 decision 6). Section 14.4.* |

Draw table (every random choice; nothing else is random): *A decision 2026-11-02: section 14.15 (F9).*

| Experiment `e` | Choice `label` | Range `n` and use |
|---|---|---|
| `ITEM1` | `OWNER_W00` … `OWNER_W39` (wallet index, two digits) | `n = 10`: owner of that wallet |
| `ITEM1` | `ANCESTRY_W00` … `ANCESTRY_W39` | `n = 100`: shares ancestry when the value is below 30 |
| `ITEM1` | `KEY_REUSE_W00` … `KEY_REUSE_W39` | `n = 100`: reuses the owner's base Anchor key when the value is below 30 |
| `ITEM1` | `WINNER_G000` … `WINNER_G199` (gate number, three digits) | gates 0 to 99 and 120 to 199 only (gates 100 to 119 are fixed by the `item 1 gate winners` row): `n = 40`, winner = wallet `W<value>` |
| `ITEM2` | `BID_FEE_0` … `BID_FEE_4` (bidder 0 is the private bidder when present) | `n = 3b + 1`: feerate `b + value` (unconstrained runs) |
| `ITEM2` | `PRIVATE_FEE` | `n = b + 1`: private feerate `b + value` (the 50 order-constrained runs) |
| `ITEM2` | `PUBLIC_FEE_1` … `PUBLIC_FEE_4` | `n = 4b − p + 1` for private feerate `p`: public feerate `p + value` (the 50 order-constrained runs) |
| `ITEM2` | `GATE_INTERVAL` | `n = 1,200`: `t_G` = value + 1 seconds (uniform in [1, 1,200]) |
| `POOL` | `PRODUCER_H` followed by the block height in decimal | `n = 100`: producer A below 50, B below 80, else C |

Item 3 and the four item-4 sub-scenarios use no random draw; their fixed
parameters are the `item 3 cohorts` and `item 4` rows of the input table above. *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S13): item 4's fee-asymmetry runs do draw (`BID_FEE_2` … `BID_FEE_4` of `ITEM4_FEE_ASYMMETRY`). The sentence reads: "Item 3 and item 4's self-dealing, censorship and reorg runs use no random draw. Item 4 fee asymmetry uses the draw row below" (A #258 issuecomment-6554792879). Section 14.4.* *A decision 2026-11-02: section 14.15 (F9).*

Percentile estimator (every p50 and p95 in this document): nearest rank.
For `N` values sorted ascending, `pq` is the value at 1-based rank
`ceil(q × N / 100)`, with no interpolation. Durations are whole seconds,
each measured duration rounded up. Example: for 20 values, p95 is the 19th
and p50 the 10th.

Stopping rule: fixed N, no early stopping, no extension after seeing
results. Excluded runs are counted and reported; exclusions above 10% of an
experiment's runs make that experiment NOT_ESTABLISHED (section 4.3 item 2
rule applied to every row).

Re-dispatch (Claude decision, A #258 issuecomment-6537782009 §3;
2026-10-31): each unit is dispatched once. Only a unit whose slice record
shows it never started may be dispatched again. A unit with any recorded
observation ends as PASS, FAIL or an exclusion, and an exclusion counts
under the 10% rule.

## 5. S17-PREP criteria owned here (XD-1, BTC-4, IMPL-OPS-1)

(source) S17-PREP criteria, tools and scope MUST be preregistered before the
confirmatory collection they evaluate; synthetic and Class T results keep
their labels; Signet fees do not establish mainnet fee survivability
(MBA:252-279).

### 5.1 XD-1: post-H0−1 timing

- (source) The whole post-H0−1 pipeline sits in one block interval; the
  chance the interval outlasts a T-minute pipeline is at most `exp(-T/10)`
  (AUD XD-1). FD-CT-03 moved signing before H0−1 (B1:43-58); FD-CT-03a adds the
  H0−1 Rust/Python prestate comparison and says a pipeline over about 7
  minutes re-raises A-1 to MAJOR (B2:92-100).
- (Claude plan) **Metric**: `T` = seconds from the H0−1 block's first arrival
  at either node to the broadcast call returning on both nodes, covering the
  FD-CT-03 step 5 list: prestate derivation and comparison, input/gate
  rechecks, signed-final validation, Node A/B `testmempoolaccept`, the human
  broadcast record, broadcast.
- **Runs**: exactly 20 timed confirmatory runs in the 044c-1 two-node
  Regtest (044c-1 A7), each with the human broadcast decision performed live.
  They run only after the frozen policy's registration receipt (section 3),
  under the frozen bytes. Fixed N, no early stopping and no extension
  (section 4.6 stopping rule). Runs 044c-1 makes before the freeze are
  labelled `EXPLORATORY`: they inform the design and the vsize of `v`, and
  they are not S17-PREP evidence.
- **Criterion**: p95 of `T` ≤ 420 s (7 minutes, B2:99), with the nearest-rank
  estimator of section 4.6 (the 19th of 20 sorted values). The criterion
  is frozen; the measured `T` distribution is a post-freeze result, not a
  frozen field.
- **Failure**: p95 > 420 s re-raises A-1 (XD-1) to MAJOR and goes to the
  Founder (FD-CT-03a). The Class E notice states the single-interval
  structure and the success probability `exp(-p50 / 600)` either way, with `p50` in seconds (the 10-minute mean block interval is 600 s) (AUD XD-1
  option b). *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): this criterion and its A-1 rule cover `T`, the H0−1 path, only; a REGISTER broadcast at tip ≥ H0 has a separate Class T bound, and :610 is unchanged; see §14.19 item 8.*
- **Missing data**: a run without a complete timestamp chain counts as
  `T = ∞` (a failure), never excluded.
- **Event lines for two run shapes** (Claude plan, B42 §3.4; 2026-10-25;
  citations corrected 2026-10-26 after B's fact check, #518
  issuecomment-6406801105):
  - L0, the path XD-1 runs take (`FD-CT-BATCH40_2026-10-23.md:150`, Q-B3):
    the candidate is signed before H0−1 and its retained bytes are reused.
    Step numbers are those of the 044c-1 H0−1 step list
    (`CT-P3-044C1_PLAN_EN.md:151-195`), not FD-CT-03 item 5. At H0−1 step 6
    is that reuse, so before the `RecordBroadcastObservation` act the host
    writes `STEP_6_START`, `STEP_6_END` and `STEP_7_START`, in that order, and
    `STEP_7_END` after its record (#495;
    `crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/events.rs:297-301` (doc) and `:346-361`
    at main `ec318eab`).
    This fits the metric above: signing moved before H0−1, and `T` covers
    only the post-H0−1 path.
  - A run whose broadcast (step 10) returned a transport `Err`: a retry in
    the same process writes no second `STEP_10_START`. If a new process sends
    it again, that process writes `STEP_10_START` again, so the run's chain
    holds two and the V1 event-chain check
    (`docs/claude-track/formats/XD1_EVENT_CHAIN_V1.md` §5) reports
    `EVENT_DUPLICATED`.
- **Runs counted as `T = ∞`** (Claude plan, A #258 issuecomment-6392274893
  §2; 2026-10-25; corrected 2026-10-26 after B's fact check, #518
  issuecomment-6406801105): these are cases of the missing-data rule above. Each is a
  failed run; the criterion fails only when the p95 exceeds 420 s.
  - A run in which the H0−1 block was reorganized to another block at the
    same height (whether its Reorg67 lock stayed raised or was released), and
    a run re-sent by a new process after a transport `Err`, count as
    `T = ∞`. They are neither excluded nor split into two records. The
    section 4.6 exclusion clause does not apply to XD-1, whose missing-data
    rule above comes first (one run, one record: `run_index` and the declared
    N).
  - With the nearest-rank p95, one `T = ∞` among 20 runs takes rank 20, so
    the p95 (rank 19) is the largest finite `T` of the other 19 runs; two make
    the p95 infinite and fail the criterion. Counting a run as `T = ∞` can
    only raise the p95, so this rule can give a false FAIL but never a false
    PASS.
  - The V1 checks (`XD1_EVENT_CHAIN_V1.md` §5, `tools/claude_track_xd1_check.py`)
    give the re-sent run `T = ∞` (`EVENT_DUPLICATED`), and a run cut short
    `BASE_EVENT_MISSING`. A new process's first pass at the new H0−1 block
    writes `S2` and `STEP_1_START` again, so a reorganized run re-run by a new
    process is `EVENT_DUPLICATED` too (#471; the lines are main's,
    `events.rs:328-336`; PR #515, still open, lets the run reach the new
    block and tests them). For these no
    run-record field, event-line kind, checker change, V1 format change or
    host-store `FORMAT` change is needed. The checker computes no percentile
    (V1 §6); the p95 over its rows counts each null `decision_ns` as ∞. *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S2): PR #515, called still open at :661, merged on 2026-10-26 (`5ce0f225`). Section 14.5.*
  - **Open, to settle before the freeze (section 10 item 8):** a process
    that continues over a reorganized H0−1 block writes no line it has
    written itself (`events.rs:15-18`). The run's lines are split between two
    processes: the first pass writes `S2` and `STEP_1_START`, the compared
    pass the rest (`events.rs:305-311`). If the process that wrote the run's
    first pass continues, its chain has no second `S2` or `STEP_1_START` and
    can pass V1 with a finite `T`; whether a continuing compared-pass process
    writes them depends on whether its turn still carries the comparison
    (`crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/turn.rs:2099`). The XD-1 driver (1e-4b)
    must record such a run so that it counts as `T = ∞`. A decides the means
    in the 1e-4b unit.
  - **Settled (2026-10-26 15:5xZ, Claude plan, A #258 issuecomment-6419729708):** the
    1e-4b driver reads the H0−1 block hash on both nodes three times: after
    the H0−1 `generate` returns, after `S2` is stamped and before the ack,
    and after `I` before the record. Any difference among the six values, or
    a second `run_prestate` at a different height in one run, records
    `stop_reason: H0_MINUS_1_CHANGED` with `T = ∞`. The field and the value
    are XD-1 event chain V2's, written in 1e-4b; V1 is not changed.

### 5.2 BTC-4: relay assumptions and the two carriers

- (source) S17-PREP requires declared supported version, configuration and
  relay assumptions (MBA:263-266). The REGISTER carrier is 50 bytes and the
  BEAT carrier 42 bytes (SSoT:1530-1531, per AUD BTC-4). The release needs a
  relay-policy compatibility statement (SSoT:2798).
- (Claude plan) The relay matrix lists, for each carrier, acceptance by:
  Bitcoin Core 30.x and 31.x defaults (the supported matrix); Bitcoin Knots
  defaults before and after the 83-byte default change (AUD BTC-4 cites
  v29.1 = 42, v29.2+ = 83); and a node configured with a 42-byte
  `datacarriersize`. Expected: both carriers relay under Core defaults and
  Knots ≥ v29.2; the 50-byte REGISTER is refused at a 42-byte limit; the BEAT
  sits exactly at it.
- Evidence: Class T `testmempoolaccept` per row on Regtest. A Knots row that
  cannot be run is reported as not run, never as PASS.
- Fallback declared in advance: direct submission to a willing miner, as a
  separately recorded broadcast decision (AUD BTC-4 recommended action). It
  does not bypass any check.
- The Class E notice carries the matrix (CT-P7-004).

### 5.3 IMPL-OPS-1: retry after a missed opening block

- (source) While tip ≥ G and the gate is unchanged, a retry for the open gate
  must be signable; a missed opening block must not leave the project unable
  to register (AUD IMPL-OPS-1; B1:58 step 6; MBA:340-342).
- (Claude plan) S17-PREP case "opening block missed, then retry", on two-node
  Regtest:
  1. a competing or absent REGISTER leaves gate G open at tip G;
  2. the project builds, freezes, approves and signs a fresh set targeting G
     with `nLockTime` = construction height;
  3. it is admitted by both nodes and mined at G + k, and replay records the
     actual birth height (never relabelled as G);
  4. the same with a competing birth at G first: the stale set is refused for
     signing and replaced under Option B (045a §3.4).
- Pass: all four steps behave as stated; the birth height recorded equals the
  inclusion height. *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): once the code under test has the §14.19 REGISTER gate, this case runs with RM's comparator, and step 2 signs and step 3 broadcasts only on a MATCH chain through its own tip; see §14.19 item 10.*

## 6. First-BEAT operations, BEAT count and budget record changes (CR-17, FD-CT-05)

### 6.1 Operations section (pointer)

`docs/claude-track/preregistration/FIRST_BEAT_OPERATIONS_DRAFT_EN.md` holds *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C19): frozen as `FIRST_BEAT_OPERATIONS_V1_EN.md`, SHA-256 `bbac8f48…` (§14.21 :1893, :1895).*
the CR-17 section: roles and time windows only, no identities (CRD:52;
MBA:359-370).

### 6.2 BEAT count (Founder Q3)

- (Founder) Maintain until block 1,050,000, about weekly (1,008 blocks),
  about 50 BEATs, then by default stop and let the Heart time out; the exact
  preregistered count and the BEAT replacement sentence were not approved
  (B3 §1, :21-38).
- (Founder) FD-CT-92 row 2 (Q3 (a), decided 2026-10-27, B45:62): the formula
  below is frozen, with `beat_count` 60 and `max_beat_replacements` 100
  (added 2026-10-31).
- (Claude plan, from B3 §1) Let `F` be the first BEAT height. The scheduled
  later BEATs are `n = floor((1,050,000 − F) / 1,008)`; the reserve count is
  `n + 1`. At `F = 1,000,000` that is 49 + 1 = 50.
- The first BEAT height is unknown at freeze, so the draft proposes freezing
  the formula **and** a fixed `beat_count` for the budget record and the fee
  reserve. Claude recommendation: `beat_count = 60`. The plan is about 50
  BEATs by the formula; 60 is the cap. If the actual `F` makes `n + 1`
  lower, the schedule uses the formula and the reserve keeps 60. A later `F`
  only lowers the count needed, so the cap still holds and nothing needs to
  be asked again (the freeze comes before H0, so the B3:37 re-check cannot
  arise as written).
- Why not 50 (Claude plan): since #279 `beat_count` is a release cap over the
  ledger's whole life, and going over it stops every BEAT of every Heart
  (`crates/bitagi-wallet/src/release_budget.rs:636-705` at main `1eed6b00`;
  045a §4.4). 50 equals
  the formula's maximum, so it leaves no margin. SSoT:1818 asks for routine
  continuation no later than `heartbeat_age = 1,008`, and the Founder runs the
  BEATs alone (FD-CT-53), so a BEAT before an absence comes early and the
  mean interval falls below 1,008.
- Margin (estimates; `F` ≈ 1,000,036, the W1 target `B + 36`): staying
  CURRENT at 1,050,000 needs a last BEAT at or above 1,047,984, so the slots
  needed are `1 + ceil(47,948 / mean interval)`.

  | Cap | Holds for a mean interval of | Extra reserve tied up (estimate) |
  |---|---|---|
  | 50 | 979 blocks or more | — |
  | 55 | 888 blocks or more | 5 BEATs |
  | 60 | 813 blocks or more | 10 BEATs: 263 vB × 10–300 sat/vB ≈ 0.0003–0.008 BTC |

  Reserve tied up is not spent; the spend is the BEATs actually made. The
  BEAT spend bound `beat_count × max_fee_per_candidate_sat` rises with the
  cap. If the Founder reads 60 as outside FD-CT-05's "about 50", the fallback
  is 55.
- `max_beat_replacements`: recommended 100, above the TDS 8.10 floor of 20
  (TDS:1151), inside the row bound (1.4). Under the geometric escalation of
  the fee policy §5, one BEAT reaches the feerate cap (at least the top
  REGISTER rung, about 17 × the freeze-time REGISTER `b`) in about 7 replacements
  when its own `b` equals that REGISTER `b`, and in about 10–13 when its `b`
  is 3–11 times lower, and use over a year is about 10–50 (estimates). The reserve *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C23): at the freeze the binding rate is F_B = 114,068 sat/kvB (19 × `b` at `b` 6,000; FEE V1 :119), reached in about 8 rounds at ratio 1.5 (1.5^7 ≈ 17.1 < 19.01 < 1.5^8; estimate); `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb` is 1,464,000 (:1179).*
  model sums every replacement rung, each above the last
  (`crates/bitagi-wallet/src/fee_policy.rs:1330-1369`), so the reserve grows
  faster than the count: under the current linear ladder 200 would tie up
  about 0.007–0.04 BTC more (estimate). The 11-03 numbers draft shows the 100
  and 200 reserves. *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S4, S5): `max_beat_replacements` is 100, decided (FD-CT-92 row 2, B45:62). The reserve's replacement ladder is fee policy §5's `Step` rule (A #258 issuecomment-6545342828), so the linear-ladder estimate for 200 no longer applies, and the numbers draft shows n = 100 only. Section 14.6.*

### 6.3 Budget record changes during the campaign (Claude plan)

This is Claude's reading, not a Founder decision.

- A new budget record that only raises BEAT caps (`beat_count`,
  `max_beat_replacements`, or the per-candidate cap the BEATs use) is a
  disclosed operational change. It has a new record hash and a stated
  reason, results under the prior record are retained, and it is posted on
  the Q5 channel. The Founder still approves its exact
  `budget_record_sha256` (045a §4.2).
- It changes no preregistered threshold, so it needs no new confirmatory
  test. The frozen 045b states this rule as the scope-change process the
  policy must name (SSoT:2847); such a change is not a data-informed
  revision (SSoT:2851).
- A change that alters a section 4 threshold, or a value a section 4.5
  threshold uses, is a revision under SSoT:2851: new policy identity, reason,
  retained results and new confirmatory testing. For example, a higher
  per-candidate cap that also needs a higher `max_fee_exposure_sat` (045a
  §4.2: exposure ≥ per-candidate) changes the losing-fee row's
  `max_fee_exposure_sat / max_gate_attempts`.
- The wallet moves its pin only through `clear_budget_stop`, which clears an
  open budget stop (`crates/bitagi-wallet/src/reservations.rs:8980-8983`,
  `:9069-9072`, at main `1eed6b00`). A budget stop can be raised on purpose
  first (`raise_stop`, `reservations.rs:9017`), so a re-pin need not wait for
  a cap to be used up. The first-pin and re-pin operating commands are not
  built yet (Q2). *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S6): #583 (merged 2026-10-29, `37fee9b0`) built the host's first pin and re-pin (`LaunchHost::pin_budget`, `LaunchHost::repin_budget`; first-BEAT operations §4 row 98). A binary command comes with the unit that adds a turn runner. Section 14.6.*
- Open, to settle before freeze: whether such a change needs a new CR-13
  receipt (section 3) or the 43,200 s deliberation (B1:25). *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S7): FD-CT-95 removed the 43,200 s deliberation from this track (B46 §1.1), so that half is answered. The CR-13 receipt half stays open (section 14.Z). Section 14.6.* *A decision 2026-11-02: section 14.15 (S7).*

## 7. CN-01 residual risk (disclosure for Founder acceptance)

(Founder-facing text, Claude plan; 044c-1 A20 and Q1, main decision 2026-10-04.)

A REGISTER rung signed in advance carries `nLockTime = G − 1`. If the G−1 block
is reorganized away before the rung is included, the rung can still be mined in
a later block where its gate has moved, and then it pays its fee and creates no
Heart (NO_EFFECT). 044c-1 detects this and marks the attempt
`REORGED_BEFORE_INCLUSION`; it reports a NO_EFFECT inclusion and never
relabels it. The loss is already inside the counted exposure (045a §2.2: the
rung counts from release), so it does not need a new cap. It is part of the
residual-risk acceptance in Q2 together with 045a §3.2, which the Founder
accepted (FD-CT-92 row 10 = Q2 (a), decided 2026-10-27, B45:75).

---

## 8. G5-02: broadcast lock and incident record

### 8.1 Sources

- (source) Reorganization: stop wallet construction and broadcast, roll back,
  replay, compare Rust/Python, restore only after Node A/B agreement
  (TDS:851-864, §6.7).
- (source) A critical-height disagreement is an automatic broadcast lock and
  NO-GO condition until resolved (TDS:880, §6.8).
- (source) An import/restore failure returns `REPLAY_STATE_ERROR`, locks wallet
  broadcast and opens an incident (TDS:948-961, §7.5).
- Owners (044c-1 A12): 044c-1 implements the lock; CT-P6-003/004 rehearse it;
  045b and CT-P5-005 own the record format.

### 8.2 Incident record (Claude plan)

One UTF-8 JSON object, strict parse as in 045a §4.2 (no duplicate, unknown or
missing keys; integers only; lowercase 64-hex), hashed as exact bytes. No
secret material, no key, descriptor, RPC credential or signed mainnet
transaction appears in it (AGENTS.md §9); transactions are named by txid.

| Field | Type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `schema` | string | `"BITAGI_CT_BROADCAST_LOCK_INCIDENT_V1"` |
| `incident_id` | string | `INC-` + 8 decimal digits, unique per deployment; shared by the open and release versions of one incident |
| `record_version` | integer | `1` the open record, `2` the release record of the same `incident_id` |
| `supersedes_sha256` | 64 hex or omitted | in version `2` only: SHA-256 of the exact version-`1` bytes it releases |
| `release_class` | enum | `CLASS_T` \| `CLASS_E` |
| `substrate` | enum | `REGTEST` \| `PRIVATE_SIGNET` \| `MAINNET` |
| `profile_sha256` | 64 hex | the active Profile |
| `trigger` | enum | `REORG_TDS_6_7` \| `CRITICAL_HEIGHT_DISAGREEMENT_TDS_6_8` \| `IMPORT_RESTORE_FAILURE_TDS_7_5` \| `PRESTATE_MISMATCH_FD_CT_03A` *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): gains `RUST_PYTHON_MISMATCH_TIP_GE_H0`, a failure named in §14.19 item 5 (a Rust/Python MISMATCH or divergence-family refusal, or a TDS 14.8 digest or start-state case) recorded at a run tip at or above H0; see §14.19 item 5.* |
| `stop_code` | enum | the matching 045a §4.4 code (`BLOCK_NODE_UNAVAILABLE_OR_DISAGREEING`, `STOP_DEFECT_DETECTED`, `STOP_PRESTATE_UNREPRODUCIBLE`, …) |
| `detected_height` | integer | local committed tip height at detection |
| `node_a_tip_hash`, `node_b_tip_hash` | 64 hex (internal byte order) | tips observed at detection |
| `fork_point_height` | integer or omitted | for `REORG_TDS_6_7` only |
| `state_digest_local`, `state_digest_reference` | 64 hex, or omitted with a reason | Rust and Python digests at the compared height. Either may be omitted only when it was never produced; then `digest_absent_reason` is required |
| `digest_absent_reason` | enum or omitted | required exactly when a digest is omitted: `STATE_PARSE_FAILED` \| `DIGEST_RECONSTRUCTION_FAILED` \| `NO_COMPARISON_FOR_TRIGGER` (for example a pure node disagreement) *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): gains `REFERENCE_DIVERGENCE_REFUSAL`, for a divergence-family refusal that leaves no Python digest; see §14.19 item 5.* |
| `lock_applied` | boolean | must be `true`; the record documents an applied lock |
| `affected_txids` | array of 64 hex | project candidates whose status may change; may be empty |
| `release_evidence` | object or omitted | in version `2` only: one member per condition the trigger requires (table below), each a 64-hex SHA-256 of that condition's evidence; no other member |
| `released_at_height` | integer or omitted | in version `2` only |

Required release conditions per trigger (Claude plan; every listed condition
must be present, none may substitute for another):

| `trigger` | Required `release_evidence` members | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `REORG_TDS_6_7` | `REPLAY_AND_DIFFERENTIAL_PASS`, `NODE_AGREEMENT_RESTORED`, `PUBLIC_CLAIMS_RECLASSIFIED` | TDS §6.7 steps 2-8 (common ancestor, rollback, digest, replay, Rust/Python comparison, Node A/B agreement, and step 8: prior public transaction claims marked disconnected or replaced); 044c-1 plan release table (`CT-P3-044C1_PLAN_EN.md:143`) *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-105, BATCH53): the BEAT cutover of §14.19 does not bypass this lock; see §14.19 item 6.* |
| `CRITICAL_HEIGHT_DISAGREEMENT_TDS_6_8` | `NODE_AGREEMENT_RESTORED`, `DISAGREEMENT_RESOLUTION_TDS_14_7`, `REPLAY_AND_DIFFERENTIAL_PASS` | TDS §6.8 ("until resolved") and TDS §14.7 (software versions and data directories verified, headers and chainwork compared, no third untrusted API as tie-breaker, complete resolution evidence recorded; TDS:1683-1692); 044c-1 plan release table (`:144`). The replay check is a Claude-plan addition so a resolved disagreement is also re-verified against state *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-105, BATCH53): the BEAT cutover of §14.19 does not bypass this lock; see §14.19 item 6.* |
| `IMPORT_RESTORE_FAILURE_TDS_7_5` | exactly one of two sets: (a) `REPLAY_AND_DIFFERENTIAL_PASS`, `NODE_AGREEMENT_RESTORED` (replay from H0−1 to the tip), or (b) `FRESH_IMPORT_TDS_7_5_PASS`, `NODE_AGREEMENT_RESTORED` (a fresh import or restore of validated state, or an authenticated checkpoint, passing every §7.5 step) | TDS §7.5 item 8 (replay or an authenticated checkpoint policy); 044c-1 plan release table (`:146`) *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-105, BATCH53): the BEAT cutover of §14.19 does not bypass this lock, and set (a) stays a replay from H0−1; see §14.19 item 6.* |
| `PRESTATE_MISMATCH_FD_CT_03A` with `STOP_DEFECT_DETECTED` | `DEFECT_FIXED_AND_VERIFIED` | 045a §4.4 release table *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): this row is the H0−1 comparison at tip H0−1; a failure named in §14.19 item 5 and recorded at a run tip at or above H0 uses `RUST_PYTHON_MISMATCH_TIP_GE_H0`; see §14.19 item 5.* |
| `PRESTATE_MISMATCH_FD_CT_03A` with `STOP_PRESTATE_UNREPRODUCIBLE` | `DEFECT_FIXED_AND_VERIFIED`, `TDS_14_8_PROCEDURE` | 045a §4.4 release table; TDS:1694 *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): tip H0−1 only, as :883; the table gains two `RUST_PYTHON_MISMATCH_TIP_GE_H0` rows, one per stop code; see §14.19 item 5.* |

Rules: an incident's state is the state of its highest `record_version` for
that `incident_id`. Version `1` carries no release fields and opens the lock.
Version `2` repeats every version-`1` field unchanged except `record_version` (which is `2`), adds
`supersedes_sha256`, `release_evidence` and `released_at_height`, and
closes that incident. Broadcast stays refused while any `incident_id` has no
valid version `2`. A version `2` is refused if its `supersedes_sha256` is not
the exact hash of that incident's version `1`, if any repeated field differs,
if a second version `2` exists for the same incident, or if its
`release_evidence` members are not exactly one of the sets the table allows for
the trigger (and stop code). Both versions are kept, as exact bytes; nothing
is rewritten.

## 9. CR-20 approval-record authentication (interface only)

- (source) CR-20: the P7-001 entry check refuses an unsigned approval record;
  the method is Founder decision 13 (CR original, `BITAGI_FINAL_CHANGE_REQUIREMENTS_KO.md:614-630`).
  CRD:55 acceptance: exit 6 for a missing signature, a different key or
  different bytes; 0 if valid; refuse a timestamp later than first
  observation; the Class T validator uses TEST_ONLY keys only.
- (Founder) FD-CT-92 row 7 (Q6 (a), decided 2026-10-27, B45:73): a dedicated
  BIP340 approval key on the offline signer, separate from the release key
  (7.1) and from the L2 key.
- (Claude plan, split with CT-P7-001) 045b-3 builds a method-neutral verifier
  interface and a TEST_ONLY Class T fixture only, mirroring the node-record
  pattern (`crates/bitagi-bitcoin/src/node_configuration.rs`). The real
  authenticator, after Q6, belongs to CT-P7-001 (or a successor item). *Dated line 2026-11-08 (A #258 issuecomment-6567032374 Q-B1, issuecomment-6728403349 Q-qa-4): the real CR-20 authenticator is QA-U2, a separate P6 FD-CT-62 unit by B after QA-U1, merged before 01-10 (FD-CT-101).*
- **The 045b-3 verifier is not the CR-20 verifier of 045a §4.2; Class E stays
  refused.** Every Class E approval record exits 6 until a real
  authenticator for the FD-CT-92 row 7 method is built and reviewed.
- The signed timestamp in an approval record is self-asserted. Independent
  time comes only from a section 3 receipt; the Class E path will require
  both.
