The freeze target is 11-12–11-13 (November, ROADMAP §3; Claude plan,
`docs/claude-track/plans/CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:68` at main `1eed6b00`). *Correction 2026-11-02 (045b freeze draft 1; checklist S17): it is still :68 at main `d9322e59`; the freeze re-cites it at the freeze SHA.* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C12): by construction (:30 dated line).*
The freeze happens only when all hold:

1. 044c-1 has recorded the nodes' `incrementalrelayfee` (1.3) and its
   exploratory XD-1 runs, labelled `EXPLORATORY` (5.1); the confirmatory XD-1
   runs come after the freeze receipt; *Met 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C1; A9 :1183, A10 :1184, :1096): `r_inc` is the Founder's console read of Node A's `incrementalfee`, 100 sat/kvB, not a 044c-1 record. Node B is not read; its read is a confirmation line before the P5-005 registration, and a different value stops the registration until A re-decides (§14.21 :1874). The EXPLORATORY XD-1 runs are 40425684629, 40428311146 and 42547211298 (:1096).*
2. the Founder has answered Q1, Q2 (residual-risk acceptance), Q3 and Q7
   (ARDR thresholds); FD-CT-92 answered Q2, Q3 and Q7 on 2026-10-27 (rows
   10, 2 and 8; B45:75, :62, :74), and Q1 is open; *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C2): met on 2026-11-02: FD-CT-102 (#258 issuecomment-6581164081; A1 :1175) set `max_fee_per_candidate_sat` 30,000 sat; `max_fee_exposure_sat` 500,000 and `max_principal_exposure_sat` 240,000 stand as drafted (A's reading, A1 :1175).*
3. the frozen bytes contain no `PENDING` value, no "filled at freeze" cell
   and no unset count or input (SSoT:2847: numerical placeholders are not
   completed gates); the freeze refuses otherwise; *Read 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C14): FEE V1, FBO V1 and the disposition extract carry no PENDING value and no unfilled cell (§14.21 :1892-1894; FEE V1 :10's "filled at freeze" is a quoted phrase). In this file each PENDING or "filled at freeze" cell carries its value in a same-line note (:100, :104-106). Factor 10 and item 4's DRAFT values belong to item 4's follow-up registration (:1198). The seed is written before the merge under the :1901 rule.*
4. every 045a §4.2 field has a value and the budget record passes the 045a
   §4.3 refusal table with no refusal (044c-1b); *Read 2026-11-09 (A, freeze; §14.21 :1900): the Class T record passes every §4.3 row in its Regtest context; the corrected Class E record (:942) fails only `ClassEWithoutApproval` in the Main H0 1,000,000 context, a refusal that stays until CR-20 (condition 7, :943-946).*
5. the fee policy, first-BEAT operations and 045a §3 extract are frozen as
   exact bytes and their hashes entered in the budget record;
6. the Founder approves the exact `budget_record_sha256` (045a §4.2); *Met 2026-11-09 (A record): the Founder approved `e9cef7c2795410cf671f15f9e386edf262a474629a0ee12576b51b1af2744308`, the Class E record of section 14.21 at `b8c92640` (#258 issuecomment-6743274408 request, -6743375133 receipt; FD-CT-106 candidate).* *Corrected 2026-11-09 (A error, found by A's freeze verification; record 58): `e9cef7c2…4308` carried as `profile_sha256` the SHA-256 of the `bitagi profile build` JSON document (`0886ce0e…`), not the Profile hash the product compares (TDS 12.4: SHA-256 of the 79 Profile bytes, `d75bad7a…6ce9`). In the Main H0 1,000,000 context the product refuses it `ContextMismatch` (:1900). The corrected record changes only that field: `82b5d263915470f1426c79fec56a898b7a6b9ed759373c625dbc1c4410ff5f4a`. Condition 6 is open again until the Founder approves that SHA-256.* *Met again 2026-11-09 (Founder, #258 issuecomment-6745650687 receipt, -6745466785 request): the Founder approved `82b5d263915470f1426c79fec56a898b7a6b9ed759373c625dbc1c4410ff5f4a`, which equals the file's SHA-256 at `ef7649015`; it replaces the approval of `e9cef7c2…` (FD-CT-106 candidate).*
7. for Class E use, the CR-20 real authenticator (Q6), the time-authentication

Claude asks for an answer by ~11-10 (recommendation; deadline unchanged) |
| Q7 | ARDR thresholds (section 4.3 item-1 recall and precision rule and item-2 private-order-flow threshold, the section 4.5 thresholds, 4.4 thresholds) | FD-CT-17 item (ARDR 임계값) | (a) all Claude plan candidates as written: section 4.4 (4 / 15 common-control, 15 zero-BEAT, with its Class T expected-report rule and default withdrawal meaning), section 4.3 item-1 link-based recall and precision 1.0 (a tooling-defect check; ownership-based recall reported as a published limit, SSoT:2452), the private-order-flow win share 0.5 at equal or higher public fees, and the five section 4.5 candidates (candidates per gate: observed count differs from the schedule-predicted count in any gate; more than 25% of attempts with a mined losing project rung; losing fees per attempt above `max_fee_exposure_sat / max_gate_attempts`; any 045a-conforming project replacement refused; pool Herfindahl index above 0.5); (b) record-only for Class E (no withdrawal trigger): this cannot satisfy ARDR item 6 (SSoT:2476), so no Class E Profile can be published (SSoT:2736); (c) other values | (a). Changed in the 10-24 re-review: recall 0.8 is removed, since 30% shared ancestry and 30% key reuse (section 4.6) let even a perfect heuristic link only about 1 − 0.7 × 0.7 ≈ 51% of wallets (estimate). 4 / 15 / 15 stay. In Class T, reaching 4 or 15 is not NO-GO (section 4.6 plants runs past 15); NO-GO is only when the tool's 4- and 15-reports differ from the expected list computed from the frozen seed (section 4.4). Withdrawal by default means support `WITHDRAWN` and a CT-P7-004 notice; before birth REGISTER broadcast stops, after birth the project Heart's BEATs continue (section 4.4). Breaks if the Founder sets withdrawal to include stopping BEATs (then (a) costs more, and (b) or other values may be better), or if in live Class E the common-control heuristic gives many false links (for example exchange batch withdrawals), so the 4 and 15 alarms fire wrongly | **Decided 2026-10-27:** FD-CT-92 row 8 (B45:74). Was: ~11-17. This section holds the lines the freeze adds to sections 0–13. It is appended, so every line above keeps its number: `:N` is a line of this file at main `d9322e59`, before this section. Each line names what it amends and its source. A source written `-N` is A's #258 issuecomment-N, as in section 4.6; a comment on another issue names that issue. **[A wording draft]** marks a line whose substance was decided before its wording; A reviewed and finalized that wording on 2026-11-09. Section 14.Z lists what is still open. A stale line above carries a dated note on the same line. On a table row that note sits inside the row's last cell, and the matching line here is the one to read. This section freezes nothing. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): every "once FD-CT-104 is recorded" note in this section now applies, since FD-CT-104 is recorded (`decisions/FD-CT-BATCH53_2026-11-05.md`, on main).*

### 14.3 Registration (amends section 3.2)

- (amends :311-321; A -6566266961, Claude decision; Reviewer #654 -6566195300) Registration condition: from the registration to the verdict, `main` changes only by a merge commit or a squash merge. Otherwise the verdict record names that commit. Reason: the judge (`RUN_NOT_ON_MAIN_HISTORY`) and the recollection (`RECOLLECT_RUN_NOT_ON_HISTORY`) bind a run to `main`'s first-parent history, and a fast-forward push or a GitHub rebase merge puts on that history a commit `main` never pointed at. The repository settings that would remove the gap (a `refs/tags/main` tag ruleset, or rebase merge turned off on `main`) stay the Founder's option.
- (amends :311-321; ARDR_RUN_V1 §4 :146-152; A -6561758412) The CT-P5-005 registration file carries a new key, `d2_2_tool_sha256`: the SHA-256 of `tools/claude_track_ardr_cluster.py` at the registration commit. The judge workflow also compares the run commit's copy of that file (`D2_2_TOOL_NOT_AT_RUN_COMMIT`). ARDR_RUN_V1 records the key in a dated addition at its end, naming its section 4.
- **[A wording draft]** (amends section 3.2; QA S-015, FD-CT-101 item 3, B49:129) The CT-P5-005 registration carries a P5-002 capture and retention list: tx hex with attempt ids; broadcast, first-seen and inclusion times; monitor and CT-P5-004-2c alarm logs; outage intervals; reserve snapshots. Fleet-scale items are NOT_MEASURED. The list is fixed in the CT-P5-005 registration (11-13–11-16), before P5-002 observes (11-25–11-26, `CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:592`), since wall-clock data not logged inside the window cannot be rebuilt. It lives in the registration, not in this file.
- **[A decision 2026-11-02]** (amends :311-321; J3 non-author check P2-1, A's J3 fix round) The judged set of an experiment is every completed `ardr <mode> <experiment>` dispatch whose head is on `main`'s first-parent history. The judge refuses a run list that leaves one out (`RUN_LIST_INCOMPLETE`): a dispatch left out would drop its judged gates from the pool index and could turn a NO-GO into a GO. The judge also refuses a listed CONFIRMATORY run that started before the bound, the committer time of the first commit that introduced the registration bytes (J3 re-check P2-A, A's fix round 2). That bound is git committer time, which whoever makes the commit can set; it is a consistency filter, not an authenticated time. The authenticated time of the registration is the Bitcoin-confirmed OTS proof over the signed tag (:307, :326), and a run that starts before that proof's block time is not CONFIRMATORY whatever the judge's bound says.
- **[A decision 2026-11-02]** (amends section 3.2; U1 For A 2, A -6549827052 Q3-Q4) The registration lists are keyed by the method policy's SHA-256, and each list fixes one schema.
- In the draft, no change: :311-321 (A -6537782009 §3).

### 14.4 ARDR (amends sections 4.3–4.6)

Item 1 and the judge:

- (amends :380, :388-405; A -6557890079 decision 3) Gate-level expected link closure. ANCESTRY joins every gate won by a flagged wallet of one owner; one flagged wallet that wins twice joins those two gates. ANCHOR_KEY joins every gate of a wallet that reuses its owner's base key, and the base wallet's `h = 0` gate, where `h` is the number of gates that wallet won before in the run (from 0, in gate order). Reports are compared on the key (clustering, cluster id, threshold, `trigger_gate`). ARDR_JUDGE_V1 states the same.
- (amends sections 4.3, 4.5 and 4.6; -6557890079 decision 4) When the block bytes differ from the frozen plan (winner, producer, ladder fees, funding layout, `bidders_sha256`), that gate or run is EXCLUDED with `JUDGE_PLAN_MISMATCH:<field>`. It counts under the 10% rule and is reported as a driver defect, not as NO-GO; A opens a finding. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, a plan mismatch in an ITEM3W run makes that run's W and D rows NOT_ESTABLISHED, since ITEM3W allows 0 exclusions; see §14.18.*
- (amends sections 4.3–4.5; -6557890079 decision 5) Every NO-GO threshold is a judge constant that cites its line in this file, and the judge record carries this file's SHA-256. The policy schema does not change: a threshold is a sentence of this file, not a run parameter. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, four W keys enter a W policy of their own as tool inputs, as the thresholds list does in the method policy (the registered method policy and the D2-2 method schema stay unchanged, §14.18 tooling 1); each must equal its judge constant that cites §14.18, so a threshold stays a sentence of this file; see §14.18.*
- (amends :530; -6557890079 decision 13) The driver tries `k` from 0 to 2^20 − 1 for one choice and then refuses (`DRAW_TRIES`); the judge has no such cap (`k` < 2^32). It is written here so that a split between the two shows.

Item 2:

- (amends :381 and :538; A -6537782009 Q3; ARDR_RUN_V1 §7) The item-2 miner policy is four rules, and the frozen bytes bind all four: descending ancestor feerate; ties by txid; parents before children; a cut at the weight cap: when a package would take the block past its weight budget, selection ends, and no smaller package after it is tried. It is ARDR_RUN_V1 §7's `ITEM2_DEFAULT`; any other rule is a new policy name.
- (amends the :381 missing-data cell, :538 and :571-574; #633 -6549379243) The 10% rule counts every exclusion code, not only a failed node or a missing relay log. Item 2's gate codes: `RELEASE_NOT_IN_TEMPLATE` (a bidder's last released rung is not in G's template or private set), `RELEASE_AFTER_G` (a release sent at or after `t0 + t_G`), `G_LATE` (block G's template taken 1 s or more after `t0 + t_G`), one per gate in that order; and `REPLACED_RUNG_NOT_IN_MEMPOOL` (public rung k − 1 is not in Node A's or Node B's mempool within 5 s before rung k's test; no block G is mined). None of them reads a result. The judge reports exclusions by condition (the order-constrained runs, the other even runs, the odd runs), by seat and by code; the L0 seat's exclusions are a line of their own (A -6551547851 Q7).
- (amends :537 and :538; #633 -6549379243 For A 3) Rung 0 is released when the G−1 block has reached all four nodes (miner, relay, Node A, Node B), not at the relay node only: every rung is locked to G−1, so Node A's and Node B's `testmempoolaccept` need that block.
- (amends :535 and the draw table :556; A -6551547851 Q1) The L0 seat takes no `BID_FEE` draw.
- (amends :537; A -6552107427 Q-L1–Q-L3) The L0 tool, as the operator, sets each rung's `f_k` by the :537 formula, and a test cell pins it. Its inputs are `p_0`, `b` (the 4b cap), `i`, `v` and `k` only; it reads no other seat's result and no mempool. The log keeps the computed `f_k` and compares it with the wallet row's `fee_sat` and `vsize` and with the charge base for every rung of the set, released or not, and with both nodes' `getmempoolentry` for released rungs; a difference fails the run (`L0_FEE_MISMATCH`). (B's fact-check -6567392721.) `p_0 = ⌈2.5 × b⌉` sat/kvB, the middle of the public seats' `[b, 4b]`, fixed with `b` at the freeze. `i` is the larger `incrementalfee` of the two nodes, read each run. The L0 seat's DRAFT budget has `max_gate_attempts` 24. After D3a-2b-2b, a CONFIRMATORY run reads these values from the policy keys `experiments.ITEM2.l0_seat` and `experiments.ITEM4_<scenario>.l0_seat`: `p0_rule` `CEIL_2_5_B`, `max_gate_attempts` 24, `exact_height_miss`, `budget_sha256` (A -6565897037 Q1); the freeze writes their values. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C15; §14.21 :1901): `b` 6,000, so `p_0` 15,000 sat/kvB and the public range [6,000, 24,000]; at `v` 213 and `i` 100 the L0 set is [3,195, 4,793] sat (rung 2's target 33,750 > 4b). The ITEM2 key's values are in the frozen policy B wrote (B-FZ-pol, `c1baa8db`): `p0_rule` `CEIL_2_5_B`, `max_gate_attempts` 24, `exact_height_miss` `CONTINUE_WITHIN_LIMITS`, `budget_sha256` `7d0a7a35…`, seat 1; the item-4 keys come with item 4's follow-up policy (:1901).*
- (amends the section 4.6 inputs; #633 -6549379243, A -6551547851 Q2, -6554792879 Q7) Every item-2 run and every item-4 run uses H0 = 1000. Item 2's lowest H0 is 107 (`ITEM2_MIN_H0 = MIN_H0 + 1`; the owner funding block is 105), refused at planning.
- (amends :536; A -6557723699, #645 For A 2) Rungs signed and not released, in Class T: they count as exposure already charged under R2; they are never broadcast after the gate and are deleted with the run folder; each seat's report gives their count and fee sum, and the judge does not count them as released rungs. The custody risk after Class E (rungs signed before G − 1) belongs to the Class E operating documents.

Item 3 and section 4.5:

- **[A wording draft]** (amends :382; QA S-011 and S-012, FD-CT-101 item 3, B49:129; SSoT:2486-2519) Item 3's first-BEAT failure rate is SSoT's fixed birth-cohort rate: a birth's boundary is its height + 2,017; it fails when no BEAT is accepted below that boundary; a same-block BEAT counts as success; a birth stays in the matured denominator once its boundary is processed, whatever ends it later; the rate is undefined while no birth has matured; missing or incomplete history is NOT_ESTABLISHED, never zero failures; a reorg means recomputing the affected births on the same evaluated prefix. Population realization is `actual_CURRENT(H) / N_dense(H)`, with `N_dense(H) = 0` below H0 and `min(21,000, floor((H − H0) / 144) + 1)` from H0; it is undefined when `N_dense(H) = 0`. Item 3 also reports survival, ending causes and censoring in one table (SSoT:2519). The item-3 judge's tests must carry the QA fixtures (one failure in a matured cohort of two at H = 3200, SSoT:2506; `N_dense` 0, 1, 1, 2) and their mutants; no item-3 judge exists at the freeze SHA.
- **[A wording draft]** (amends :539; assurance table :108, WA-01) One item-3 zero-BEAT cohort, or one EF-settle test, carries the condition-6 path: Born at H, no BEAT, TIMEOUT judged at H + 2,017, the ledger rotation at the writer's first turn after the birth and settlement at H + 4,096 (523-b, #581). It counts as "not sustained success". *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): the EF-settle test `wa01_a_timed_out_birth_rotates_at_once_and_settles` (`crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/ef_settle_tests.rs:577`) carries this path; no P5-001 case does yet (:1258 item 3).*
- **[A wording draft]** (amends :496-506; QA S-014, FD-CT-101 item 3; SSoT:2635-2649) The section 4.5 rows that count transactions, weight or fees use one attribution policy, this line, over SSoT 8.5's three ledgers: A, active-chain realization (mined losing candidates, their weight and fees); B, submission and operating workload (candidates per gate, replacement and cancellation traffic, unmined offered fees); C, disconnected history and recovery (reorg-replaced winners), counted by outcome only, since :1148 does not claim disconnected workload; a transaction that leaves the active chain is reversed in Ledger A and kept in Ledger C, and a re-included one counts once in Ledger A (SSoT:2645). The ids are the txid, the block hash, the gate or cycle id, and the attempt and rung. Ledger B and C counts are never added to Ledger A as paid fees. A row the policy cannot attribute is reported NOT_MEASURED, never as satisfied.

Item 4:

- (amends :383; A -6554792879 Q8, corrected by #643 -6556082072 For A 1; A -6557723699) Self dealing: the test miner includes its own REGISTER first at the product floor fee, below every honest rung 0 in G. A zero-fee REGISTER stays a scenario not run, because the product signer does not sign it. The fee check is absolute: at the candidates' measured vsize the project's rung-0 fee is above the self REGISTER's fee (DRAFT 600 sat, so `b` ≥ 1127). It is written before the freeze, so it is not chosen from results.
- (amends :383; A -6557723699 Q2) E7 is in no item-4 miner policy, so item 4 lists it as not run; 1e-2b-2b's E7 real-node row covers that label for item 4's entry set.
- **[A wording draft]** (amends :383, "a scenario not run is listed as not run"; A -6551547851 Q3-Q4, #643 -6556082072 For A 3; ARDR_RUN_V1 §23 "Not run") Every item-4 run logs the cases it does not cover. The list has three cases: `Done:Born{late, at_opening: false}` (censorship: the synthetic competitor holds G in every run), `ITEM4_SELF_ZERO_FEE_REGISTER` (self dealing), and the L0 project seat in the stand-in runs (in the L0 runs, E7 takes that place). Where a run's Reorg67 release was the TEST_ONLY step, the judge report says "the release is a Class T test step", since at the freeze SHA the product has no TDS 6.7 release path for this lock (#446, open).
- (amends :418; #658 -6565529813 For A 1, A's draft there; A -6557723699 Q4 (다)) The E9-d row's expected label: `Done:Born` (G, and G + 1 at depth 2) → `Refuse:Locked(Reorg67)` at the replacement tip, every rung, at its first turn and at the release turn itself (the turn decides before step 6 releases) → the TDS 6.7 release (Class T Regtest: the TEST_ONLY `RELEASE_REORG67_TEST_ONLY` phase, step 6 not performed) → `Stale` at the same replacement tip G + d (the REORGED, RELEASE and STALE turns all run there; `Refuse:GateAdvanced` for the released rung), `opening_block_missed` true from the replacement tip on, `reorged_before_inclusion` false throughout; every step is kept in the label line. The two locked turns pair the verdict `Refuse:Locked(Reorg67)` with the outcome `Stale@<H0>><next birth>`, and the judge compares both. The line is rung 0's; the other rungs are `Done:ProjectBornByOther@<H0>` at G and G + 1, and an unreleased rung is `Wait:OfflineSignaturePending` at the STALE turn. These expectations hold under the budget's `exact_height_miss` `CONTINUE_WITHIN_LIMITS`; any other value is refused (`ITEM4_L0_MISS`). (B's fact-check -6567392721 against `tools/claude_track_ardr_item4.py:159-168`, `:396-429`.) A CONFIRMATORY run may use the TEST_ONLY release (A decision, next line). *2026-11-05 (J5 Q2, A #258 issuecomment-6656311995; ARDR_RUN_V1 §24 and the real-node rows of the D3a-2b-1 note §6; `tools/claude_track_ardr_item4.py` `L0_TURNS`, `ITEM4_LABELS`): the expected labels of the other three sub-scenarios, under the budget's `exact_height_miss` `CONTINUE_WITHIN_LIMITS` (any other value is refused, as above). The planned turns are G for self dealing and fee asymmetry, and G, G + 143 and G + 144 for censorship. At each turn every rung of L0's set is judged by its verdict, its report outcome and its two flags. Rung 0 in a block (self dealing and fee asymmetry at G, the E10-s row :419 being self dealing; censorship at G + 144): outcome `IncludedNoEffect@<height>`, verdict `Done:IncludedNoEffect@<height>`. Rung 0 released and not mined (censorship at G and G + 143): outcome `Stale@G><next birth>`, verdict `Refuse:GateAdvanced`. A rung k ≥ 1, charged and never released: outcome `Stale@G><next birth>`, verdict `Wait:OfflineSignaturePending`. Every rung at every turn: `opening_block_missed` true (this is the value :419 leaves to the row table) and `reorged_before_inclusion` false. The next birth is the judge's own replay at the turn's height. Any one of the four that differs is a mislabel under :383.*
- **[A decision 2026-11-02]** (amends :418; A -6565897037 Q2, option (ㄱ)) A CONFIRMATORY E9-d run with the L0 seat uses the TEST_ONLY release, with its mark in the raw log; the judge report labels the row "release: Class T test step", and the row is evidence for item 4's labels after a release, never for the TDS 6.7 release path. When that path merges, the row is run again and the earlier result is not upgraded. Reason: the release mechanism is not what item 4 measures, and a reasoned NOT_RUN (option (ㄴ)) would leave the label line without evidence. B lifts `ITEM4_L0_REORG_CONFIRMATORY` once the mark and the report label are in place.
- (amends :543; A -6554792879 Q6, -6551547851 Q5) Reorg depth by run index: depth 1 for even `r`, depth 2 for odd `r`. A depth-d run replaces d blocks from G (depth 2 replaces G and G + 1) with a chain of d + 1 blocks.

The self-dealing, censorship and reorg runs pay fixed fees. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, ITEM3W also uses no random draw; see §14.18.*
- (amends the draw table :552-560; A -6554792879 Q2; added before item 4's follow-up registration) One more row:

| Experiment `e` | Choice `label` | Range `n` and use |
|---|---|---|
| `ITEM4_FEE_ASYMMETRY` | `BID_FEE_2` … `BID_FEE_4` (the seat number) | `n = 3b + 1`: feerate `b + value` (honest seats 2 to 4) |

- (amends :541; A -6554792879 Q1, Q4) Fee asymmetry: the asymmetric bidder is public seat 0, L0 is seat 1, and seats 2 to 4 draw `BID_FEE_j`. The median is the nearest-rank p50 of the honest four's rung-0 feerates, computed only from values fixed before any rung 0 is released (L0's configured `p_0` and the drawn `BID_FEE_2` … `BID_FEE_4`), never from a mempool. L0's actually sent fee is logged apart, for the judge to compare. The asymmetric bidder sends one rung at 10 × that median, outside the 4b ladder cap. The factor 10 stays PENDING_FREEZE (section 14.Z). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C22): factor 10 is fixed by item 4's follow-up registration (F15 :1198).*
- **[A wording draft]** (amends the section 4.6 item-4 rows; #643 -6556082072 For A 3-4; ARDR_RUN_V1 §23) Item 4's values go on the freeze list with the rows above, and stay DRAFT until item 4's own registration (`CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:532`): the self REGISTER fee, 600 sat (the product floor); the project seat's `p_0 = ⌈2.5 × b⌉`; the censorship and reorg competitor pays 2 × the stand-in's rung-0 fee + 1 at the same vsize (`competitor_fee` in `tools/claude_track_regtest_offline_export.py`); an asymmetric fee above 90,000 sat is refused before any node (`ITEM4_ASYMMETRIC_FEE_CAP`); the censorship window is G … G + 143 (144 blocks), set by G's height and lifted at G + 144 (A -6554792879 Q5). The reorg replacement is checked by txid and by funding outpoint, written before results: none of the d + 1 replacement blocks may hold a txid of the winner's set or any spend of its funding outpoint (`ITEM4_WINNER_READDED`).

Units and stopping:

- (amends :546; A -6549827052 Q2) An item-1 job that dies at gate `g`: gates `g` … 199 count as missing. With the gates EXCLUDED otherwise they go under the 10% rule, and a total above 20 makes item 1 NOT_ESTABLISHED. The verdict uses the remaining gates, with the prefix's D2-2 metrics. Whether the prefix runs depends only on the cause and `g`; after `SECRET_IN_LOG` or `LOG_BOUND` it does not run, and every gate is EXCLUDED. The report keeps `g`, the cause and `prefix: true`.
- (amends :546; A -6537782009 §3, B49:479) Items 3 and 4 keep A's default units, made final here: item 3's unit is the cohort, item 4's is the run. *2026-11-05 (J5 Q1 and Q4, A #258 issuecomment-6656311995): item 4 is a statistical market-measurement row under :1089, and its 10% rule counts per sub-scenario: more than 5 of a sub-scenario's 50 runs missing or EXCLUDED makes that sub-scenario not run (:383), and so does a sub-scenario with no judged run. A run that the driver ends on its own label check (`ITEM4_LABEL_MISMATCH`, or `L0_RELEASE_NOT_REORG67` for the `released` line :1068 names) is not an exclusion: the judge judges its label lines from the raw log. A run whose planned turns are all judged, with no plan mismatch, is a judged run whether its labels match or not, and a mismatch is NO-GO evidence (:383). A run that stopped partway is NO-GO evidence when a turn it already judged mismatches, and EXCLUDED otherwise. Inside a sub-scenario a NO-GO comes before NOT_ESTABLISHED, as item 1's order in :1086.*
- (amends :546; -6557890079 decision 6) The pool's "a run of 4 gates" defines its run unit. Its 10% rule counts gates: more than 20 of its 200 gates. ARDR_RUN_V1 :169 ("run for item 4 and the pool run") is superseded for the pool by this line; B corrects ARDR_RUN_V1.
- **[A decision 2026-11-02]** (amends :504 and :571-574; J3 Q1) Pool outcome order: exclusions above 10% give NOT_ESTABLISHED even when the index is above 0.5, since with that many gates missing the index can be biased either way; the index and its threshold are still reported. Item 1 keeps section 4.3's order (a NO-GO found on judged gates comes first), since its NO-GOs are deterministic defects, not a statistic. Neither order can give GO.
- **[A wording draft]** (amends :504; J3 non-author check P3-7) What the Class T pool row tests: the producer of each gate G is drawn from the frozen seed (`PRODUCER_H<G>`) and a block whose tag differs is excluded, so the index over judged gates equals the index of the seed's own draws at those heights and is fixed before collection. The row therefore tests the draw → coinbase tag → judge pipeline and the 10% rule, not concentration in a market; it is reported that way, and it supports no claim about real mining-pool concentration.
- (amends section 4.6, a new input row; A -6557726767) `unit_minutes` and the gate-loop line rest on the measured distribution of ITEM1 run 41298737612 (200 gates, heights up to about 28,800): gate loop 1,829.6 s (17% of the 10,500 s unit); per gate mean 9.15 s, p50 8.59 s, p90 12.24 s, p99 16.75 s, max 18.77 s; setup 10.1 s; D2-2 88.1 s; gate time about 6.84 s + 0.16 s per 1,000 blocks of height. The value is not picked to fit results: this measures the time margin and is not a verdict metric. A run of the same shape whose heights go well above about 28,800 (more gates or a higher H0) is measured again before the freeze. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, ITEM3W is not this shape; A sets its unit_minutes and slice plan from a measured EXPLORATORY ITEM3W run before its registration; see §14.18.* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C24): at H0 1,000, against the DRAFT 110 (`ARDR_RUN_V1.md:1458`), the top height rises by about 890 blocks (estimate), about +0.14 s per gate by this line's model; that is not "well above", so no new measurement.*
- **[A wording draft]** (amends :571-574; assurance table :196) The 10% exclusion rule applies to the statistical ARDR market-measurement rows only: items 1–4 and the pool, each with the unit and count its own line sets (:546, :1083-1085). A required inventory case allows 0 exclusions, 0 unexplained skips and 0 unreached hooks; a combination that cannot run gets its reason and a substitute witness before observation. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, ITEM3W is a required inventory experiment under this rule (0 exclusions); see §14.18.*
- **[A wording draft]** (amends the confirmatory record formats of sections 4 and 5; assurance table :241, V-01 and V-03) Each CONFIRMATORY record format of sections 4 and 5 (the ARDR judge record and the XD-1 record) carries `expected` and `observed` fields, so "observed REFUSED, required PASS" can be written, and an `INCONCLUSIVE` value, so a timeout is not folded into FAIL or PARTIAL. A mapping that folds values keeps the cause. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): no such format has these fields at the freeze SHA; each adds them before its first CONFIRMATORY verdict.*
- **[A wording draft]** (amends sections 4.6 and 5.1; assurance table :251, V-15 and V-16) A required PASS of a negative or incident case is not survival-cohort success. A wait case is complete only when every BEAT needed during the wait, and the next BEAT after the return (R2 §16, WA-05), are done.

### 14.8 Incident record and operator lines (amends section 8.2)

- (amends section 8.2; A -6446910221 §8 (i), keyed as #563 -6459502834 §4 asks; the #552 change merged as #563, `5f151550`) Ledger rotation after the fix. The operator keys on the host's typed outputs, not on file names (`ledger status` shows no segment number):
  1. When start, step 0 or `recover` refuses with `Io`, `Replay` or `StorageFull`, the ledger is not opened with another tool.
  2. `<ledger>-segment-*`, `.partial`, `-rotation` and `-rotation-journal` are never moved, renamed or deleted.
  3. Start the host; after a restore with recovery pending, run `recover`. The host finishes the rotation first.
  4. `Io`, `StorageFull` or `Busy`: fix the cause (space, permissions, another process holding the ledger) and start again.
  5. `Replay`: that archive is not this live file's; usually the live file was restored or changed. Stop, change nothing, leave the directory as it is, and tell the Reviewer and A. Never rotate over it.
  6. A ledger is restored only with the same backup's archive set, and a live file older than the newest archive is never restored. Archives at or above the restored live segment are for a non-author Reviewer or the Founder to decide.
  7. A directory, symlink or other non-regular entry under one of those names is not a rotation file; it is removed only with a non-author Reviewer present.
- (amends section 8.2; A -6446910221 §8 (ii)) A ledger already locked by `Replay` before the fix is not repaired. The whole test batch retires together: the ledger and every `-segment-*` and `-rotation*` file, the host state directory, the host file, the store and the off-host records. It is kept read-only when it is a finding's evidence. A new ledger is initialized in a new directory (the same path gives `Replay` at the first rotation). Today such a ledger can only be a Class T Regtest test ledger. Repairing a ledger that matters takes a Founder or Reviewer decision, never a runbook step, and a non-author checks first.
- (amends section 8.2, the release step; A -6437537312 with the exit-1 wording as corrected by #556 -6447489466 §2 P2 and B's `4f72ac0b`; `docs/claude-track/plans/notes/CT-P3-044C1_PLAN_EN/2026-10-27_541-q8a-tip-above.md` §4) A Q8-ㄱ procedure file refused with `HOST_PROCEDURE_NOT_RELEASABLE` has two ways out:
  1. The agreed chain grows past the local tip: present the same file again; the turn that follows the new block takes it (the store follows the agreed chain first).
  2. The reorg comes back first: that turn refuses any file (`ProcedureReorgThisTurn`); the next file names the ancestor the host holds (the operator report's `reorg_seen.ancestor`), and a file naming the local tip is then `ProcedureAncestorMismatch`.
- (same source) The earlier wording that a file given in the growing turn is `ProcedureReorgThisTurn` is wrong and is not used: that refusal needs the hook to have run (`reorg_seen`), and a chain that only extends the local tip calls no hook.
- **[A wording draft]** (amends section 8.2; B45:291, A-Q17-0; `docs/claude-track/formats/REORG_STEP6_RECORD_V1.md` §8.4, §12) A-Q17-0's lines:
  - (amends :872) Each `release_evidence` member is "the 64-hex digest named for that condition (a tagged hash where the format names its tag)", in place of "a 64-hex SHA-256 of that condition's evidence".
  - With `step6=performed` the record maps as REORG_STEP6_RECORD_V1 §8.4 says: `fork_point_height` = `step2_common_ancestor.height`; `state_digest_local` and `state_digest_reference` = the Rust and Python `final_state_digest` of the replacement manifest; `REPLAY_AND_DIFFERENTIAL_PASS` = the bundle digest `procedure_record_sha256`; `NODE_AGREEMENT_RESTORED` and `PUBLIC_CLAIMS_RECLASSIFIED` = SHA-256 of the canonical bytes of `step7_node_agreement` and of `step8_public_claims` (a `NOT_APPLICABLE` object with its reason is the latter's evidence). *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): this maps REORG_STEP6_RECORD_V1, whose step 6 replays from H0−1 with no STATE start, covers only T_e ≤ H0 + 4,095 and never runs on main; a later version that reuses the §14.19 comparison chain needs its own mapping; see §14.19 item 6.*
  - A not-performed release has no `REPLAY_AND_DIFFERENTIAL_PASS` evidence, so its incident cannot reach version 2: Regtest not-performed releases stay outside 045b incidents.
  - A Reorg67 lock that 1D3 Q17 leaves open (a release refused above a bound, fail closed) ends the run as an explained interruption, in a GOV072 interruption class of its own.
  - The claims-list format and the meaning of "public claim" are open (section 14.Z); until they merge the host refuses `RECLASSIFIED`. *A decision 2026-11-02: section 14.15 (H7).*
- (amends section 8.2; `docs/claude-track/plans/CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:513-517`) Signet: a Reorg67 lock stays open; that deployment's decisions and BEATs are held (wait registry W7), and the run ends as a stop with its reason (fail closed). A retry needs a new deployment (a new ledger and store, new H0 and H0−1 steps), and the S13 86,400 s span counts again from its start (GOV072). So P5-002 (a) adds no reorg that needs a Reorg67 release. The same line goes into the P5 stop rules (section 14.Z).
- **[A wording draft]** (amends the field table :854-873; QA S-018 part 1, FD-CT-101 item 3) The record gains the TDS §21.3 build identity and the affected block: `git_commit` (40 lowercase hex), `build_id` (64 lowercase hex, the RX-02 binary SHA-256), `generated_at_utc` (integer seconds), `affected_height` (integer) and `affected_block_hash` (64 hex, internal byte order). The P5-005 verifier requires them. They are part of `BITAGI_CT_BROADCAST_LOCK_INCIDENT_V1` as it freezes with this file (`CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:142`).

### 14.9 A decisions settled in this draft (2026-11-02)

- **(C4)** (amends section 4.6 and the section 8.2 no-secret rule :850-852; #652 -6562013983 note 1) Class T Regtest raw logs keep the signed transaction hex as today (`L0_TURN.lines.raw_tx_hex`, every rung, released or not; TEST_ONLY keys). In Class E, a published log keeps only the SHA-256 of a signed transaction; the hex of a rung that was never released is never published, and a released transaction's hex is published only after its funding outpoint's spend is final under the depth the Class E operating documents fix. A Class E tool that writes such logs is checked against this line in its own unit.
- **(E11)** (amends :479-481) No withdrawal stop code is added. Before birth, withdrawal is the Founder not approving, or the operator not signing, a further REGISTER release; after birth it holds nothing, so no BEAT is ever held by it.
- **(F11)** (amends section 4.6; A -6543158220, #620 -6543164894) `b` is a multiple of 250 sat/kvB, so that no ladder is empty at `v` = 213 vB.
- **(F12)** (amends :516-517; #613 -6539379807) "A count unset is refused" applies to all eight experiments, listed or not. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, it applies to nine experiments; ITEM3W's count is 19 runs; see §14.18.*
- **(H5)** (#563 -6459502834 §3; `docs/claude-track/plans/notes/CT-P3-044C1_PLAN_EN/2026-10-28_552-rotation-finish.md` :80-86) No `HostFileRotationPartial` operator line is needed: since #563 start retries a stopped host-file rotation before the writer opens. A host-file-only restore beside a later archive stays a halt the runbook reports (section 14.8 item 6).

### 14.14 BEAT execution unit lines (A #258 issuecomment-6567718863, answering B's draft -6566921778)

- (amends the wait registry section 2.4, new rows; B's draft §1) The BEAT unit's own waits are WB1–WB8: WB1 signing handed off the host (W25's shape); WB2 the per-BEAT authority file (W23 and W24's shape); WB3 waiting for mining (W26's shape; the same bytes may always be broadcast again); WB4 replacement (first-BEAT operations §3–§4, fee policy §5); WB5 a BEAT-cap stop (BLOCKED: a recorded refusal, then the TIMEOUT report); WB6 the BEAT deadline passed (`HeartTimedOut` reported, no revival); WB7 the anchor moved or the slot was superseded (held, with no raise); WB8 a cap reached (reported; no over-cap charge is attempted). The registry note's section 5 points here. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-105, BATCH53): at tip ≥ H0, WB3's broadcast again at a new tip is a Broadcast at that tip, so below the cutover height E it needs a MATCH chain through that tip and from E it may go as `CutoverReplay`; bytes a mempool still holds only wait and need no comparison; BEAT-3b adds WB9, the BEAT replay comparison below E; see §14.19 items 2, 3 and 11.* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze, R19-7): WB1–WB9 are in the registry note's section 2.4 as built at main `15233151`; where the build differs from this line (WB2 two subject-bound waits; WB4 no wait type of its own; WB5's exit by host re-pin; WB6 also `HeartMaxAge` and `HeartBroken`; WB7's superseded slot final as `BeatAnchorSpent`), the registry's difference list says so.*
- **[A decision 2026-11-02]** (amends first-BEAT operations §4, the #555 BEAT replacement rule) "A signed BEAT may always be broadcast again" has two stated limits: the per-tip attempt bound (`crates/bitagi-launch/src/machine.rs:271-282`) stops a further attempt at the same tip, and the attempt is made again at the next tip; and the host counts the caps before it charges (the `budget_pin.rs:284-307` count), so it never attempts an over-cap charge whose stop would hold the in-flight BEAT's re-broadcast. It reports "cap reached" instead. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-105, BATCH53): at tip ≥ H0 a third limit applies: below the cutover height E the bytes are broadcast again at a new tip only on a MATCH chain through that tip, and from E an availability-only gap lets them go as `CutoverReplay`; a MISMATCH or a divergence-family refusal holds them at any height (`STOP_DEFECT_DETECTED`); see §14.19 item 3. Re-cited (A's answer to B's BEAT-3 draft, #258 issuecomment-6629923155): :271-282 is `bounded` at main `d9322e59`, where section 14 cites; from `c5195fd1` (and at `3031f432`) `bounded` is `machine.rs:284-304` and :271-282 lies in `stop_refuses`.*
- **[A decision 2026-11-02]** (amends section 6 and first-BEAT operations §4) A BEAT's authority is one record per BEAT subject (FD-CT-01's separate signing and broadcast authority, met per subject), not per tip; REGISTER's binding to the run's checks id does not apply to it. Fresh checks still run at every tip. The authority survives a lock release (#555: BEAT approval itself is never held), and the first broadcast after a release is reported. The deadline is computed each tip from the store's Heart state, never read from the file. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-105, BATCH53): at tip ≥ H0 the decision report that records this authority also shows the basis, the cutover height E and the cutover rule, and a switch between `ComparedReplay` and `CutoverReplay` is reported, not authorized again; after the release of a Rust/Python mismatch stop whose Reviewer record shows that Rust departed, an authority recorded on a decision report at or above the first differing height does not survive, and a new one is needed; see §14.19 items 3 and 5.*
- **[A decision 2026-11-02]** (amends section 6; wait registry W7–W11, §3.3 item 2) The campaign's terminal state: once no non-final candidate is bound (about the last accepted BEAT + 4,096), a NodeState, Reorg67 or CriticalHeight68 lock opened then has no release path in this ledger. Under FD-CT-05 every campaign ends this way. No operation of this track needs a decision, an import, a construction or a broadcast in that ledger after the campaign ends: reserve recovery and the final report use the report fields and an off-host procedure. A later unit that needs the ledger after the end brings its own release path.

### 14.15 A decisions, second batch (2026-11-02)

Settled by A in this draft from the cited sources; each bullet names the condition under which it would be wrong. None changes a Founder value (FD-CT-92) or Exact D3 Core.

- **(E13)** (amends :381 and :540, and replaces :381's NOT_ESTABLISHED cell "fewer than the preregistered run count (section 4.6) completed", which one EXCLUDED run would meet; S-009 crosswalk gap 5) A run of item 2 is "completed" when it ends OBSERVED. The qualifying sample is the 50 order-constrained runs fixed by run index (the first 50 private-present runs, `r` = 0, 2, …, 98), never runs selected by their results; an unconstrained run whose public fees happen to sit above the private fee does not count as qualifying. Item 2 is NOT_ESTABLISHED when more than 10% of its 200 runs are missing or EXCLUDED (every gate exclusion code, section 14.4 S14), or when more than 5 of the 50 qualifying runs are missing or EXCLUDED, that is, fewer than 45 OBSERVED qualifying runs. :540's "fewer than 50" is read as this 10% rule applied to the qualifying stratum. Reason: under "all 50 must be OBSERVED" a 2% per-run exclusion rate alone gives NOT_ESTABLISHED with probability 1 − 0.98^50 ≈ 64%, so the item would measure exclusion luck, not order flow; the stratum rule has the same form as item 1's (missing and EXCLUDED add, ARDR_RUN_V1 :729) and is fixed before any data. The item-2 statistics over the qualifying sample use its OBSERVED runs. Order: as for the pool (:1086), NOT_ESTABLISHED comes before a NO-GO, since item 2's threshold is a share that this many missing runs can bias either way; the win share and its threshold are still reported, and neither order gives GO. This fails if, over at least 20 order-constrained EXPLORATORY item-2 runs (11-10 to 11-12), 5% or more end missing or EXCLUDED (at a 5% per-run rate more than 5 of 50 are lost with probability about 4%, at 7% about 13.5%, at 8% about 21%); then A over-provisions the constrained runs (a driver change on `main` before the registration, :316) instead. With fewer than 20 such runs, A states their count and observed rate at the freeze, and the rule stands with that limit. *(Reviewer RV-121 P3, #664 issuecomment-6580212705.)*
- **(F2)** (amends :536; A -6543158220 Q4, -6565897037 Q1) The L0 project bidder holds public seat S1 in all 200 item-2 runs: one fixed integer, never the private seat (`check_l0_seat`, item2 :117-122). Item 4 does not use the same index: its project seat, which the L0 seat takes, is S1 in self-dealing and fee asymmetry but S0 in censorship and reorg, where S1 is the competitor (item4 :101-112); only the stand-in's wallet label (W01) is common there. The seat goes into the policy key as a field (`experiments.ITEM2.l0_seat.seat` = 1, beside `p0_rule`, `max_gate_attempts`, `exact_height_miss` and `budget_sha256` of :1053 and the F3 row), so the judge reads it from the frozen record; this is B's D3a-2b-2b key shape. The line holds with the default (ㄱ) and is final with F1 on 11-10; under (ㄷ) L0 leaves item 2 and this line lapses. It fails if the EXPLORATORY logs show a seat-index effect on inclusion or timing; then the seat rotates (`1 + (r mod 4)`) before the registration. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A): F1 is (ㄱ) (:1192), so this line is final.*
- **(F9)** (amends :548 and :562-563; #633 -6549379243 For A 4) Item 2's and item 4's runs keep the ITEM1-named funding draws and the freeze registers them: every item-2 run and every item-4 run (`_gate`, `_gate_l0`) calls `assignment(seed, r)`, which draws `OWNER_W<ii>`, `ANCESTRY_W<ii>` and `KEY_REUSE_W<ii>` (ii = 00–39) with e = `ITEM1` and that experiment's own run index (bidders :96-107). These rows are already admitted by the driver's ITEM1 draw table (driver :102-112), so no code changes; the frozen draw table states the three families, the run index used, and that item-2 run `r`, item-4 run `r` and item-1 run `r` therefore share the same values. :548's "nothing else is random" and :562-563's "use no random draw" read with this exception. No item-2 or item-4 metric reads ownership, ancestry or key reuse, and no item-2 or item-4 judge (J4 and later) may attribute a Heart, a link or a winner by Anchor key: winners are read by txid (item2 :638-642) and, in item 4, also by the funding outpoint (item4 :573, :603), because in some runs two seats share an Anchor label. This fails if a later judge needs Anchor-key attribution; then the item-2 and item-4 seats are funded directly (three call sites in `tools/`: item2 :529, item4 :751, :858) before the registration.
- **(F13)** (ARDR_RUN_V1 :288-297; A -6537782009 §3) B's DRAFT key rule is frozen as written: the scalar for label `L` in run `r` is the first valid secp256k1 secret key among `SHA-256(seed ‖ "TEST_ONLY_KEY" ‖ 0x00 ‖ u32be(r) ‖ L ‖ 0x00 ‖ u32be(k))`, k = 0, 1, …. The freeze adds: `TEST_ONLY_KEY` is reserved and is never an experiment name or a draw label; `L` is one of the key-set labels of ARDR_RUN_V1 :282-283 (`SYNTHETIC_*`, `COMPETITOR_*`), which share no prefix with the draw labels, so keys are apart from the draw table by both e and label; the rule covers the seed-derived sets only, and the L0 seat's keys are the offline-export tool's fixed `TEST_ONLY_PRIVATE_SCALAR_KNOWN` fixtures (l0 :53-54; the label is at `tools/claude_track_regtest_offline_export.py:653`, `:1005`), not seed-derived; the rule has no experiment field, so the same (`r`, `L`) gives the same key in ITEM1, ITEM2 and ITEM4 run `r`, which is harmless because each run is a fresh Regtest chain; and because the seed is published, every derived scalar is public, which is acceptable only because these are Class T Regtest test keys never used on any public network. At the freeze, B flips the `KEY_RULE_DRAFT_PENDING_FREEZE` log label (bidders :64), a label change only. This fails if two experiments ever share a chain, store or ledger, or a check keys on a public key across experiments; then the experiment is bound into the digest. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): once FD-CT-104 is recorded, the key-set labels also include `ITEM3W_<plant>_<role>_<n>`, which name NUMS points (an uncontrollable Anchor, or the internal key of an `OP_TRUE`-leaf Anchor), not F13 keys, so ITEM3W uses no private key; see §14.18.*
- **(S7)** (amends :812-813; P5:116; :316-321) A section 6.3 record change (one that only raises BEAT caps) needs its own receipt. It is a follow-up registration of the :318-321 shape: it cites the prior registration hash, carries its own receipts (the Bitcoin-confirmed OTS proof over the signed tag as the primary witness, FD-CT-92 row 1, and GitHub server time as the second), and one more Founder tag signature. The 24 h lead of :307-310 (a Claude-plan value) does not apply to it; instead both required witnesses, the confirmed OTS proof and the GitHub server time, must be strictly earlier than the first broadcast under the new record (every required witness, not only the earliest: :300-302; FD-CT-92 row 1, "둘 다 첫 관측 전에 기록한다", FD-CT-17 bundle :50), and until both are recorded the old caps hold, so a cap stop stays a stop. A 6.3 change is still not a new confirmatory test (:790-799 stand). Reason: the registration binds the budget record's SHA-256 (P5:116), so without a receipt the record in force would differ from the registered hash, and a later reader could not tell when the change was made. Cost: one Founder tag signature per change, and the A11 repository and key must exist before Class E; the Founder is told in the A3 request. This fails if a cap stop needs a raise faster than one OTS confirmation and the second witness allow inside the BEAT deadline; the guard is cap margin at the freeze (A3). *(Reviewer RV-121 P2-1, 2026-11-02: both witnesses.)*
- **(C2)** (amends :185-190 and section 10 item 8; assurance table :187, :525) Option (ii): with the replacement form, rungs are reserved and signed in advance (FD-CT-03 item 2), so after a refusal at tip H0−1 only the last reserved rung is broadcastable and there is no next-rung recovery at H0−1. This is a residual risk, stated here and in section 10 item 8: the next tip is handled only as an exact-height miss under `exact_height_miss` (`CONTINUE_WITHIN_LIMITS`, :103, :1023), and the record counts it as a miss, never as a recovered inclusion. Option (iii), reserving and signing a next rung after the refusal inside `T`, changes FD-CT-03 and the Q6 premise and is not used without a new Founder decision. Option (i), 045a set rows on the launch path, is a B unit under FD-CT-62 if the schedule allows; it is not a freeze condition. No product launch path reserves set rows today (:174 note); the L0 seat's set-row flow is test code. This fails if a refusal at tip H0−1 that no planted fault explains appears where that path runs: the EXPLORATORY XD-1 runs, the L0 seat's item-2 and item-4 runs (their gate is the launch host's own H0: such a run must use H0 = 1000, oe's `GATE`, ARDR_RUN_V1 :1733 for item 2 and :2223 for item 4, the H0 that :1054 sets for every item-2 and item-4 run; and `exact_height_miss` is in the L0 policy key, :1053) or the Class T rehearsal (P5-002 scope (a)). One such refusal is enough, since Class E has one H0 and no second try; then A asks for (i) before the registration. Two of these sources run only after the registration: the L0 seat's item-2 and item-4 runs are the CONFIRMATORY ones (the driver's EXPLORATORY item 2 has no L0 seat and its item 4 uses the stand-in, :1224), and P5-002 observes from 11-20 to 11-27 (`CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:71`), after the 11-12 freeze. For such a refusal found there, A records it with that run or rehearsal and asks for (i) before the first Class E REGISTER broadcast instead, as a disclosed change of this line. Item 1 is no witness: its one public seat per gate is a synthetic winner with no H0−1 path (:534). *(Reviewer RV-121 P2-2; RV-127 P3-1 and P3-2, #664 issuecomment-6593050735.)* *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): §14.19 changes nothing at tip H0−1, so these witnesses stand; at the next tip the retained rung is broadcast only on a MATCH chain through that tip, and the L0 seat's turns after block G are decided before that gate; see §14.19 item 10. Citation fix (main `3031f432`, in #664 since `5f5b69ed`): ARDR_RUN_V1 :1733 and :2223 there are :1736 and :2226.* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): P5-002 now observes 11-25–11-26, with retries 11-27–12-01 (`CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:592`); read the dates above as that.*
- **(F3 command)** (amends the F3 row and section 10; A #667 -6569676867 For A (2)) The freeze checklist names one command that applies the frozen policy's `budget_sha256`: a local run in the shape of `RealNodesConfirmatoryEntry` with the frozen bytes under a throwaway `TEST_ONLY` registration, or the Python API in EXPLORATORY with `l0_policy=confirmatory_l0_seat(...)`. Its record must show the digest compared and equal. No EXPLORATORY CLI path applies the policy digest today (driver EXPLORATORY ITEM2 has no L0 seat; item 4 uses the stand-in; the L0 CLI passes no `l0_policy`), so without this command the comparison would first happen in a CONFIRMATORY run. B checks in the freeze unit that the command runs, and that its records label the source consistently (#667 A -6571519328 P3-1: `budget_source` must not read `REGISTERED_POLICY` outside CONFIRMATORY). This fails if the command cannot be run before the registration; then the comparison falls to the first CONFIRMATORY run, which refuses a mismatch only after the nodes start and the charge is made and released: the pre-node check `confirmatory_l0_seat` (l0 :1230-1243) compares `p0_rule` and `max_gate_attempts` only, and `budget_sha256` is compared in `L0Seat._budget()` (l0 :787-795, `L0_POLICY_BUDGET_SHA256`) when `finish()`, which first waits for L0's last release (l0 :606-618), writes `L0_BUDGET` (l0 :643; the comment at l0 :1226 says "right after the charge"). That unit then has a recorded observation and cannot be dispatched again (:577-581), so it ends as FAIL or an exclusion. A asks B in the freeze unit whether the comparison can move ahead of the nodes; until then the command is the only check before the registration. *(Reviewer RV-121 P2-3; RV-127 P3-3, #664 issuecomment-6593050735: "and released".)* *Dated note 2026-11-09 (A): B takes this ask as FZ-2 of its freeze unit B-FZ-a (PR due 2026-11-10; B -6738952904, -6739062946; A -6739587648): before the nodes start, the policy's `budget_sha256` is compared with the budget record digest the product uses in `exact_height_miss`, and the comparison after the last release stays. The product reads the frozen budget record only through B's FD-CT-62 unit B-FZ-p, built after A3 approves the exact `budget_record_sha256` and merged before the first CONFIRMATORY ARDR run (item 2 window 2026-11-17 to 2026-12-04, F1 at :1192); B-FZ-p is not a gate of this freeze merge. Until the frozen bytes exist the F3 command is checked with a `TEST_ONLY` policy, and A reruns it on the frozen bytes.* *2026-11-09 (A): FZ-1 and FZ-2 merged with #756 (`b9e060cb`); A reruns the FZ-1 command on the frozen policy after B-FZ-p (section 14.21).*
- **(H7)** (A's section 8.2 edit for `REORG_STEP6_RECORD_V1.md` :674-702 and its item 10; A-Q17-0 answer 7) **Public claim.** A statement by a project role (the Founder, A, B, RM, the Reviewer, Desktop) in one of the sources below that names a transaction id, a block hash, or a height of the network the record is for, and says it is included, accepted, confirmed or final. A raw log, artifact or record that only reports an observation is evidence, not a claim, unless a statement relies on it. **Sources** (the allowed values of `claim_sources_checked`, sorted): `EXTERNAL_POSTS` (anything the project posts outside GitHub; none is planned, listed so that checking it is explicit), `ISSUE_258`, `REGISTRATION_REPO` (the Founder's public registration repository: tags, receipts, notes), `RELEASES` (this repository's release notes and assets), `REPO_COMMENTS` (other issue and PR bodies, comments and review comments in this repository), `REPO_HISTORY` (commit messages, and files on branches other than `main`, such as the `codex-desktop-audit` reports), `REPO_MAIN` (files on `main` of this repository). **Claims list** (the bytes behind `claims_record_sha256`): canonical JSON as in that format's section 2, schema `bitagi.claude_track.public_claims.v1`, an object with `network`, `ancestor_height`, `old_tip_height` and `claims`, a non-empty array sorted by (`source`, `locator`, `id`, `kind`), no two entries equal in all four, whose entries are `{source, locator, kind, id, height, new_status, new_height, replacement_txid}`: `source` one of the seven values; `locator` the path at a commit for a file (`REPO_MAIN`, `REPO_HISTORY`, `REGISTRATION_REPO`), the commit SHA for a commit message, the comment id or the issue or PR number for a body (`ISSUE_258`, `REPO_COMMENTS`), the release id (`RELEASES`), the tag name for a registration tag, or the URL (`EXTERNAL_POSTS`); `kind` `TX_INCLUDED`, `BLOCK_ACCEPTED` or `STATE_AT_HEIGHT`; `id` the txid or block hash, 64 lowercase hex (a claim that names only a height is entered as `STATE_AT_HEIGHT` with `id` the hash of the old chain's block at that height, which the operator records); `height` within ancestor + 1 .. old tip; `new_status` `NOT_IN_ACTIVE_CHAIN`, `REINCLUDED` or `REPLACED`; `new_height` an integer only for `REINCLUDED`; `replacement_txid` 64 lowercase hex only for `REPLACED`. Each claim is reclassified in public by a dated note in its own source naming the claims list's SHA-256 (a new comment for `ISSUE_258` and `REPO_COMMENTS`; a same-line dated note for `REPO_MAIN`, as for the frozen files; a dated note appended to the release notes for `RELEASES`; a new dated note file for `REGISTRATION_REPO`, whose tags never move; for `REPO_HISTORY`, which is not edited, a new `ISSUE_258` comment naming the commit or branch file; a follow-up post in the same place for `EXTERNAL_POSTS`). **Host acceptance of `RECLASSIFIED`** (shape, the seven sources, and the list's digest equal to `claims_record_sha256`) is a B unit under FD-CT-62 (host code), required before the first Class E REGISTER broadcast and not a freeze condition: in the Class T Regtest experiments no public claim names a Regtest block, so the step-8 answer there is `NOT_APPLICABLE` (`NO_PUBLIC_CLAIM_ON_DISCONNECTED_BLOCKS`). On Class T Signet (P5-002, P5-003) a project comment can name a Signet transaction or block, and "Class T" alone is not a reason (`REORG_STEP6_RECORD_V1.md` :690-691), so there the operator checks the sources at each reorg that disconnects a block. Until the unit merges, a reorg that disconnects a claimed block stops the turn (fail closed) and the operator writes the incident record by hand (section 8.2). This fails if a claim appears in a place outside the seven sources (for example a personal post); then that source is added before Class E, as a disclosed change of this line. *(Reviewer RV-121 P3, 2026-11-02: the seventh source, locators, reclassification per source, height-only claims, the sort key and the Signet reason.)* *Dated line 2026-11-08 (A #258 issuecomment-6727751167): a step-8 `NOT_APPLICABLE` record and a mismatch `NOT_APPLICABLE` object list exactly these seven sources; a claims-list item's `source` is one of them.* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A #258 answer to B issuecomment-6729929318, Q-cf2-2): the claims list object also carries the key `schema` with the value `bitagi.claude_track.public_claims.v1`, beside `network`, `ancestor_height`, `old_tip_height` and `claims`; a list without it is refused.*

### 14.16 R2 §10 order-interaction pairs (J3 R2 table; draft 2026-11-02)

*Claude plan, draft, not frozen. It answers the J3 row of section 14.Z (:1210) and the assurance table's row :197 ("045b에 표 하나: 쌍마다 담당 캠페인과 case ID, 또는 불가능 근거와 대체 witness. P5-001 fault case의 분포·최대 자원·최소 전이 coverage"). Citations are at branch `claude/a-045b-freeze` `1637b151c5671844a718cd45a2a8f8b41c95556c`; lines of this file below 1012 are the same at `d9322e59` (section 14 intro). R2 is `docs/claude-track/inputs/assurance-handoff-20261026/BITAGI_PreRelease_Assurance_TDS_v20261024_R2.md` (`R2:n`); IG is the integration guide in the same folder (`IG:n`); "assurance table" is `docs/claude-track/plans/ASSURANCE_APPLICATION_TABLE_KO.md`.*

R2 §10 requires the preregistered inventory to include at least ten order interactions, and requires the reason and a substitute witness for any combination that cannot run to be fixed before observation (R2:384). This table maps each pair, in R2's order, to a campaign and a named case that exercises both orders of its two events (or the interaction R2 names). Where no such case exists, the row says what is missing, names the new case and its owner, or gives the reason the pair cannot run and its substitute witness. The mapping is fixed before the first P5-001 or XD-1 confirmatory observation (11-17, `CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:69`). It is not changed after a result, and the 10% exclusion rule never applies to these cases (:1089). The status is a mapping status, not a verdict. COVERED means that a named case reaches the order. It does not mean that the case's evidence is accepted for the current subject: reuse still needs the subject and change-impact checks of the assurance table (for example :113, :366-372). Layers are R2's L1 model, L2 real library and storage, L3 real nodes and processes, and L4 people (R2:373-378). R2 separates an injection request from a real termination and restart (R2:382). Here a "seam" is an in-process, `cfg(test)` stop "as if the process ended"; an "abort" is a real process termination. No P5-001 inventory is in the repository yet (its freeze is about 11-10; assurance table :505; `docs/claude-track/plans/notes/CT-P5-001_DRIVER_EN/2026-10-31_d1t-throughput.md:16`). That inventory cites these rows. P5-001 is A's, and B builds its driver (FD-CT-BATCH45 :63).

| Pair (R2:384) | The two events and the order concern | Covering campaign and case | Orders the case exercises | Status | Missing; substitute witness or new case | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. signature delivery × crash (서명 전달×crash; FC-01 R2:462, IC-04 R2:438, IG:281) | Signed bytes cross a boundary: host Sign hand-off, offline signing, media export, host import, transport hand-off. A crash just before or just after each crossing must keep the signature, charge, exposure and approval, with no second hand-off. | Offline signer crash fixtures (CT-P3-044a-2 E3c, `offline-crash-fixtures` debug build, real abort via `--crash-at`): `crates/bitagi-signer/tests/offline_durable_sign.rs:1176` `each_crash_point_restarts_to_its_table_row`, `:3407` `each_export_crash_point_restarts_to_its_row`, `:3508` `each_verify_crash_point_restarts_to_its_row`; `crates/bitagi-signer/tests/offline_conflict_set.rs:690`. Host seams (1d-3d-3r-1, #473): `crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/step8_tests.rs:773` `a_stop_between_the_file_and_the_entry_resumes`, `:814`, `:1184`; `crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/intent_tests.rs:135` `a_stop_after_the_hand_off_keeps_the_cn01_mark`, `:331`; `crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/retry_bound_tests.rs:211`. Wallet: `crates/bitagi-wallet/tests/reservation_recovery.rs:668` | Signer: one abort at each named crash point of the sign, export and export-verify tables, L2 abort; the export and export-verify cells (`:3407`, `:3508`) return early and pass without `BITAGI_OFFLINE_REAL_MOUNTS`, so they reach their points only on the privileged job. Host: after the Sign file and before its entry, and after the transport hand-off and before `Submitted`, L2 seam only (`crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/act.rs:1655-1668`, `:2201-2216`). REGISTER path only | PARTIAL | No real host-process abort at the Sign hand-off or at the import (seam only; R2:382). No BEAT export/import crash (WA-03 cells, assurance table :109). No L3. S08 is not a witness, because it observes store hooks only (`CRASH1000_PLAN_CT-P4-006_EN.md:351`). New case: `abort_at` points after `SigningHandedOff` and after the operator import in the real-node L0 worker, in the way `crates/bitagi-launch-host/tests/two_node_reorg.rs:364` aborts, run by `tools/claude_track_regtest_offline_export.py`. The BEAT cells come with the BEAT unit. The FC-01 non-store census (assurance table :215) lists both | B (cases), A (inventory), Reviewer |
| 2. restore × reorg (복원×reorg; FC-05 R2:466, DR-02 R2:825) | A backup restore (ledger, store, host file, off-host record) taken on one side of a reorg and put back on the other must not lose the Reorg67 raise or its ancestor, rewind Heart time, or reuse a pre-restore entry. Both orders: reorg then restore, and restore then reorg | `crates/bitagi-launch-host/tests/hooks.rs:738` `a_ledger_restored_below_a_recorded_raise_is_refused`; `:971` `a_ledger_restored_below_a_raise_while_the_host_runs_is_refused_on_a_turn`; `crates/bitagi-launch-host/tests/rotation.rs:583` `a_ledger_restored_below_the_binding_with_no_ancestor_is_finished` (a restore below the binding, with no reorg) | Reorg raise recorded, then the ledger alone restored below it: refused (L2, stand-in nodes) | PARTIAL | No case for a restore followed by a reorg. No BEAT re-broadcast case (WB3, :1166-1167). A re-sent XD-1 run counts `T = ∞` (:640-647); that is a rule, not a case. New cases: (a) a control, with the late acceptance before any resend: an L2 transport cell where the fake node accepts the bytes after the read returned absent, then a host turn, expecting `AwaitingInclusion` and no second hand-off. (a′) The missing order itself: the proxy keeps the first send and returns a timeout to the caller, the host resends on its next turn, and only after the proxy has seen the resend does it release the first send. (b) The L0 ladder cannot produce the reverse order: a release that raises ends the ladder with the error stored (`tools/claude_track_ardr_l0.py:956-967`), and a release that returns while rung k is in neither mempool ends it with `L0_RUNG_NOT_IN_MEMPOOL` (:1073-1075), so rung k + 1 is never released while rung k is unresolved. The L0 cell therefore asserts that fail-closed end: with rung k held by the proxy, the run ends with one of those two codes, and no rung k + 1 bytes reach either node. A host-level reverse order between different bytes needs a product path that releases rung k + 1 while rung k's hand-off is unresolved; B names that path and its transport fixture in the pair-6 unit, or records that none exists, which closes the rung half by construction. (a′) records both sends' txids and their order, and the final mempool read; that tells it apart from (a). (c) A BEAT-unit WB3 cell. *(Desktop r4440821458 and r4440887415, 2026-11-02.)* | B, A |

Status count: 0 COVERED, 8 PARTIAL (pairs 1–7 and 10; pair 3 was COVERED until RV-123), 2 NOT_COVERED (pairs 8 and 9). Pair 9 becomes CANNOT_RUN, with the substitute above, if the BEAT unit misses item 3's registration. No pair is CANNOT_RUN today. Five pairs (1, 4, 5, 6, 10) have a BEAT share that waits for the BEAT unit; their REGISTER share is mapped above.

**P5-001 fault cases (R2:386).** R2 asks a campaign with random draws to fix its seed list, run length, repeat count, fault distribution, maximum resources and minimum transition and pair coverage first; empty quantities make it NOT_READY (R2:386). The current P5-001 cases give the following:

- *Seed, length, repeats.* One 32-byte seed with SHA-256 draws (:530); its value is set at the freeze. Counts (:519-524, :546; :1083-1085): item 1 is 200 gates in one run; item 2 is 200 one-gate runs; item 3 is 30 cohorts of 17 gates, each past birth + 2,017; item 4 is 50 runs for each of four scenarios; the pool is 50 runs of 4 gates. N is fixed, with no early stop (:571-574). *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): a ninth experiment, ITEM3W, is 19 single-plant runs to height 12,000 with no random draw; see §14.18 (:1585).*
- *Distribution.* Faults are placed by scenario and run index, not drawn. Item 4 reorgs are depth 1 for even `r` and depth 2 for odd `r`, 25 each (:1070). Censorship covers G … G + 143 (:1079). The self REGISTER pays the 600-sat floor (:1065). The fee-asymmetry factor 10 is PENDING_FREEZE (:1078). Item 3 alternates abandonment and capture cohorts, 15 each (:539). Draws touch market inputs only: item 2 fees are uniform in [b, 4b] and `t_G` in [1, 1,200] s (:556-559); item 1 draws ownership, ancestry, key reuse and winners (:552-555); the pool producer is 50/30/20 (:560). The DRAFT test fault is refused in CONFIRMATORY (`ARDR_RUN_V1.md:1385-1389`). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C22): factor 10 is fixed by item 4's follow-up registration (F15 :1198).*
- *Maximum resources.* Per dispatch: at most 64 slices; a slice must fit 30 + units × `unit_minutes` + 15 ≤ 330 minutes; at most 400 observations (`ARDR_RUN_V1.md:570-577`, `:1077-1096`). A node call has 300 s and a worker call 1,800 s, with no retry (`:1691-1696`). The ITEM1 unit is 10,500 s, with a measured gate loop of 1,829.6 s at heights up to about 28,800 (:1088). The L0 seat has `max_gate_attempts` 24 (:1053). An asymmetric fee is at most 90,000 sat (:1079). Reorg depth is at most 2. H0 = 1000 (:1054). FD-CT-91 job-minute records apply (FD-CT-BATCH45 :64).
- *Transitions.* Item 4's label table is the de facto list of judged launch-core transitions: `Stale` with the opening missed, `IncludedNoEffect`, the E9-d chain `Done:Born` → `Refuse:Locked(Reorg67)` → Class T release → `Stale`, and the E10-s same-block loss (:413-419, :1068). Item 4 logs what it does not cover (:1067). Item 3 judges CURRENT → TIMEOUT for zero-BEAT and capture cohorts, and the zero-BEAT report at the 15th gate (:539). *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-104, BATCH53): ITEM3W also judges CURRENT → TIMEOUT at B + 2,017 and at B + 4,033, and a TIMEOUT after a same-block BEAT; see §14.18 (:1585).*

Missing:
1. *Pair coverage is zero.* Every item-4 run has one fault kind and no run combines two faults, so P5-001 as designed exercises none of the ten pairs. Natural P5-001 cells are pair 2 (a reorg run plus a restore), pair 4 (a reorg run plus a disagreement across G), pair 6 (the L0 ladder plus a held answer) and pair 9 (item 3 plus repeated BEATs).
2. *No minimum transition list.* No stated set of launch-core and Heart transitions has a minimum count of 1 that the judge checks as reached. Examples: `Done:Born`, `Stale`, `IncludedNoEffect`, the Reorg67 lock and release, `HeartTimedOut`, and a second BEAT moving the CURRENT Anchor. R2's "도달하지 못한 필수 hook 0" (R2:390) has no P5-001 counterpart.
3. *Cases already named elsewhere and not yet in P5-001.* The HC-03 burst case (assurance table :191), IC-08/DR-07's second BEAT (:195), HC-05's first-BEAT censorship or removal (:194), WA-01's condition-6 cohort (:108; :1060) and WA-04's reorg cell with a waiting BEAT (:110). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): WA-01 is now a named case: `wa01_a_timed_out_birth_rotates_at_once_and_settles` (`crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/ef_settle_tests.rs:577`), an EF-settle test, not a P5-001 case. The P5-001 inventory (`plans/CT-P5-001_INVENTORY_EN.md`) lists the rest.*
4. *Item 3 resources.* No `unit_minutes` or slice plan is set for runs past birth + 2,017. A 4,096 witness needs about 6,100 accelerated blocks at an estimated 1–6 h of machine time each (assurance table :485, :505). D1t (#621) measures host-turn throughput, and A reads it on 11-10. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C22): read on 2026-11-09 (F1 :1192). Item 3's `unit_minutes` and slice plan stay open for item 3's follow-up registration (`CT-P5-001_INVENTORY_EN.md:189`).*
5. *Values still open.* The seed value, factor 10 (F15) and `b`/`gates_per_run` (F10) are freeze values (section 14.Z). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): factor 10 is fixed by item 4's follow-up registration (:1198). The seed and the F10 values are this freeze's and are written before its merge.* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C22): the F10 values are written in §14.21 :1901, and the seed follows its rule there.*

**This fails if** a case marked COVERED (or the covered share of a PARTIAL row) does not reach its order in its own log (a seam never set, a crash point not reached, or a phase skipped), if a cited case is renamed or moved before the freeze SHA without its row being cited again, or if the BEAT execution unit has not merged before item 3's follow-up registration. In that last case pair 9, and the BEAT share of pairs 1, 4, 5, 6 and 10, become CANNOT_RUN for that registration with the substitutes named above, written before the first observation. *2026-11-04 (FD-CT-105, BATCH53): the BEAT execution unit is now BEAT-3a and BEAT-3b; pair 9 and the BEAT share of pairs 1, 4, 5, 6 and 10 need BEAT-3a, the host BEAT act, merged before item 3's follow-up registration, and the share of pairs 4, 5, 6 and 9 that runs on the gated path at tip ≥ H0 (the cutover at E, the re-broadcast below E, the basis switch) needs BEAT-3b; a share whose unit has not merged becomes CANNOT_RUN for that registration, with the substitutes above; see §14.19 item 3.*

**A decisions on this table (2026-11-02).**
- *"Unit" in pair 7* (R2:384 "단위 변경") is read both ways: a unit of measure (sat/vB against sat/kvB, blocks against seconds) and a record format or schema. The unit-of-measure share needs the new negative fixture the row names: an input in the wrong unit is refused, never converted (owner A, with the Q17 read that feeds `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb`).
- *KS-04 for pair 10* is required, not optional: the exposure record and `ChargeRevoked` must survive a campaign stop. It is a B cell before the first Class E REGISTER broadcast. KS-02 and KS-03 wait for the BEAT path.
- *Pair coverage in P5-001* is stated, not empty: the CONFIRMATORY runs of items 1–4 and the pool combine no two faults, by design. The preregistered experiments, counts and Founder values (FD-CT-92) stay as they are. Each pair's witness is the case named in its row: L2 or L3 cells in B units, EXPLORATORY runs, or the substitute. The P5-001 inventory (about 11-10) lists these as non-confirmatory witness cases with their ids. Adding combined-fault cells to the CONFIRMATORY runs would change the registered design after this mapping and is not done. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): the inventory is `plans/CT-P5-001_INVENTORY_EN.md`; its section 1 lists each pair's non-confirmatory witness cases with their ids.*
- *Minimum transition list.* A writes it into the P5-001 inventory before the freeze: each listed transition (at least `Done:Born`, `Stale`, `IncludedNoEffect`, the Reorg67 lock and its Class T release, `HeartTimedOut`, and a second BEAT moving the CURRENT Anchor once the BEAT unit exists) has a minimum of one reach, checked by the judge from the raw logs. A transition that is not reached makes that experiment NOT_ESTABLISHED, never a pass. The judge check is a J4 item. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): the list is section 2 of `plans/CT-P5-001_INVENTORY_EN.md` (TR-1 to TR-9, with E10-s and the first BEAT added). "A J4 item" reads as the judge stage of each experiment: item 4 in J5 and item 3 in J6 (`formats/ARDR_JUDGE_V1.md:520-521`).*
- *Pair 8 (human absence).* Under FD-CT-53 no second person holds a role (FIRST_BEAT_OPERATIONS §7.9). The absence rehearsal therefore declares the only Signer absent and expects the cap stop and an exact record, with no substitute signer and no Founder action. If the Founder prefers to take part in it, that preference goes into the A3 freeze request, not a separate question. The EXPLORATORY run with the decision held past the cap is A's.

### 14.17 A decisions, third batch (2026-11-02)

Settled by A in this draft, in the form of section 14.15. Neither changes a Founder value (FD-CT-92) or Exact D3 Core.

- **(B3)** (amends fee policy section 6 and section 8's SSoT:1798 bullet; SSoT:1798; TDS 8.10, TDS:1546, TDS:1917; FD-CT-92 row 2) **Reserve-cycle target.** SSoT:1798 requires a release policy to state one but does not define it. For SEM-1 the target is one cycle that spans the campaign: the fee reserve is funded once, before the first REGISTER broadcast, to cover the section 6 preregistered counts (24 gate attempts, 168 REGISTER replacements, 60 BEATs, 100 BEAT replacements, the two-node recovery minimum and the prolonged high-fee incident), each priced as section 6 and A -6545342828 price it (`max_fee_per_candidate_sat`, `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb` × measured vsize, the linear `Step` reserve). No refill is planned between H0 and the last of the 60 BEATs. The operator checks the remaining reserve against the remaining counts before H0 (TDS:1546) and before each BEAT broadcast. TDS:1546's "emergency reserve" is read as the part of this one reserve that prices the two-node recovery minimum and the prolonged high-fee incident (section 6's last two rows): it is checked with the rest and never counted as free for routine BEATs or replacements (Reviewer RV-125 P3); when the reserve falls short, broadcasting stops (TDS:1917, "Reserve below policy -> broadcast lock") and the stop is written in the incident record, never continued on a short reserve. This is an operator line: no code in `crates/` or `tools/` at `d8e5e5dc` locks on the reserve, and a code lock would be a B unit under FD-CT-62, not a freeze condition. A refill during the campaign never lowers the counts; one that changes a cap is a section 6.3 record change (section 14.15, S7), and one that does not is an operator line in the incident record. Reason: SEM-1 has one Heart and a fixed BEAT count, so a rolling cycle (some number of days of BEATs between refills) would add a funding transaction and a signing step the campaign does not need, and each refill would be one more point where the reserve could be short. This fails if one campaign-length cycle cannot be funded once: #671 §6.4 prices it at 42.8–44.1 M sat at the freeze-like `b` with the feerate cap at 1,464 sat/vB, before any per-candidate cap (whether the reserve rule takes that cap is open there); then A proposes a two-cycle target with a named refill point, asked with the Q1 re-ask. A per-candidate cap below 263 × `p` (A6, triggered since A4) is not fixed by any cycle target; the Q1 re-ask decides it (RV-125). *2026-11-02 (FD-CT-102, #258 issuecomment-6581164081; #671 note §9 A-1, A-2, §9.5, the file B4 cites, on `main` since `8abe2362`): this note replaces two parts of the body above. (1) In "each priced as section 6 and A -6545342828 price it (`max_fee_per_candidate_sat`, `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb` × measured vsize, the linear `Step` reserve)", the BEAT replacement `Step` reserve and the two-node recovery now price at F_B = min(`max_feerate_sat_per_kvb`, ⌊1,000 × `max_fee_per_candidate_sat` / BEAT vsize⌋) = 114,068 sat/kvB, not at `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb` (1,464,000); the incident trajectory is clamped at F_B; and the REGISTER replacement step's c takes the cap term 140,845 (A-1, A-2). (2) In the failure condition, "#671 §6.4 prices it at 42.8–44.1 M sat at the freeze-like `b` with the feerate cap at 1,464 sat/vB, before any per-candidate cap (whether the reserve rule takes that cap is open there)" reads: §9.5 prices it at 5.2–6.5 M sat, 5,207,646 sat (`b` = 3,000) or 6,466,482 sat (`b` = 5,000), with the cap in the reserve rule (A-1). The freeze-time run fixes the amount, and a raised cap is funded before it applies. (Reviewer RV-127 P3-4, #664 issuecomment-6593050735.)* *2026-11-03 (A #664 issuecomment-6593322614): two phrases in the failure condition predate FD-CT-102. "asked with the Q1 re-ask" reads "asked as a new Founder question", and "the Q1 re-ask decides it (RV-125)" reads: FD-CT-102 decided it; the Founder chose 30,000 sat, below the 385,032-sat floor, and accepted the risk (A6).
