It is a ceiling: the 30,000-sat cap refuses the REGISTER replacement rungs 118–168 and BEAT replacement rungs 51–100 that the run prices (#671 §6.4).*
- **(B4)** (amends fee policy :136-139 and section 8's bullet on them; QA S-061, B49:128; `CT-P3-009_BRIEF_EN.md:334`) **S-061 timing.** Option (a) applies: the Q17 output (A4) arrived on 2026-11-02, before the 11-09 bound, and the reserve model has run on the Q17 record (#671, `docs/claude-track/plans/notes/CT-P3-045b_PREREGISTRATION_DRAFT_EN/2026-11-02_q17-numbers.md` §6: the product planner, `bitagi preflight` in mode `FEE_RESERVE_PLAN`, built at main `dda14567`; five runs over the incident period of A7 and the freeze-like `b` against the section 6 counts, each with its request and result SHA-256). The #671 note cited above is on `main` since `8abe2362`; the § numbers here are its. This note replaces four parts of the body above. (1) "section 10 cites the Q17 file's SHA-256, the build commit and the five SHA-256 pairs": the B4 reading is §9.4's `b3000` and `b5000` runs at FD-CT-102's cap, and section 10 cites the Q17 file's SHA-256, the build commit and those two runs' request and result SHA-256 pairs (§9.4); the three `inc` runs are stress cases, not the B4 reading (§9.2 A-8, §9.11). (2) "The planner prices at `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb` (1,464,000 from the record) and reads no per-candidate cap, so the Q1 answer changes only …": the Q1 answer is FD-CT-102 (A1), and the two runs take F_B = 114,068 sat/kvB in place of 1,464,000 for the BEAT replacement step and the two-node recovery rate, clamp the incident trajectory at F_B, and give the REGISTER replacement step's c the cap term 140,845 (c stays 52,000 and 86,000 in these runs), so the cap enters the planner through F_B and c (§9.2 A-1, A-2, §9.3); B3's amount is §9.5's. (3) The #672 limit: the fee read's refusal at 1,778 of the 4,320 incident heights stands, but "the product's 1,000 sat/vB admission ceiling is below 66–73 of the 100 priced BEAT replacement rungs" does not hold at the cap. No priced line of either run is above that ceiling (§9.4 :330): the BEAT ladders top at 223,000 and 221,000 sat/kvB, and with A-1's step ⌈2 × (F_B − `b`) / 101⌉ the 100th rung stays below 2 × F_B + 100 = 228,236 sat/kvB at every `b` the ladder accepts. What the runs price but the product does not build is now rungs 51–100 of the BEAT ladder (and REGISTER rungs 142–168 at `b` = 5,000), above the 30,000-sat cap, which the launch host refuses before signing (§9.2 A-1, §9.4). (4) Reason's "the run is recorded and the Reviewer can see it before the 11-12 freeze": the two runs are recorded in the note by their inputs, totals and SHA-256 pairs (§9.4), which the Reviewer reads before the freeze (RV-129 recomputed the totals, #258 issuecomment-6593008350); the run files are not in the repository, and the freeze-time run with the live `b` (F10) replaces them, its files going into the repository with the freeze record (§9.11). (Reviewer RV-127 P3-4, #664 issuecomment-6593050735.)* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze): #672 is VERIFIED_FIXED (merge `42a26db9`, RV-135), so this line's #672 residual does not arise.* *2026-11-09 (A, freeze): the freeze-time run at the live `b` is in section 14.21 (`b6000fz`).* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C7): the injected-broadcast-failure half of SSoT:1798 is assigned to CT-P5-001's first-BEAT failure cohort (item 3; `CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:69`, "첫 BEAT 실패 코호트"; owner A). As preregistered, item 3 injects no broadcast failure and has no reserve metric (:382, :1059), and ITEM3 CONFIRMATORY is refused until its cohort unit is built (`CT-P5-001_INVENTORY_EN.md:21`). Its window is 11-17–12-04 with the result record 12-08 only if the BEAT execution unit merges by about 11-24 (`CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:537`); otherwise A adds a date line there. Until that cell has a result, this half is untested and no claim cites it as met. FEE V1 :139's "Done" and :156's "Settled" cover the historical-trace half only; FEE V1 is frozen and not edited.*

### 14.18 ARDR withdrawal and review rows (amends sections 4.3–4.6 and 10; FD-CT-104, BATCH53, 2026-11-04)

(Claude plan; FD-CT-104, BATCH53. Sources: A's red-team review of 2026-11-04, findings ECON-01 and CORE-01, both P2 and both confirmed by a non-author verifier.) Every rule in this section is a proposal. It takes effect only once FD-CT-104 is recorded. Until then §4.4 stands as FD-CT-92 row 8 adopted it. The one exception is freeze condition 10 below, a Claude-plan freeze check that applies from now: before the freeze, FD-CT-104 is either recorded or this section is taken out. *2026-11-05 (Claude plan; Reviewer RV-144, #664 issuecomment-6650421190): on the same-line notes marked "FD-CT-104, BATCH53" the record id reaches only the consequence change of FD-CT-92 row 8 for both 15-rows; the W values and residuals 1–13 stand as tooling 6 (:1646) states.*

Scope: this section would amend sections 4.3, 4.4, 4.5, 4.6 and 10, add a dated note to the section 11 Q7 row (:977), and amend the section 14 lines :1043, :1044, :1071, :1088, :1089, :1141, :1148, :1221, :1250 and :1253. It changes no Exact D3 Core rule, `rules_hash` or replay result. These are release-policy rows (SSoT:2479, :2855). Section 14 lines (:1012 and above) are cited at #664 head cf47e639; lines below 1012 are the same at d9322e59 (section 14 intro).

Status and amendment:

- (Founder, 2026-11-04) "권고대로 진행" (#258 issuecomment-6623155110) approves fixing both findings in #664. Asked whether that also covers changing FD-CT-92 row 8's consequence for the two 15-counts from withdrawal to review, with withdrawal moving to the W rows, the Founder answered "네 그렇게 읽어도 됩니다" (#258 issuecomment-6624331618).
- (for the FD-CT-104 record, once made) FD-CT-104 would record both lines and name the consequence change explicitly.
- (for the FD-CT-104 record, once made) The values of FD-CT-92 row 8 for the two rows would stay on record as the prior policy, with this section as the reason.
- (amends :434, :435, :437-439; FD-CT-92 row 8) Under FD-CT-92 row 8 (Founder, 2026-10-27), each of the two 15-counts of §4.4 leads to withdrawal. Once FD-CT-104 is recorded, both 15-counts would lead to a disclosure and a Founder review instead.
- Automatic withdrawal would then move to the three paid or evidence rows W-C, W-P and W-DATA below. The 4-count is unchanged.
- (on :483-485, unchanged; SSoT:2476, :2845, :2851) This would be a pre-freeze change. No confirmatory run has started under §4.4 (:366-367), and no confirmatory P5-001 observation is planned before 11-17 (:1231). So no result is kept under, or relabelled from, the old rows.
- (SSoT:2476; Q7 :977) The W rows are quantitative withdrawal thresholds with missing-data and amendment rules. So ARDR item 6 stays met, and the defect of Q7 option (b) (record-only) does not arise.

Why the rows would change (findings):

- (ECON-01; Core :600, :604, :651) The §4.4 15-counts can be forced for about 3,200–8,100 sat. That is 15 minimum-fee REGISTERs at 1 sat/vB, with or without 330-sat Anchor outputs; the red team's figure is about 7,500.
  - Path A: one funding source, which gives the common-control row.
  - Path B: 15 unrelated wallets whose Anchors are distinct provably unspendable P2TR keys. That gives the zero-BEAT row about 4,177 blocks after the project's birth (4,033 after the first non-project gate opens).
  - Core needs no proof of possession (Core :651).
  - The project REGISTERs only for its own Heart, so later gates are uncontested.
  - A count on gates cannot tell capture of empty gates from exclusion of honest applicants.
- (CORE-01; SSoT:2504, :2514, :2519) A careful capturer evades both rows. He uses a fresh key and unlinked funding per Heart, makes one token BEAT (a same-block BEAT counts as first-BEAT success, SSoT:2504), then lets the Heart TIMEOUT. Each gate is a singleton cluster and never zero-BEAT, while `population_realization` falls toward 0 with no threshold.
- (critique of 2026-11-04) Any automatic trigger on observables the attacker controls on both sides is cheap to force. This includes "contested gates only" (a free unmined decoy), losing-candidate fees (a miner gets its own fee back) and mempool timing. So no row below counts contest, losing candidates, mempool observations or fee rank.

Definitions used by the new rows:

- (Core :243-246, :590-600, :604) **Gate g** is the g-th accepted non-project birth at or after `first_effective_height`. The project's gate, if any, is gate 0 in plans and is in no window.
  - **B_g** is its birth height.
  - **O_g** is its opening height. It is `first_effective_height` when no accepted birth, project or not, precedes B_g. Otherwise it is B_prev + 144, with B_prev the latest accepted birth before B_g, project or not.
  - So when the project is born first, gate 1 opens at H_P + 144.
  - A gate is **opening-block** when B_g = O_g.
- **Package pkg_g** is the winning REGISTER R_g, its ancestors inside block B_g and its descendants inside block B_g.
  - Leave out every transaction that spends a CURRENT Anchor of the deployment (BEAT or BROKEN spend), together with its descendants.
  - Siblings (another child of an ancestor) are not in pkg_g.
  - F_g is the sum of pkg_g's fees, read from prevouts on the active chain. V_g is the sum of their vsizes.
  - Every vsize in this section is ⌈weight / 4⌉, not the sigop-adjusted mempool vsize.
- **Other transactions of B_g** are every transaction of B_g except the coinbase, pkg_g, every REGISTER-shaped transaction and every transaction that spends a CURRENT Anchor at its running-state position, with its descendants.
  - REGISTER-shaped means `vout[0]` is the REGISTER carrier script of Core §7.2 for this deployment, with any `target_gate_height`.
- **Block floor m_g.** For each other transaction i, r_i = fee_i / vsize_i, with its own fee read from prevouts. It is not an ancestor or package feerate. All values are exact rationals.
  - m_g is the smallest r_i such that the other transactions with r ≤ r_i have total vsize at least half the total vsize of all other transactions.
  - m_g is 0 when there are no other transactions.
  - Why a median: the critique's minimum can be set to 0 by one zero-fee CPFP parent.
  - A median needs half the other vsize. In a full block no single sender sets it. In a block that is not full one sender can. But lowering m_g by Δ raises c*_g by at most Δ × max(V_g, 213), and the package must still pay at least 30,000 + m_g × V_g.
- **Counted cost** c*_g = 0 unless gate g is opening-block. Otherwise:
  - c*_g = max(0, floor(min(30,000; F_g − m_g × V_g; 213 × (F_g / V_g − m_g)))).
  - It is computed with exact rationals and floored once at the end.
  - V_REF = 213 vB is a constant of this section. It is the current draft value of ARDR_RUN_V1 section 9 `v` and of the judge's `CANDIDATE_VSIZE`. It does not follow the measured `v` of §14.Z A10.
  - Not counted: BEAT fees, Anchor amounts, out-of-band payments, losing candidates (mined or not) and mempool observations.
- **Why c*_g looks like this:**
  - c*_g ≤ F_g, so nobody counts more than was paid in block B_g.
  - The feerate term counts only the premium over the block floor, scaled to one 213-vB REGISTER. A large batch or a routine payout that is also a REGISTER counts about 213 × (r − m). For example, 10 kvB at 3 sat/vB with m_g = 1 counts 426 sat, not 30,000 (critique A2).
  - To reach 30,000, a package must pay at least max(30,000 + m_g × V_g; (30,000/213 + m_g) × V_g). That is at least 30,000 sat above the floor value of its bytes.
  - The opening-block rule stops a miner from waiting for its own block in an empty market. A REGISTER that is to be born in O_g must be public, so its fee goes to whoever mines O_g.
- **Cap 30,000 sat** is `max_fee_per_candidate_sat` (FD-CT-102, :1175). It would be fixed here as its own constant `W_COST_CAP_SAT`, frozen with the rest of section 14 at the freeze.
  - A later raise of the budget cap (045a §4.2; FD-CT-102 "the Founder raises it with a new budget record") does not move it.
  - Changing it is an amendment (SSoT:2851).
  - Meaning: a gate counts fully only when its winner paid at least what the project itself would pay at most for one candidate.
- **Strong-link clustering (STRONG_LINK)**, used by W-C only. Two gates are linked when either holds:
  - (K) their winning REGISTERs' Anchor keys are equal (the 32-byte witness program, ARDR_CLUSTER_V1 §5); or
  - (S) gate h's winning REGISTER directly spends an output of gate g's winning REGISTER R_g (g < h) worth at least 30,000 sat.
  - Links join transitively. A cluster's id is `S` followed by its first gate number.
  - Why (K): the Anchor key is the Heart's control key, so a third party that copies another's key hands that Heart to the key holder.
  - Why (S): only R_g's author chooses R_g's outputs. A third party is joined to an honest winner only if that winner pays it at least 30,000 sat, or if it gives the winner at least 30,000 sat that the winner's REGISTER spends.
  - Outputs of R_g's in-block ancestors and descendants are not used. An exchange batch confirmed in B_g as R_g's parent would otherwise link every co-recipient who registers from its withdrawal.
  - A child attached to an anyone-can-spend output of R_g would otherwise let any third party create such an output from its own funds at no cost.
  - Ancestry beyond one direct spend and outputs below 30,000 sat are not used either (dust sweeps, shared funding sources; critique A3; :463-465).
- **Tenure.** Birth i fails tenure when its Heart is ENDED (any cause) at a height ≤ B_i + 4,033.
  - Its status is established at the ending height if it failed, otherwise once B_i + 4,033 is processed.
  - 4,033 = 2 × 2,016 + 1. A Heart is CURRENT at B + 4,033 only with at least two BEATs, the last at B + 2,017 or later.
  - One BEAT at B + 2,016 ends the Heart at B + 4,033 (Core :520-521, :734-735). A same-block BEAT followed by TIMEOUT ends it at B + 2,017.
  - Several BEATs in one block bank no time (Core :741).
  - This is consistent with SSoT:2504 and :2519: first-BEAT success, but failed survival.
- **Established prefix.** At each height, let i be the highest non-project birth index such that every non-project birth with index ≤ i has an established tenure status.
  - The **tenure window** is the non-project births i−29 … i (fewer if fewer exist).
  - A birth whose status is not yet established holds i, even when later births are established.
  - An unresolved birth holds i until its 2,016-block deadline counts it as failed (W-DATA below).
  - Why a prefix: a failure is established up to 2,016 blocks before an earlier-born success. A window of the highest-indexed established births would admit recent failures ahead of older successes, and would fire below 15 failures in 30 consecutive births.
- **Evaluated prefix.** Every row reads the chain at 6 confirmations. A report with trigger height h takes effect at height h + 5, or at a later W-DATA deadline (below).
  - In Class T, effective heights are judged in ITEM3W only. Item 1 and item 3 keep the :1042 key.
- **The project.** The project's gate breaks every run (reading (b), :459-467). It is left out of every window, sum and cohort.

Withdrawal rows (automatic once FD-CT-104 is recorded; default meaning :474-478):

- **W-C, paid strong-link run.**
  - Runs are those of §4.4: R3 and reading (b) as written.
  - Clusterings: STRONG_LINK; in Class T also ownership.
  - W-C fires at the first gate t of a run where gates t−14 … t are all in the run and each has c*_g = 30,000. The sum over that 15-gate window is then 450,000 sat.
  - There is one report per run. Key: (clustering, cluster, `W_C`, trigger gate t), with the window's first gate recorded. Trigger height: B_t.
  - Why 15: 15 = floor(2,016/144) + 1, the most CURRENT Hearts under full capture (AUD ADM-1, :434).
  - Why a window at the cap and not a running sum: a miner cannot build the total from its occasional self-mined opening blocks.
  - One gate below the cap resets the window (plant T8).
  - Why STRONG_LINK only: a false link must not buy withdrawal. The cost is that a capturer avoids W-C with one intermediate transaction (residual 2).
  - W-C sets the price of forcing withdrawal. It is not a detector of careful paid capture.
- **W-P, paid tenure floor, linkage-free.**
  - Read the tenure window. E is the sum of c* over its births that failed tenure. A is the sum of c* over its births that succeeded.
  - W-P fires when E ≥ 450,000 and E > A.
  - It is edge-triggered: it reports again only after the condition has been false. Key: (`LINKAGE_FREE`, `W_P`, height).
  - Why: it does not depend on links or on how a Heart died. So CORE-01's singleton, token-BEAT capturer is counted once he has paid 450,000 sat of counted premium for Hearts that died.
  - Why a window: design 1 compared a running total of dead Hearts with the current live stock, which fires on any honest namespace older than its mean Heart lifetime (critique A1). A 30-birth window has no running total.
  - E > A means the forcer must outweigh the counted cost of honest Hearts that stayed.
- **W-DATA, unresolved evidence.** Two cases:
  - (i) Lag. Let H_e be the highest height through which the W rows are evaluated: replay `SYNCED` and node agreement `AGREED` (TDS:2426-2427), and every prevout read. *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): `SYNCED` here is the Rust replay's standing; a Rust/Python comparison gap in the availability family does not lower H_e, and an unreleased MISMATCH or divergence-family refusal holds H_e below the first height it names; see §14.19 item 3.*
    - While H_e is below the 6-confirmation tip, the public status shows `W_PENDING_DATA`.
    - If H_e stays more than 2,016 blocks below the 6-confirmation tip, the namespace withdraws (`W_DATA`).
  - (ii) Inside the evaluated prefix, a gate or birth may be unresolved: its winner, package fee, block floor (every prevout of B_g's other transactions), opening flag, link or ending status cannot be established.
    - It shows `W_PENDING_DATA` naming the gate.
    - Its deadline is 2,016 blocks after the gate's birth height B_g, or, for a birth's ending status, 2,016 blocks after the height at which that status would be established.
    - At the deadline each unresolved field takes its adverse value: an unresolved winner, package fee, block floor or opening flag gives c*_g = 30,000; an unresolved winner or link gives unknown control inside every adjacent run (R3); an unresolved ending status gives tenure failed.
    - If W-C or W-P is then met, the namespace withdraws.
    - Such a report keeps its key, and its effective height is max(trigger height + 5, the deadline).
  - Why: rule R3 (unseen capture is never read as healthy, :450-458), and SSoT:2506 (missing history is not zero failures).
  - Public chain data can always be replayed from raw blocks (TDS §14.4, TDS:1652-1659), so only the project's own failure, or an exploit of a replay divergence, can cause it.
  - A namespace the project cannot replay for 2,016 blocks is one it cannot support.

Review rows (disclosure plus Founder review; never automatic withdrawal):

- **D-C4** is §4.4 :434's 4-count, unchanged.
- **D-C15** is the same count at 15. Once FD-CT-104 is recorded, its consequence would change from withdrawal to review.
  - Clusterings in Class T: ownership and the item 1 link method (:380).
  - Class E: the item 1 link kinds over a mainnet shared-ancestry boundary, which A fixes before the Class E freeze (tooling 7).
- **D-Z15** is §4.4 :435's zero-BEAT 15-count, unchanged in counting (item 3 still expects it at the 15th gate, :539). Once FD-CT-104 is recorded, its consequence would change from withdrawal to review.
- **D-T, short tenure, linkage-free.**
  - Read the tenure window (the established prefix).
  - D-T fires when its tenure failures first reach 15. It is edge-triggered. Key: (`LINKAGE_FREE`, `D_T`, height).
  - It catches CORE-01's single-BEAT pattern at any fee.
  - To stay under it, a capturer must keep 16 of every 30 consecutive births CURRENT through B + 4,033.
- **D-P, population band, linkage-free.** Checkpoints are at H_k = H0 + 2,016k.
  - The cohort is the non-project births with H_k − 10,080 ≤ B ≤ H_k − 4,033, i.e. ages 4,033 to 10,080.
  - It is evaluated when the cohort has at least 15 births. A checkpoint whose cohort has fewer than 15 births is not evaluated and counts as false for the edge trigger.
  - It fires when CURRENT × 2 < cohort size. It is edge-triggered over checkpoints. Key: (`LINKAGE_FREE`, `D_P`, H_k).
  - Why a band: raw `population_realization` (SSoT:2514) falls with time in any empty or thin market, and a running survival ratio fires with certainty on finite honest lifetimes (critique A1).
  - The band asks whether births aged 4,033–10,080 are still CURRENT. Tenure failures are in the cohort too, so D-P also repeats what D-T saw. Its own reach is tenure-then-abandon at ages below about 7,056.
  - `population_realization(H)` itself is published at every checkpoint with no threshold.
- **Report contents.** Each linkage-free report also carries its counts: D-T the failures and the top index i; W-P the values E and A; D-P the cohort size and the CURRENT count. The judge compares them.
- **Repeat reviews.**
  - A D-C or D-Z run that continues gets a new review at every further 15 gates (lengths 30, 45, …).
  - While D-T's condition stays true, it gets a new review each time the window's top index i reaches i_0 + 30, i_0 + 60, …, where i_0 is the top index at the D-T report (`D_T_REPEAT`).
  - While D-P's condition stays true, it gets a new review at every third checkpoint after the first (k_0 + 3, k_0 + 6, …), which is one full cohort turnover (`D_P_REPEAT`).
  - Why: otherwise one Founder "continue" in a persistently thin market would cover any later capture.
  - Keys: (clustering or `LINKAGE_FREE`, subject, `D_C_REPEAT` or `D_Z_REPEAT`, trigger gate); (`LINKAGE_FREE`, `D_T_REPEAT`, height); (`LINKAGE_FREE`, `D_P_REPEAT`, H_k).
  - They are computed outside ARDR_CLUSTER_V1's report list, in `repeat_reviews`. So the [4, 15] thresholds (`METHOD_THRESHOLDS`) and the item 1 and item 3 expected lists do not change.
- **Consequence of every review row.**
  - A public disclosure update within 144 blocks of the effective height.
  - A dated Founder statement within 2,016 blocks of it: continue (with reasons), withdraw (`PUBLISHER_WITHDRAWAL`, with a reason, never reported as a threshold outcome), or narrow the scope.
  - If the statement is late, the status shows `REVIEW_OVERDUE`. Nothing is paused and nothing is withdrawn automatically.
  - The Founder cannot override a W row.
  - Corrections run one way: a recomputation by a fixed tool may add a W report, but never removes a withdrawal already carried out.
- **Why the 15-counts would lose automatic withdrawal.** Forcing them costs about 3,200 sat (ECON-01), and a review keeps a human backstop for real low-fee exclusion. That exclusion cannot be told apart on chain from an honest thin market (residual 2).

Missing data in the review rows:

- D-C and D-Z keep R3 and the item 3 censoring rule (:382, :450-458).
- A D-T birth or D-P cohort member with unresolved history is PENDING. At its W-DATA deadline it counts as failed (D-T) or as not CURRENT (D-P). PENDING (not yet established) is never UNKNOWN.

Review record contents (published; from design 2's R-MEAS and R-FR):

- The record holds:
  - the report key;
  - the gate or birth table (winner txid, clusters under each clustering, B_g, O_g, F_g, V_g, m_g, c*_g, BEAT heights, ending cause and height);
  - the SHA-256 of every input and of the tool, with the tool commit;
  - the Founder line.
- Published measurements, every 2,016 blocks and in every record, none with a threshold:
  - `population_realization`;
  - first-BEAT failure rate (SSoT:2497);
  - tenure-failure share;
  - age-specific survival and ending causes (SSoT:2519);
  - BEATs per birth within B + 4,033 (0 / 1 / ≥ 2);
  - singleton-cluster share;
  - mined losing candidates and their fees per gate.
- Mempool observations of competitors and mined losing candidates go into the record as non-binding material. An attacker controls them on both sides (critique B1-B5), so no outcome reads them.

Cost to force automatic withdrawal (before → after):

- Before: about 3,200–8,100 sat. Path A fires about 2,016 blocks after the first birth. Path B fires about 4,177 blocks after the project's birth (4,033 after the first non-project gate opens).
- After, W-C, non-miner:
  - Each of 15 consecutive opening-block gates of one strong-link cluster pays at least max(30,000 + m_g × V_g; (30,000/213 + m_g) × V_g).
  - That is at least 450,000 sat. It is about 60 times the red team's 7,500 and 0.9 × `max_fee_exposure_sat` (500,000, FD-CT-102).
  - It fires no earlier than 2,016 blocks after the window's first birth.
- After, W-P, non-miner: the failed births of one tenure window must carry at least 450,000 sat of counted cost. Each c* is at most 30,000, so that takes at least 15 failed births.
  - The forcer must also pay more than the counted cost of every tenure success in that window.
  - With zero-BEAT or same-block-BEAT births it fires 4,033 blocks after the first of them at the earliest.
- Miner with hash share s:
  - Public broadcast is forced by the opening-block rule, so the expected net cost is (1 − s) × 450,000: 405,000 at s = 0.1, 315,000 at 0.3, 225,000 at 0.5.
  - Self-mining needs all 15 opening blocks, with probability s^15.
  - A coalition of pools of share S that rebates fees out of band pays about (1 − S) × 450,000.
- Decoys: an unmined decoy (about 0 sat) and a mined losing decoy (its fee) change nothing (plants T6a, T6b).
- Batch or routine-flow REGISTERs count only their premium over the block floor (plant T12).
- Gift links below 30,000 sat do not join clusters (plant T13a), and neither do outputs of a descendant of R_g (fixture (ix)).
- Review rows stay cheap to force (about 3,200 sat at 1 sat/vB), but they cause only a disclosure and a review.
- Before birth, W-C lets a griefer stop the project's campaign after 15 consecutive gates outbid at the cap premium (about 450,000 sat). That is cheaper than exhausting the 24 attempts (about 24 × 30,001 = 720,024 sat). This is residual 11.
- W-DATA cannot be bought with fees.

What the careful capturer (CORE-01) would trigger:

- Pattern: fresh key, unlinked funding, one BEAT (same block or later), then TIMEOUT.
- Link rows (D-C, W-C under STRONG_LINK): nothing, by design. Core identifies no control (SSoT:2452). In Class T the ownership clustering shows the run, and that gap is the published limit.
- D-Z15: nothing, since a BEAT was accepted.
- D-T: a review at the 15th failure. With same-block BEATs that is B_1 + 4,033 (H_P + 4,177 when births follow the project every 144 blocks). With each BEAT at B + 2,016 it is B_1 + 6,049.
- W-P: withdrawal if the failed births carry 450,000 sat of counted premium (plant T4: W_P at H_P + 4,177). Below that: review only (plant T5; residual 2).
- D-P: a review at the first checkpoint whose cohort has at least 15 births of which more than half are not CURRENT (plants T2, T4, T5: H0 + 8,064).
- Evasion:
  - Keeping 16 of every 30 consecutive births CURRENT through B + 4,033 escapes D-T and W-P. That is real occupancy for about 28 days at about 2 BEAT fees per Heart.
  - Keeping Hearts past about age 7,056 before dropping them escapes D-P (residual 4).
  - Unlinked capture with Hearts kept alive is undetectable (residual 3).

Honest false positives:

- A thin honest market of short-lived Hearts gives D-T and/or D-P reviews, never a withdrawal (plant T11b: D_P only).
- An empty or very thin market gives nothing. D-P needs at least 15 births in its cohort, and `population_realization` is only published.
- An honest hot market gives W-P when, in one tenure window, the births that died within B + 4,033 carry at least 450,000 sat of counted cost and more than those that stayed. An example is 15 deaths each paying at least 30,000/213 ≈ 140.85 sat/vB above the block floor.
  - That is a real paid-population collapse, and SSoT defines the concern by outcome, not motive (SSoT:2440). It is disclosed.
- A wave of honest TIMEOUTs during a fee spike can give W-P if those births had paid the cap premium (residual 6).
- One honest service whose winners are strong-linked and pay the cap premium over 15 consecutive gates gives W-C. That is real concentration.
- The project's own replay outage longer than 2,016 blocks gives W-DATA (self-inflicted; TDS §14.4). *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): a Rust/Python comparison outage alone is not a replay outage and never gives W-DATA; see §14.19 item 3.*
- Item 1 judges no W, D-T or D-P row: its pinned tool does not compute them, and ITEM3W judges those rows.
- Item 3's W list is empty at rung-0 fees (⌈b × v / 1000⌉) for every v while b ≤ 70,417 sat/kvB, which is b ≤ 70,250 as a multiple of 250 (F11).
  - Then every c* is at most 14,999, so 30 failed births count less than 450,000 and no gate reaches the cap.
  - W-C alone stays empty up to b ≤ 140,840 sat/kvB (140,750 as a multiple of 250).
  - The judge computes the list from the plan in any case.

Class T: experiment ITEM3W (amends :382, :503, :523, :571-574):

- A new CONFIRMATORY inventory experiment, the ninth. Each plant runs once, as a required inventory case with 0 exclusions (§14.4 :1089, "A required inventory case allows 0 exclusions").
- NO-GO when the tool's W/D report list differs from the plan's expected list: a report missing, added, at the wrong trigger, with the wrong effective height or with the wrong counts. The judge pairs a missing and an extra report as `wrong_trigger`, like `wrong_gate`.
- A missing log or a `JUDGE_PLAN_MISMATCH:<field>` (new fields `package_fee`, `birth_height`, `beat_height`, `filler`) makes that run's W/D rows NOT_ESTABLISHED.
- Item mapping. A wrong D-C or W-C report is a NO-GO of the §4.5 repeat-winner row (:503). A wrong D-Z15, D-T, D-P, W-P, `W_PENDING_DATA` or `W_DATA` report is a NO-GO of item 3 (:382).
- ITEM3W NOT_ESTABLISHED makes both that row and item 3 NOT_ESTABLISHED.
- Order as item 1 (:1086): a NO-GO found on judged runs comes first, since ITEM3W's NO-GOs are deterministic.
- There are no random draws and no private keys. Every Anchor comes from a label `ITEM3W_<plant>_<role>_<n>` by the NUMS rule (F13 amended below). *(2026-11-04: first written as TEST_ONLY keys from the frozen seed; changed so the driver needs no signing code, B Q-W0 #664 issuecomment-6625963291, Q-W1 #258 issuecomment-6625977965.)*
- The judge computes every expected list from the plan with the rules above, never from the chain.
- Common plan:
  - H0 = 1000 and H_end = 12,000. Reports are listed when their effective height is ≤ H_end.
  - Gate 0 is the project: H_P = H0, born in the opening block. Its first BEAT runs under the frozen first-BEAT operations, as in item 3 (:539). Its later liveness affects no row and is not judged.
  - Gate j ≥ 1 is born at B_j = H0 + 144j in its opening block (O_1 = H_P + 144), unless stated.
  - No transaction goes in a block B_j other than the coinbase, pkg_j, transactions that spend a CURRENT Anchor (with their descendants) and REGISTER-shaped decoys, unless stated (T14 filler). Funding for later gates and every fee input of a BEAT confirm in a block that is not a B_j. So m_g = 0.
  - Cheap fee: 600 sat (the product floor).
  - Cap fee: F_cap(V, m) = max(30,000 + ⌈m × V⌉, ⌈(30,000/213 + m) × V⌉), with V the package's measured vsize.
  - "Linked": owner X, wallets W0–W3 round-robin. Gate 1 is funded by one X funding transaction inside the boundary. Each later winner's REGISTER directly spends an output (≥ 100,000 sat) of the previous gate's winning REGISTER. So item 1 ANCESTRY links (ARDR_CLUSTER_V1 §5: the ancestor set includes the winner itself) and STRONG_LINK (S) both join every gate. Cluster ids: `X`, `L1`, `S1`.
  - "Unlinked": owner X in the ownership truth, but a fresh key and fresh funding from the excluded source per Heart, so there are no links.
  - "Uncontrollable": a distinct NUMS Anchor per Heart used as the output key with no script path (F13 amended below), so nobody can spend it. Every other Anchor is a NUMS internal key with one `OP_TRUE` leaf, spent by script path. Core does not classify key path versus script path (SSoT §6.2), so those BEATs are Core BEATs.
  - "Maintained": a BEAT every 1,008 blocks after birth, to H_end.
- Reports are written as row@trigger. Cluster rows give clusters and trigger gate (birth height); linkage-free rows give the height. "Both" means ownership X plus link-based L1 (D rows) or STRONG_LINK S1 (W-C).

| Plant | Plan | Expected reports (nothing else) |
|---|---|---|
| T1 cheap linked (ECON-01 path A) | gates 1–15, linked, cheap, maintained | both D_C4@4 (1,576), both D_C15@15 (3,160) |
| T2 cheap path B (ECON-01) | gates 1–15, unlinked, uncontrollable, cheap | ownership D_C4@4, D_C15@15; D_Z15@gate 15 (5,177); D_T@5,177 (15 failures, i = 15); D_P@9,064 (k = 4, cohort 15, CURRENT 0) |
| T3 paid linked | T1 at F_cap(V, 0) | as T1, plus W_C@15 (3,160; window 1–15) under ownership X and STRONG_LINK S1 |
| T4 paid careful capturer (CORE-01) | gates 1–15, unlinked, F_cap, one same-block BEAT each, then no BEAT (TIMEOUT at B + 2,017) | ownership D_C4@4, D_C15@15, W_C@15; D_T@5,177; W_P@5,177 (E 450,000, A 0); D_P@9,064 (cohort 15, CURRENT 0) |
| T5 cheap careful capturer | T4 at 600 sat | ownership D_C4@4, D_C15@15; D_T@5,177; D_P@9,064 (demonstrates residual 2) |
| T6a / T6b decoys | T1 / T3, plus in each B_j one mined losing REGISTER from an unlinked wallet at 600 sat placed after the winner, plus one unmined decoy: a REGISTER-shaped transaction from an unlinked wallet that the miner node's mempool accepted by `sendrawtransaction` and holds just before B_j is mined, but that no explicit list includes *(2026-11-04: first written as below the miner node's `-blockmintxfee`, which an explicit-list miner does not apply; B Q-W0 #664 issuecomment-6625963291)* | identical to T1 / T3 |

| T9a in-block CPFP | T3, but each REGISTER pays 600 sat and has two change outputs; a child of change output 1 in B_j pays F_cap(V_R + V_child, 0) − 600; change output 2 (≥ 100,000 sat) funds the next gate | as T3 |

| T11a honest thin, long-lived | 20 births by 20 distinct owners at B_j = H0 + 432j (not opening-block), maintained | none |
| T11b honest thin, short-lived | 30 births by 30 distinct owners at B_j = H0 + 288j, BEATs at B + 1,000, B + 2,000 and B + 3,000 while ≤ H_end, then none (end B + 5,017) | D_P@11,080 (k = 5, cohort 20, CURRENT 3); no D_T, no W |
| T12 batch REGISTER (critique A2) | T1, but each REGISTER is a batch of V ≥ 2,130 vB paying 30,000 sat absolute (c* ≤ 3,000) | as T1 (no W_C) |
| T13a gift link below the bound (critique A3) | gates 1–15 by 15 distinct owners, F_cap, maintained; winner j's REGISTER pays a 29,999-sat output to owner j+1, whose REGISTER spends it along with its own fresh funding | link-based L1 D_C4@4, D_C15@15 only |

| T14a block floor | T3, plus in each B_j one filler transaction from the excluded source (not REGISTER-shaped) of about 1,000 vB whose fee is exactly 10 × its measured vsize (m_g = 10); REGISTERs at F_cap(V, 0) | as T1 (no W_C) |
| T14b | T14a with REGISTERs at F_cap(V, 10) | as T3 |

- Run count: 19 runs, heights at most 12,000. ITEM3W is not ITEM1's shape, so :1088 does not set its time. A sets ITEM3W's `unit_minutes` and slice plan from a measured EXPLORATORY ITEM3W run before its registration, under the same rule (:1088). The item 3 resource gap of :1259 applies too.
- Judge unit fixtures. These are plans, not regtest:
  - (i) T3 with gate 10's package-fee input removed: `W_PENDING_DATA` names gate 10 from tip 2,445 (B_10 + 5). Its deadline is B_10 + 2,016 = 4,456. The W_C key is unchanged (trigger gate 15, B_15 = 3,160), and its effective height is max(3,165; 4,456) = 4,456.
  - (ii) T3 with a one-block reorg, before 6 confirmations, that moves gate 15's birth to 3,161: gate 15 opens at 3,160, so it is not opening-block. No W_C; both D_C15@15 at 3,161.
  - (iii) W-P strictness and window size: 31 births by 31 distinct owners, no links; births 1–15 at F_cap, maintained; births 16–30 at F_cap, one same-block BEAT each, then TIMEOUT; birth 31 at B_30 + 145 (not opening-block), maintained. Expected: D_T@7,337 (i = 30); W_P@9,498 (i = 31, window 2–31, E 450,000, A 420,000). At i = 30, E = A = 450,000 gives no W_P. (iii-b) Counted cost below the cap (2026-11-04, Desktop #664 r4464876284): 16 births by 16 distinct owners, no links, at the common gates, each package F = 29,999 with V = 213 and m = 0 (c* = 29,999), one same-block BEAT each, then TIMEOUT. Expected: D_T@5,177 (i = 15); W_P@5,321 (i = 16, window 1–16, E 479,984, A 0); D_P@9,064 (cohort 16, CURRENT 0). The same with 15 births: D_T@5,177 and D_P@9,064 (cohort 15, CURRENT 0); no W_P (E 449,985).
  - (iv) T1 extended to 31 gates: both D_C4@4 (1,576), both D_C15@15 (3,160) and both D_C_REPEAT@30 (5,320).
  - (v) 200 distinct-owner births at every gate (B_j = H0 + 144j), F_cap, BEATs at B + 1,008, 2,016, 3,024 and 4,032, then none, evaluated to 40,000: D_P@11,080 (cohort 41, CURRENT 14); D_P_REPEAT@17,128, 23,176, 29,224 and 35,272; no W_P, no D_T, no W_C.
  - (vi) Mutants: opening flag inverted; cap 29,999 or 30,001; window 14 or 16; tenure offset 4,032 or 4,034; W-P window 29 or 31; E ≥ A in place of E > A; m_g as mean or minimum; BEAT or sibling counted in pkg_g; strong-link bound 29,999 or 30,001; (S) over outputs of pkg_g instead of R_g; a window over the established set instead of the established prefix; D-P band edges ± 1; effective height h + 4 or h + 6. Also (2026-11-04): W-P summing only births at c* = 30,000, killed by (iii-b).
  - A checks that each mutant is killed by a plant or a fixture. The sibling mutant needs a unit fixture of its own, and the BEAT mutant is killed by T4 only while T4's BEATs pay less than 30,000/213 sat/vB.
  - (vii) 120 distinct-owner births at every gate, F_cap. Births with j mod 5 ∈ {1, 3} make one same-block BEAT and then TIMEOUT; the others BEAT every 1,008 blocks. Evaluated to 40,000: no W_P, no D_T, no D_P. A window over the established set would give D_T and W_P at 8,201.
  - (viii) T2 extended to 45 gates, evaluated to 20,000: ownership D_C4@4, D_C15@15, D_C_REPEAT@30 (5,320) and @45 (7,480); D_Z15@15 (5,177), D_Z_REPEAT@30 (7,337) and @45 (9,497); D_T@5,177; D_T_REPEAT@9,497 (i = 45); D_P@9,064 (cohort 27, CURRENT 0); D_P_REPEAT@15,112 (k = 7, cohort 18, CURRENT 0).
  - (ix) T13b with each 30,000-sat gift paid by an in-block child of R_j instead of R_j itself: link-based L1 D_C4@4, D_C15@15 only (no S1 W_C).

Section 14 lines this section would amend (each once FD-CT-104 is recorded):

- (amends :1043 for ITEM3W) A `JUDGE_PLAN_MISMATCH` in an ITEM3W run makes that run's W/D rows NOT_ESTABLISHED. ITEM3W allows 0 exclusions, so the 10% rule does not apply to it.
- (amends :1044) The four W keys enter a W policy of their own as tool inputs, as `thresholds` [4, 15] does in the method policy. The registered method policy (`BITAGI_CT_ARDR_METHOD_POLICY_V1`) and the D2-2 method schema stay unchanged (tooling 1). The judge refuses a W policy whose key differs from its constant that cites this section, so the threshold is still a sentence of this file.
- (amends :1089) ITEM3W is a required inventory experiment under this rule.
- (amends :1141, F12) "A count unset is refused" applies to nine experiments. ITEM3W's count is 19 runs, registered in this section.
- (amends :1221, F13) The key-set labels also include `ITEM3W_<plant>_<role>_<n>`, which share no prefix with the draw labels. In ITEM3W they name NUMS points, not F13 keys: no ITEM3W label yields a secret scalar.
- (amends :1221, F13) An ITEM3W Anchor is not an F13 key. Its point is the x-only point of a NUMS derivation from its label, so nobody holds its scalar. An uncontrollable Anchor uses that point as the output key with no script path; every other Anchor uses it as the internal key with one `OP_TRUE` leaf. B states the hash-to-curve rule and the leaf encoding in ARDR_RUN_V1 before ITEM3W's registration, and the judge recomputes both.
- (amends :1148) In the Class E subset table, "abandoned births and age-specific survival" becomes measured for ages up to 10,080 (ITEM3W: D-T tenure to B + 4,033 and the D-P band 4,033–10,080, both Class T). Ages above 10,080 stay not claimed (residual 4).
- (amends :1071 and §14.16 :1250, :1253) ITEM3W uses no random draw (labels only: the NUMS rule, and no key). It is 19 single-plant runs to height 12,000. It judges the transitions CURRENT → TIMEOUT at B + 2,017 and at B + 4,033, and a TIMEOUT after a same-block BEAT.

Residual for the Class E notice (it cannot be designed away):

1. Withdrawal can be bought.
   - A non-miner can force it with about 450,000 sat of counted premium.
   - A miner of share s pays an expected (1 − s) × 450,000; a fee-rebating coalition of share S pays (1 − S) × 450,000.
   - A coalition controlling the opening blocks can force it for close to nothing.
   - The price is set; forcing is not impossible.
2. Capture below the cap premium per gate never triggers W-C, and capture whose Hearts stay CURRENT past B + 4,033 never triggers W-P. *(2026-11-04, Desktop #664 r4464876284: this item first read "Cheap capture never withdraws automatically", which held for W-C only.)*
   - W-C needs 15 consecutive gates each at c* = 30,000. Paying 29,999 instead of 30,000 per gate, or a birth after the opening block, avoids it (the W-C cliff, critique A5).
   - W-P sums c* below the cap too. Capture whose Hearts die by B + 4,033 withdraws once the failed births of one tenure window carry at least 450,000 sat of counted cost and more than its successes: 16 unlinked opening-block births at c* = 29,999, each with one same-block BEAT and then TIMEOUT, give E = 479,984 and A = 0 and withdraw, while 15 give E = 449,985 and do not (fixture (iii-b)). Below that sum (cheap capture at 600 sat per gate carries E ≤ 18,000 over 30 births), and for sub-cap capture whose Hearts stay CURRENT, capture triggers only disclosure and a Founder review. It cannot be told apart on chain from an honest thin market of short-lived Hearts, so real exclusion of low-fee honest applicants depends on the Founder's review.
   - W-C's STRONG_LINK clustering does not catch a capturer who has read the rules. A fresh Anchor key per Heart, plus one intermediate transaction between winners or outputs below 30,000 sat, removes every (K) and (S) link for about one transaction fee.
   - Paid capture that keeps its Hearts CURRENT past B + 4,033 therefore never reaches a W row, at any premium. It reaches D-C15 review only if the full item 1 link method joins it.
3. Unlinked capture whose Hearts are kept alive is undetectable. Core proves neither control nor independence (SSoT:2452).
4. Abandonment after about age 7,056 (about 49 days) shows up only in the published measurements. D-T covers ages up to 4,033, and D-P covers 4,033–10,080 by a majority test.
5. Off-chain payments are invisible and lower the counted cost. Examples: accelerators, private submission, direct miner deals. Capture paid that way is seen only through the D rows and item 2's measurements.
6. Honest TIMEOUT waves in a fee spike can give W-P, and so can an honest hot market of short-lived Hearts. These are real outcomes, but withdrawal follows.
7. The D rows are cheap to force (about 3,200 sat), so an adversary can create disclosure noise and review load. An overdue review pauses nothing.
8. Majority-miner censorship of honest BEATs can push W-P without fees (SSoT:2525).
9. An exploitable replay divergence between the two implementations (TDS §14.4: `UNKNOWN`, signing locked) leads to withdrawal through W-DATA after 2,016 blocks. So does the project's own replay outage (critique A6). *2026-11-04 (Claude plan, §14.19): the divergence is an unreleased MISMATCH or divergence-family refusal, and a comparison gap in the availability family is neither a divergence nor a replay outage; see §14.19 item 3.*
10. A withdrawal already carried out stands, even if a later deeper reorg or a tool correction removes the evidence. This is disclosed and not reversed.
11. Before birth, W-C stops the project's campaign after 15 cap-premium outbids by one strong-link cluster. That is about 450,000 sat, against about 720,024 sat to exhaust the 24 attempts.
12. A single honest service with linked wallets that wins 15 consecutive gates at the cap premium withdraws the namespace. That is real concentration, measured by outcome.
13. The values 30,000, 450,000, 15, 30, 4,033, 10,080, 2,016, V_REF 213 and the 6-confirmation depth would be frozen policy. Changing any of them is a new policy identity with new confirmatory testing (SSoT:2851), even if the Founder raises `max_fee_per_candidate_sat` or A10 measures a different `v`.

A named ITEM3W miner policy (:1049, "any other rule is a new policy name") mines each B_j from an explicit transaction list (no mempool fill) and logs that list as the `filler` field. The driver also runs the unchanged D2-2 tool on each ITEM3W run, with a method policy and truth for that run (today it refuses a method policy outside ITEM1, `METHOD_POLICY_OUTSIDE_ITEM1`). It produces the W-row tool's per-gate inputs from the blocks and the replay state, with every vsize as ⌈weight / 4⌉ and `open_height` from the previous accepted birth: which transactions spend a CURRENT Anchor, which are REGISTER-shaped by `vout[0]`, and which are pkg_g. It logs them with the judge fields. The miner process must be able to:
   - set an exact absolute package fee;
   - place an in-block CPFP child in B_j or B_j + 1;
   - hold a winner back one block;
   - include a same-block BEAT and BEATs at fixed offsets;

   - use distinct NUMS Anchors, with no script path or with one `OP_TRUE` leaf;
   - place mined losing decoys after the winner, and keep unmined decoys in the miner node's mempool but out of every explicit list;
   - leave gates idle.
