1. **The skipped H0−1 path.**
   - A retained Opening at tip ≥ H0 is not broadcast without a non-self `PrestateCompared` match for the real H0−1 block and an `InitialPrestateRecorded` linked to it. The comparison may run late.
   - The block is named by identity, `(H0−1, EffectiveFacts.h0_minus_1_hash)`. The lookup covers the whole journal since the current evidence epoch, not only the latest run.
   - A late entry carries its own `block` (S1). The same-block test compares `block`, not the entry's tip. So two late comparisons of one H0−1 block with different Rust digests raise the TDS 14.8 lock.
   - A reorganization below H0 that changes `h0_minus_1_hash` voids the old `PrestateCompared`. The core asks for a new comparison of the new H0−1 block and raises Reorg67, as today.
   - Cost, accepted without a Founder decision because it only adds a refusal: while the comparison is late, a third party may take the rolling gate (Core :571, :600-606).
   - (S5, the receipt's identity range) The H0−1 receipt checks only the empty-state Profile, the deployment id and the header identity. RM adds a dated note in formats §1 that states this range. RM formats v15 keep refusing `main` (formats :156, :491, :865).

2. **What is compared, and how the links chain.**
   - The objects are the Core canonical state bytes after each block from H0 through the decision tip T. Both sides start from the same raw blocks, read byte-equal on both nodes.
   - The machine decides a match from two receipts with different producer ids. No caller asserts it.
   - The first link is INITIAL from H0−1, and its start digest equals the `PrestateCompared` digest of item 1. Each later link is STATE from the previous link's last state bytes and through hash. Before the host uses a STATE link, it checks that the link's start bytes equal the store's verified state at that height. A link that fails this check is not accepted. (Claude plan, A's settlement of 2026-11-04) If no reorganization at or below that height explains the difference, the store changed under a MATCHed chain, and that also raises the TDS 14.8 lock of item 5 (`STOP_PRESTATE_UNREPRODUCIBLE`, `StateCorruption148`). Heights have no gap or overlap, and every through hash is on the active chain.
   - STATE links need RM's formats v15 comparator (Producers, below). Until v15 the chain is INITIAL-only: every link starts INITIAL at H0−1 and replays to the tip. So the gate serves Regtest and Class T only, and it fails closed once a link would pass 4,096 blocks (formats :230; about 28 days after H0 at the 10-minute mean, ESTIMATE). A BEAT whose INITIAL link cannot reach its tip under that limit gets no cutover (item 10). v15 still refuses `main` (item 1, S5). Before v15 a link at tip T also covers T − H0 + 1 blocks, so at the item 8 catch-up bound it takes (T − H0 + 1) × 120 s: as long as the 10-minute mean from T = H0 + 4, or from T = H0 + 19 at a guessed 30 s per block (ESTIMATE; Python has never been timed). The one-block link of items 7 and 8, item 3's one-link wait and FBO §7.11's routine case therefore hold only from v15. Before v15, on a network with 10-minute blocks, the lag grows with tip − H0, AL-9 and AL-10 fire in routine operation, and a BEAT reaches E or the 4,096-block refusal of item 10. On Regtest the rig sets the block rate.
   - A reorganization below a link's through height voids that link and every later one. The next link starts where the last link that still counts ends, at or below the common ancestor (INITIAL if none counts). This chain is not the TDS 6.7 step-6 comparison: `REPLAY_AND_DIFFERENTIAL_PASS` for Reorg67 (:880) comes from the **step-6 record** `docs/claude-track/formats/REORG_STEP6_RECORD_V1.md` (:1129). Item 6 says what step 6 is today and what would let it reuse this chain. This corrects ruling §3 and its case C14, which started the new link at the common ancestor and said this gate and step 6 use one mechanism. A start at the ancestor would be a STATE start that Python never checked (item 6).
   - Freshness: a Sign at tip T needs a MATCH chain through exactly T, and a Broadcast at tip T′ needs one through exactly T′, even when the bytes were signed, or already broadcast, at an earlier tip. The Rust last digest must equal the basis `replay_state_sha256` at that tip. Signed bytes that a mempool still holds at T′ only wait for mining (`pending`, `crates/bitagi-launch/src/machine.rs:1634-1642`) and need no comparison. A resend inside one tip's run (the hand-off bound, :151-153) uses that tip's chain.
   - Order: the comparison entries come before the run's `ExecutionChecks`. B passes `from = comparison.next` to `signing()` and `broadcast()` (`machine.rs:1205`, `:1663`), so the checks and the human signing and broadcast records come after the comparison. Checks recorded before the comparison are not used; the core asks for them again. For a BEAT this order binds the run's fresh checks only: its authority is one record per subject (§14.14, :1168), so a comparison does not ask for it again (item 3). (Claude plan, A's BEAT-3 answer Q4: this narrows ruling §3, whose reason was that the human approval follows the compared state. For a BEAT, the report that records the authority shows the cutover height and rule instead, item 3.)
   - The comparison follower, the evidence turn that extends this chain outside any candidate run, and `compared_through_height` are defined in item 7.
   - Producers: the host computes the Rust side from its own node reads with the real-block library. An operator runs RM's comparator for the Python side in a pinned container under a leg record. The host records `ReplayCompared` only after it checks the Python result and report against its own manifest, raw blocks and recomputed Rust result, never against the outbox copy (ruling §7; C17). RM's formats v15 comparator accepts a STATE start (`start.kind` STATE) and reports one digest per step in the formats 8.4 shape. Builder code never reads or includes reference code. After every mainnet Sign or Broadcast (none can happen before 044a-3), the Reviewer reruns the Python emit on the compared manifests that the act relied on. This is the step-6 record's post-release audit (step-6 record :956-976): it detects and does not prevent, and it is not a condition of the Sign or Broadcast.

3. **Gate or evidence, by transaction kind.**
   - **REGISTER** (a retained Opening at tip ≥ H0, and Effective): a gate, with no exemption and no cutover. Without a fresh MATCH chain the core asks for `CompareReplay` or waits, and raises nothing. A blocker after H0 stops the broadcast under TDS 14.3; if the comparison never completes, SEM-1 ends incomplete under TDS 14.10.
   - **BEAT (Founder, FD-CT-105 Q1 (가)): a gate that switches at a height.**
     - Below the cutover height E it is the REGISTER gate.
     - From E, when the only gap is in the availability family (item 4), the BEAT proceeds as `CutoverReplay`: Rust-only signing and broadcast, publicly labelled "Rust-only at broadcast" with its `compared_through` frontier. At every new tip the core first asks for `CompareReplay`, and a MATCH that arrives returns the next step to `ComparedReplay` (ruling §2). A MISMATCH stops at once, and BEATs already broadcast are disclosed.
     - E is W2 entry for the first BEAT, `B + 144` (FBO:70), and L2 entry for every later BEAT, `A + 1,152` (FBO:81).
     - Each cutover BEAT keeps its own `SigningAuthorized` and `BroadcastAuthorized` records (FD-CT-01), made on a decision report that shows its basis (ruling §2) and, as a Claude-plan addition (Authority and basis, below; A's BEAT-3 answer Q4), its cutover height and the cutover rule.
   - (Claude plan) How the core finds E. It adds the window offset to the store's CURRENT Anchor height: 144 when `first`, else 1,152 (`HeartFacts.current_anchor` and `HeartFacts.first`, `crates/bitagi-launch/src/facts.rs:541-548`; the offsets of `BEAT_WINDOWS`, `crates/bitagi-launch/src/phase.rs:114`). It uses `checked_add` and reads no clock. With the Core deadline `last_valid_beat_height` = anchor + 2,016 (Core :733-741), E equals `last_valid_beat_height` − 1,872 for the first BEAT and `last_valid_beat_height` − 864 after it. These are the points where FBO's D, the blocks left (FBO:138), is 1,872 and 864 (FBO:190). The ruling's "D" meant `last_valid_beat_height`, and so does the "(D)" of the code comment at facts.rs:547. BEAT-3b's test cells where the stored `last_valid_beat_height` differs from anchor + 2,016 keep the anchor-based E. This E is the cutover height, not §14.18's W-P sum E (:1386). This corrects ruling §2 and §4, which derived E from the stored `last_valid_beat_height` and `first` with `checked_sub`: the Founder's option names the anchor heights `B + 144` and `A + 1,152`, and the two methods differ only where the stored `last_valid_beat_height` is not anchor + 2,016. Ruling §9's E mutants become: E − 1 and E + 1 for both offsets, the 144 and 1,152 offsets swapped, `checked_add` replaced by `saturating_add` or `wrapping_add`, and the window check of the next bullet removed. Case C19 becomes: wait at `B + 143` and cut over at `B + 144`; wait at `A + 1,151` and cut over at `A + 1,152`; a short stored deadline (`last_valid_beat_height` = `B + 50`) never cuts over; a long one keeps the anchor-based E.
   - (Claude plan; A's answer to B's BEAT-3 draft, #258 issuecomment-6629923155, Q1) The cutover is allowed only while `beat_window(first, anchor_height, last_valid_beat_height, tip)` (`phase.rs:134-161`) is `W2Escalation`, `W3Late`, `L2Escalation` or `L3Late`, never in `W0Wait`, `W1Normal`, `L0Wait`, `L1Normal` or `Timeout`, or below the anchor. That one table (`BEAT_WINDOWS`, with `checked_add`) is the only source: the launch core and host write no new 144, 1,152, 1,872 or 864 literal (M5). If the stored `last_valid_beat_height` is short, so that the anchor plus the entry offset is above it, there is no cutover: the BEAT is gated as below E through `last_valid_beat_height`, then `HeartTimedOut`. A `last_valid_beat_height` − 1,872 form would put E inside W1 or below the anchor for such a deadline and fail open, so it is not used. The ruling's "the margin left is always 864 blocks or more" (ruling §2) holds only when `last_valid_beat_height` = anchor + 2,016, because `beat_facts` accepts any `last_valid_beat_height` above the anchor (`machine.rs:1507`).
   - (Claude plan) Broadcasting a signed BEAT again at a new tip is a Broadcast at that tip. `broadcast()` runs the execution checks and records a new observation of its basis at each tip (`machine.rs:1663-1890`), and MBA:415-417 already asks for fresh checks before any rebroadcast. So this is a third limit on "a signed BEAT may always be broadcast again" (:1166-1167): below E the bytes go again only on a MATCH chain through the new tip, and from E an availability-only gap lets them go as `CutoverReplay`. Bytes a mempool still holds only wait for mining (WB3) and need no comparison. W2, W3, L2, L3 and FBO's AL-5 (FBO:206) all lie at or past E, so the limit bites only below E (W1 for the first BEAT; L0 and L1 for later BEATs), where the wait is one link while the follower is current (items 7 and 8; from RM v15, item 2). BEAT-3b places the gate at the `signing()` call in `beat()` and at its `broadcast()` call after `pending` (`machine.rs:1469-1478`). Its cells cover a BEAT evicted and broadcast again two tips later, below E and at E, and one that a mempool still holds.
   - (Claude plan; the same answer, Q4) Authority and basis. A BEAT's authority stays one record per subject (§14.14, :1168) and is not renewed per tip. A switch between `ComparedReplay` and `CutoverReplay` takes no new `SigningAuthorized` or `BroadcastAuthorized`: the decision report at each tip shows that tip's basis and `compared_through`, the BEAT observation records it, and the public label goes with the broadcast. The cutover height and rule on that report (the addition marked above) let one recorded human decision know and cover a later Rust-only broadcast. In CT V2, `ComparedReplay` uses `RUST_PYTHON_EQUAL_BEFORE_BROADCAST` (`crates/bitagi-launch/src/ct_v2.rs:84`) at tip ≥ H0 too, and `CutoverReplay` keeps `SYNCHRONIZED_REPLAY_REFERENCE_ONLY` (`ct_v2.rs:92`) with the label and `compared_through` (ruling §6; B's share of FD-CT-95/96).
   - (Claude plan; the same answer, Q2) Until BEAT-3b. BEAT-3a removes the host's BEAT hold (`NotYet::BeatAct`, `crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/act.rs:808-811`), one of #692's LATENT interlocks (ruling §14). In its place it refuses every BEAT Sign, hand-off, charge and Broadcast with `Basis::SynchronizedReplay` at tip ≥ H0, before any read or write, when the descriptor's network is not Regtest. BEAT-3b turns that refusal into the gate of this item. So between the two units a BEAT at tip ≥ H0 is signed only on Regtest, with no comparison, and such a run is evidence for neither the gate nor the alarms (item 7). A updates the #692 record when BEAT-3a merges, and the FD-CT-62 order line and the QA-U3 (044a-3) mainnet release checklist name BEAT-3b as a prerequisite with B-692b (R19-20).
   - A mismatch or a divergence-family refusal never cuts over, at any height and for any kind.
   - No birth claim or BEAT-acceptance claim is made for a height h, and no Class E report is published with a cutoff at h, without a MATCH through h. The heights this binds are TDS 6.8's critical heights after H0 (TDS:866-880), which include every public report cutoff height (TDS:878). Node A/B agreement does not count as implementation agreement.
   - (Claude plan; this narrows ruling §6, whose "Class E report row" would also cover §14.18's W rows, drafted the same day) §14.18's W rows follow their own rule, not this bullet:
     - H_e (:1393) counts the Rust replay. In this codebase `SYNCED` is the store's standing against the agreed chain (`crates/bitagi-cli/src/store_commands.rs:263-279`, `:318-321` at `c5195fd1`). So a comparison gap in the availability family never lowers H_e and never gives W-DATA. Reason (Claude reading): under Q1 (가) the Founder accepted Rust-only BEATs on such a gap, which keeps the gap from ending the Heart (ruling §11, where the option with no cutover could end it). A withdrawal for the same gap is a consequence nobody has accepted; §14.18 counts only the project's own replay outage (:1505, :1610).
     - A MISMATCH or divergence-family refusal that is not yet released is an implementation disagreement (TDS:1655). H_e stays below the first height it names. If H_e then stays more than 2,016 blocks below the 6-confirmation tip before the release, W-DATA withdraws the namespace (:1395). With H_e at most one below that height, this happens once the 6-confirmation tip reaches that height + 2,016. This is §14.18 residual 9 (:1610).
     - A W report or `W_PENDING_DATA` status above `compared_through` is published with the Rust-only label and that frontier, as a cutover BEAT is. The Class E monitor (R18-11) applies these rules.

4. **Refusal families.**
   - Divergence: Python could not apply a block or start state that the manifest gave (`BLOCK_<h>:<reason>`, and `START:<reason>` for a STATE start the host store verified). It is handled as a mismatch.
   - Availability: everything else (no output, container or resource limits other than the manifest's block and result-size bounds, `INTERNAL_ERROR`, `SOURCE_IDENTITY`, I/O, an unknown code). It raises nothing. A REGISTER waits; a BEAT waits until E. Repeats go to RM as a defect lead. A network where the comparison is not admitted is not an availability gap (item 10), and neither is a link that the 4,096-block limit refuses before RM v15 (item 10). A `RESULT_SIZE_LIMIT` refusal of an INITIAL-only link (the 64 MiB result bound, formats :234) is treated as that 4,096-block case. These exclusions are Claude plan (A's settlement of 2026-11-04 and item 10). They narrow ruling §5 L3, where availability is everything else, and they only refuse. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, answer to B's BEAT-3b-1b draft, #258 issuecomment-6733960591, Q-2): "raises nothing" means no stop and no ledger record. On a network where the comparison is admitted, the host journals one `ReplayComparisonUnavailable { through }` record (host journal tag 22, FORMAT 12) for an availability-family refusal of a link that ends at the current tip, written after that tip's latest `CompareReplay` request and carrying the refusal code. It is the only evidence on which the core cuts a BEAT over (item 3), only for that tip, and never for a divergence, a MISMATCH, the 4,096-block limit, an INITIAL-only `RESULT_SIZE_LIMIT` or a network where the comparison is not admitted. It is not a MATCH: it does not move `compared_through_height` or the lag (item 7). Repeats are counted from these records for RM.*
   - RM publishes the split in formats v15.

5. **Locks and their release (every kind, every height).**
   - A Rust/Python comparison recorded at a run tip at or above H0 that ends in MISMATCH (a replay link of item 2, or a late H0−1 comparison of item 1), or a divergence-family refusal, is journal-wide evidence. It raises `STOP_DEFECT_DETECTED`, which holds every row (045a:652, :661). After birth the public status is UNKNOWN under TDS 14.4; before birth TDS 14.3 applies. No majority, no choosing a side, no Founder override.
   - A Rust last digest that differs from the stored `replay_state_sha256`, one producer giving two digests for one block, or a STATE link whose start bytes differ from the store's verified state at that height with no reorganization at or below that height to explain it (item 2), is the TDS 14.8 case (`STOP_PRESTATE_UNREPRODUCIBLE`, `StateCorruption148`). These are the existing codes. B checks whether the wallet's `STOP_CODES` needs a new one; if it does, that is B's ledger format change.
   - The mismatch stop releases under 045a:675 plus two conditions: (i) a qualified non-author Reviewer record naming the first differing height and field and the Exact D3 Core clause that shows which side departed; (ii) a new MATCH chain from INITIAL through the tip at release, recorded after the fix. Links recorded before the stop's acknowledgment epoch (`evidence_epoch`, `machine.rs:838-843`) do not count. (i) locates the defect; it does not choose a side to sign on. If no Core clause shows which side departed, (i) cannot be written and the stop remains (TDS 14.4: a Core defect is addressed only by a successor protocol, TDS:1658).
   - **(Founder, FD-CT-105 Q2) The new chain may be a segmented parallel re-replay (S3).** Segment k runs from stored STATE bytes with the fixed code on both sides. The chain counts only when the first segment starts at INITIAL and every segment's end bytes equal the next segment's start bytes. From the first point where they differ, the rest is run again from the fixed output. This meets TDS 14.4's "replay starts again from raw blocks and the known initial state" (TDS:1656), because replay is deterministic. Reason (an ESTIMATE: Python's time per block has never been measured and is guessed at 10-60 s): a sequential replay at about 30 s per block over about 50,000 blocks after H0 takes about 17 days, longer than the 2,016-block window of about 14 days.
   - If the (i) record shows that the Rust side departed, every project REGISTER and BEAT signed at a decision tip at or above the first differing height was signed on the defective replay. The incident record names each of them in `affected_txids` (Claude plan: one record in place of ruling U1's one record per transaction, because version 2 cannot add to the list, :888), and every public claim (section 14.15, H7) about a block or transaction at or above that height is reclassified. (Claude plan, A's BEAT-3 answer Q4) After that release, a BEAT authority recorded on a decision report at or above the first differing height does not survive, and a new authority is needed; this limits :1168's "survives a lock release".
   - The TDS 14.8 case releases under 045a:676: the TDS 14.8 procedure and the 045a:675 conditions. The procedure's "full replay comparison" (TDS:1703) is the new MATCH chain of (ii). (Claude plan, ruling U2: Q2 named only TDS 14.4, so a segmented chain here is A's reading of TDS 14.8, not part of the Founder's answer.) So the existing wallet release `Tds148RestoreAndReplayPassed` names that chain in its `replay_comparison_sha256` (`crates/bitagi-wallet/src/campaign_stop.rs:497-504`).
   - (S4) An injected or relabelled Python result also raises `STOP_DEFECT_DETECTED`, and with no code to fix, the 045a:675 release could never be met. So a Python result that did not come from the pinned container is a defect class of its own. The Reviewer shows it by rerunning the pinned container on the recorded manifest. The fix is a merged hand-off check that would have refused that result (for example the leg-record binding) with its regression test, the container redeployed, and a new MATCH chain from INITIAL as in (ii). Its release is still the `DEFECT_FIXED_AND_VERIFIED` release of 045a:675 with (i) and (ii), and it lowers no 045a condition. If no such fix can be made, the stop remains.
   - (Claude plan; A's BEAT-3 answer, Q5) B-692b ships the mismatch raise only together with this stricter release: it emits no stop that 045a:675 alone would release. Cases C24 and C25 go to B-692b, which amends the owners in ruling §8 and §9 (A adds a dated line on #692). C24 adds the core checks (the new chain from INITIAL, the acknowledgment epoch) to the wallet's `DefectRepairValidated` evidence (`crates/bitagi-wallet/src/campaign_stop.rs:234-242`; its release check `may_clear`, `:308-323`). C25 runs in B-692b as synthetic two-receipt cells; a real segmented re-replay waits for RM v15's STATE start. BEAT-3b runs both again as BEAT rows, or has a structure test that `beat()` reads the same chain predicate as `effective()`. *2026-11-07 (A's B-692b P3 answer, #258 issuecomment-6708051303): the stricter release is a new release evidence (tag 7) beside `DefectRepairValidated`, whose tag-2 bytes stay as they are (R10). It is stored as a `STOP_DEFECT_DETECTED` trigger branch, and acknowledgment and release read that branch, not only the code, so a later raise of the same code from another trigger neither acknowledges a mismatch at a tip at or above H0 nor lets 045a:675 alone end its hold. A late H0−1 comparison at a tip at or above H0 takes this branch; at tip H0−1 it keeps `PRESTATE_MISMATCH_FD_CT_03A` (rows :883-884). A divergence-family refusal is recorded and takes it too. "After the last release" reads `evidence_epoch` at or above that release's event.*
   - (Claude plan, from A's integration check of #702 B-692a) The TDS 14.8 test "one producer gives two digests for one block" counts only entries recorded since the last acknowledged stop was released. A fixed producer's new digest is then not new 14.8 evidence, and the older entries stay in the journal as evidence. Without this, a mismatch of a retained opening's H0−1 block could never be released for that opening (U1 (ii) unreachable). If the (i) record shows that Rust departed, a retained REGISTER or BEAT signed at a decision tip at or above the first differing height is not broadcast after the release; a new candidate is needed. If Python departed, the same candidate proceeds after the release. B-692b builds this; until then B-692a fails closed.
   - **Incident record** (Claude plan; amends section 8.2 at :863, :869 and the release table :878-884):
     - `trigger` gains `RUST_PYTHON_MISMATCH_TIP_GE_H0`: a failure named in the first two bullets of this item, recorded at a run tip at or above H0. `PRESTATE_MISMATCH_FD_CT_03A` keeps the H0−1 comparison at tip H0−1, and rows :883-884 are unchanged for it.
     - `digest_absent_reason` gains `REFERENCE_DIVERGENCE_REFUSAL`: a divergence-family refusal leaves no Python digest.
     - Version 1's `affected_txids` lists every project REGISTER and BEAT whose decision tip or inclusion height is at or above the start of the failed comparison. Version 2 repeats it unchanged (:888), so the list already holds every transaction the (i) record can later name.
     - With `STOP_DEFECT_DETECTED` the release members are `DEFECT_FIXED_AND_VERIFIED`, `FIRST_DIFFERENCE_LOCATED` (the (i) record), `FULL_REPLAY_MATCH_FROM_INITIAL` (the (ii) chain; B-692b fixes its digest) and `PUBLIC_CLAIMS_RECLASSIFIED`. With `STOP_PRESTATE_UNREPRODUCIBLE` they are `DEFECT_FIXED_AND_VERIFIED` and `TDS_14_8_PROCEDURE`, as at :884; that procedure's full replay comparison is the (ii) chain.
     - `PUBLIC_CLAIMS_RECLASSIFIED` is a claims list when Rust departed. Otherwise it is a `NOT_APPLICABLE` object with its reason, as for `REORG_TDS_6_7` (:1129). The reasons and a claims-list status for this trigger are open (R19-9). Until they are fixed, this trigger's version 2 is refused. *2026-11-08 (A, R19-9 decided; A's #740 MERGE-GO issuecomment-6716958133): For this trigger the claims list is schema `bitagi.claude_track.mismatch_claims.v1`, canonical JSON under H7's rules (:1225). It is an object with `network`, `first_difference_height`, `full_replay_match_sha256` (the (ii) chain) and `claims`, a non-empty array sorted and without duplicates as in H7. Each entry is `{source, locator, kind, id, height, new_status, corrected_sha256}`: `source`, `locator`, `kind` and `id` are as in H7; `height` is at or above `first_difference_height`; `new_status` is `UPHELD` (the corrected replay gives the same answer), `WITHDRAWN` (it does not, and nothing replaces the claim) or `CORRECTED` (it does not, and the dated note states the corrected claim); `corrected_sha256` is the SHA-256 of that note's corrected statement and is present only for `CORRECTED`. A claim that only says a transaction is in a block (`TX_INCLUDED`) is listed and is `UPHELD`, because replay does not decide inclusion. Each claim is reclassified in its own source as H7 says. The `NOT_APPLICABLE` reasons are `PYTHON_DEPARTED` (the (i) record shows the Python side departed, so no public claim rests on the defective side) and `NO_PUBLIC_CLAIM_AT_OR_ABOVE_FIRST_DIFFERENCE` (Rust departed and none of the seven H7 sources, all named in `claim_sources_checked`, holds a claim at or above that height); in the Class T Regtest experiments the second applies, for H7's Regtest reason. Acceptance of the list's shape by code is a B unit under FD-CT-62 before the first Class E REGISTER broadcast, as for H7; until it merges, a release of this trigger that carries a claims list is checked by hand, and the release command waits for it (A 6708051303 Q-p3-11). So version 2 of this trigger is no longer refused for want of R19-9.* *Dated line 2026-11-08 (A #258 issuecomment-6727751167): the `NOT_APPLICABLE` object carries `status`, `reason`, `claim_sources_checked`, `claims_record_sha256`, `first_difference_height`, `network` and `full_replay_match_sha256`, so it binds the same release facts as the list; `claim_sources_checked` is all seven H7 sources for `NO_PUBLIC_CLAIM_AT_OR_ABOVE_FIRST_DIFFERENCE` and `[]` for `PYTHON_DEPARTED`; a claim of kind `TX_INCLUDED` takes only `UPHELD`; a wallet form byte it does not know is refused. Code checks only that the seven sources are named; that no claim at or above the height exists is caller-asserted in Class T, `claims_record_sha256` is null in a `NOT_APPLICABLE` object (`REORG_STEP6_RECORD_V1.md:681`), and before Class E a person checks the seven sources named in `claim_sources_checked` for the release facts the object binds (A #258 answer to B issuecomment-6729929318, Q-cf2-1, 2026-11-09).* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, Q-cf2-2): the list object also carries the key `schema` with the value `bitagi.claude_track.mismatch_claims.v1`, beside `network`, `first_difference_height`, `full_replay_match_sha256` and `claims`; a list without it is refused.* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, #751 check issuecomment-6733909377, O-2): in a list entry `corrected_sha256` is absent, not null, unless `new_status` is CORRECTED; an entry with `"corrected_sha256": null` is refused. (H7 list entries carry `new_height` and `replacement_txid` always, null when they do not apply; the two lists differ on purpose.)*
   - A mismatch lock still open at `last_valid_beat_height` ends the Heart at `first_timeout_height` = `last_valid_beat_height` + 1 (Core :733-741), unless a BEAT broadcast before the lock is mined by then. The end is recorded under TDS 14.4 and 14.10. The only recorded human decisions are the repair priority and accepting that TIMEOUT; nothing is signed under the lock. (This qualifies ruling §4, which put the TIMEOUT at `last_valid_beat_height` + 1 without the case of a BEAT broadcast before the lock.)

6. **(S2) Lock exposure that remains.**
   - `REORG_TDS_6_7` and `CRITICAL_HEIGHT_DISAGREEMENT_TDS_6_8` (:880-881) release only with `REPLAY_AND_DIFFERENTIAL_PASS`. `IMPORT_RESTORE_FAILURE_TDS_7_5` (:882) needs it in set (a), a replay from H0−1 to the tip; its set (b) needs no Python run. The BEAT cutover bypasses none of them, and their release conditions are unchanged.
   - **Step 6 today.** 045b names a producer of `REPLAY_AND_DIFFERENTIAL_PASS` only for Reorg67: the step-6 record (:1129). It replays the replacement branch as one INITIAL manifest from the run's H0−1 hash through the export tip T_e, with no STATE start (step-6 record :52-61). RM's `step6-compare` refuses a STATE start (formats :913). A manifest holds at most 4,096 blocks, so T_e ≤ H0 + 4,095, less the disconnected blocks and the bracket heights outside H0..T_e (step-6 record :1055-1057). Above that the release is refused and the lock stays open (step-6 record :1028-1037, :1100-1103). It runs on Regtest, and on Signet only under P5 item A9 (a); never on `main` (step-6 record :175-176). For :881 and for :882 set (a), 045b names no producer yet.
   - So today this section's chain does not shorten step 6. A segmented parallel replay cannot serve step 6 either, because every segment after the first is a STATE start. FD-CT-105 Q2 covers the mismatch release of item 5, not step 6. This also corrects ruling §4 (S2): the one-link differential pass holds only after the later step-6 version below.
   - **Its size today (Class T only).** A step-6 release at T_e is one Python run over T_e − H0 + 1 blocks, plus a second INITIAL run over old tip − H0 + 1 blocks when a block was disconnected and both nodes still serve it (step-6 record :79-81), even when the follower is current. At the item 8 catch-up bound (5 blocks per 600 s), 4,096 blocks take up to about 819 block intervals (about 5.7 days), and the two runs one after the other up to about 1,638 (about 11.4 days) (ESTIMATE on the UNMEASURED item 8 bounds). Above H0 + 4,095 there is no release at all. This cannot reach a Class E Heart. Mainnet stays refused, and #446 (no Reorg67 release outside Regtest) is a fix before Class E, not a residual (Founder, FD-CT-92 row 10; B45:75; :963, :972). The step-6 record makes the chained STATE start a prerequisite of that fix (step-6 record :1038-1041).
   - **The one-link pass (Claude plan, for a later step-6 record version).** Step 6 reuses this section's chain: the links that still count after the reorganization (item 2), plus one new link from the end of the last of them through T_e (INITIAL if none counts). It needs:
     - RM: the formats v15 STATE start of item 2, accepted by `step6-compare` too;
     - B: a host check of the new link's start bytes against both the store's restored state at that height (step-6 record :473-483) and the last state bytes of the counted link before it, which Python computed before the reorganization. The store check alone is not enough. It compares Rust's restored state only with Rust's own rows, so Python never checks the restored ancestor. That is why the current version uses INITIAL (step-6 record :57-61). A failed check is the TDS 14.8 case of item 5;
     - A: the new record version, with its block bounds counted from the new link's start instead of from H0 (step-6 record :1055-1057), its disconnected side, a new :1129 mapping, and a non-author Reviewer check that it does not narrow TDS 6.7 steps 4-6, as for the current version (step-6 record :90-96);
     - set (a) of :882 stays a replay from H0−1.
   - **Its size then** (the S2 residual that FD-CT-105 Q1 accepted, item 9; Claude-plan estimates on the unmeasured item 8 bounds). With Python up, the new link covers the reorganization depth or the follower's lag, whichever is larger, plus the new blocks. A 6-deep reorganization with a current follower needs about 7 blocks, about 840 s or 1.4 block intervals, before TDS 6.7 steps 7 and 8, if the disconnected blocks rest on the follower's links from before the reorganization; if the later version compares the disconnected side again, about 13 blocks, about 1,560 s or 2.6 block intervals. With Python down, the lock holds every BEAT until Python is back. A lock that opens within (the outage + the link + the other release steps + the 46-block BEAT path, FBO:206) of `last_valid_beat_height` can end the Heart in TIMEOUT. A reorganization while Python is down is two faults at once, outside the FBO 7.5 envelope (FBO:227).
   - A later version that replays the whole branch again instead of reusing the chain would take up to the item 8 full re-replay bound (1,000 blocks at T_max), even with Python up. The Founder did not accept that size, so it goes back to A and the Founder before Class E.

7. **Follower and alarms** (Claude plan; FBO §7.11, alarms AL-9 and AL-10).
   - The comparison follower (not the store follower of the wait registry's W18) is required (ruling §3). It is an evidence turn outside any candidate run, and it extends the chain from H0, with or without a candidate. It starts a link at every new agreed block and never waits to fill a batch; after a gap, a catch-up link covers at most 6 blocks. This reads the ruling's "or in batches of at most 6" as a catch-up bound, because a follower that waited for 6 blocks would raise the 6-block alarm on every batch. From RM v15 the gate's own work is therefore usually a one-block link, and a catch-up link adds at most 6 blocks. Before v15 every link is INITIAL from H0−1 over tip − H0 + 1 blocks (item 2). Without the follower, every BEAT would reach E.
   - Every decision report carries `compared_through_height`: the through height of the last MATCH link that still counts (item 2), or H0 − 1 before the first link. The lag is the agreed tip minus that height. B-692b emits it (R19-4). Until BEAT-3b the lag is not counted: BEAT-3a reports no frontier and B-692b reports it as not yet counted, never as "lag 0" or "compared".
   - AL-9 (`COMPARISON_LAG`): a lag of 6 or more. AL-10: a lag of 36 or more, or tip ≥ E with a BEAT candidate that is not included and has no MATCH chain through the tip. An alarm holds nothing and is not a stop.
   - BEAT-3b records the frontier in the BEAT observation and counts the 6 and 36 levels (ruling §9 case C23; A's BEAT-3 answer, Q8), with the HC-01 cells of FBO §7.11 and an "alarm as a stop" mutant. Between B-692b and BEAT-3b a dated LATENT row says that no comparison alarm exists yet, and a Class T run on BEAT-3a is not evidence of alarm behaviour. AL-10 is a condition alarm, like AL-3: it alerts when it starts, not at every block. The monitor unit CT-P5-004-2c shows and delivers both alarms, and HC-01 tests them at zero and ±1 around each bound (FBO §7.11).

8. **Time basis** (Class T, the two-node Regtest rig, mainnet-sized blocks, STATE links of RM v15; pre-registered here; UNMEASURED until R19-17's runs):
   - one-block link p95 ≤ 120 s;
   - catch-up of at least 5 blocks per 600 s;
   - a signed REGISTER broadcast at tip h ≥ H0, with `PrestateCompared` recorded and the chain a MATCH through h − 1: `T_tip` = seconds from block h's first arrival at either node to the broadcast call returning on both nodes, over the steps of :599-602 with the one-block link through h in place of the prestate step. p95 of `T_tip` ≤ 540 s, that is the 420 s of :610 plus the 120 s link bound above, by the nearest-rank estimator of section 4.6 as at :610-611. This is a separate Class T bound. :610 and its failure rule (:614-617) are unchanged and cover only `T`, the H0−1 path that A-1 is about (B2:92-100; a Claude reading of FD-CT-03a's "pipeline after H0−1"). At tip ≥ H0 the exact height is already missed (:159-162). So a p95 from 420 s to 540 s passes, and a p95 above 540 s fails this bound and goes to A and the Founder (last bullet); neither re-raises A-1. ESTIMATE (#692 ruling §10): an attempt ends before the next block with probability e^(−P/600), about 41% at P = 540 s; a failed attempt costs time and, for a REGISTER, a gate opportunity (Core :571, :600-606);
   - a full re-replay INITIAL → T_max = 1,050,000, in parallel segments, within 1,000 blocks (600,000 s).
   - These bounds assume STATE links and are measured after R19-2 (RM v15). Before v15 only a link through H0 is one block, and an INITIAL-only link's time grows with tip − H0 (item 2).
   - Any failed bound goes back to A and the Founder. Nothing is relaxed silently.

9. **Residuals.** Every time, duration and bound below is UNMEASURED or an ESTIMATE, as marked; the Core heights and the formats block limit are exact.
   - **(Founder, FD-CT-105 Q1) Accepted by the Founder with FD-CT-105 Q1 (as shown then)** (the answer of 2026-11-04 10:00:15Z; FD-CT-105, BATCH53):
     - The uncompared tail (`compared_through` + 1 … T) of a cutover BEAT is Rust-only. The worst case is a BEAT with no effect, a wasted fee, or a node refusal.
     - The S2 exposure of item 6: a reorganization or critical-height lock open near the deadline while Python is down, shown with a differential pass of one link from the common ancestor.
     - A real mismatch near the deadline can end in TIMEOUT if its lock is still open at `last_valid_beat_height` (`A + 2,016`, FBO:193; TIMEOUT at `A + 2,017`, FBO:83; `A = B` for the first BEAT, FBO:141). This is by design: nothing is signed under the lock (item 5).
   - **Precision given to the Founder on 2026-11-04 after A's check (#258 issuecomment-6630675966; the Founder confirmed the choice after it, 2026-11-04 12:39Z, A's receipt #258 issuecomment-6630833869)** (Claude plan arithmetic on the item 8 bounds and FBO 7.3-7.5; R19-1 records it with FD-CT-105). Each point below was in that notice. Added here are the 7- and 13-block sizes of the later step-6 version, the 320 of 864 blocks, the injected-result example and the "can" case of the TIMEOUT bullet:
     - S2 size today: step 6 is the step-6 record's version 1 (item 6), one Python run from H0−1 over T_e − H0 + 1 blocks even with a current follower, and a second run over H0..old tip when a block was disconnected and both nodes still serve it (step-6 record :79-81). At the item 8 catch-up bound, 4,096 blocks take up to about 819 block intervals per run, about 5.7 days, or about 1,638, about 11.4 days, for two runs in sequence (ESTIMATE on UNMEASURED bounds), and above H0 + 4,095 there is no release. That size is not a Q1 residual: it is part of #446, a fix before Class E (Founder, FD-CT-92 row 10).
     - S2 size after the later step-6 version that reuses this section's chain (item 6): a 6-deep reorganization with a current follower needs about 7 blocks, about 840 s or 1.4 block intervals, if the disconnected blocks rest on the follower's links from before the reorganization, or about 13 blocks, about 1,560 s or 2.6 block intervals, if that version compares the disconnected side again (ESTIMATE). This is the one-link size the Founder was shown, now with a figure. A (record), RM (v15) and B (host) build that version as part of #446's fix (R19-12). A later design that replays the whole branch again would take up to the 1,000-block re-replay bound, and it goes back to the Founder before Class E.
     - TIMEOUT horizon: a release needs the fix and the Reviewer steps of item 5 (no registered bound), then the new chain from INITIAL (up to 1,000 blocks, item 8), then a BEAT (at least 46 blocks, AL-5, FBO:206; or 784 blocks in the `B_total` envelope, FBO:166). So even with an instant fix and review, the bounds do not assure the Heart through a mismatch found after about `A + 970` (2,016 − 1,000 − 46); the fix and review time comes on top. For a later BEAT that point is before L1 opens at `A + 1,008` (FBO:80), so it covers the whole window in which that BEAT is due. For the first BEAT (`A = B`, FBO:141) it is `B + 970`, inside W2 (FBO:70). Either way it is the last 1,046 blocks, about 7.3 days, of each interval. In the `B_total` envelope the point is `A + 232` (2,016 − 1,000 − 784), the last 1,784 blocks, about 12.4 days. All UNMEASURED: they rest on the item 8 bounds, AL-5 and `B_total`. ("Can": a BEAT broadcast before the lock may still be mined in time.) Q2 「예」 is what leaves this horizon: a sequential re-replay (about 17 days at about 50,000 blocks after H0, item 5) would leave no window late in the campaign.
     - A mismatch whose cause cannot be fixed never releases, so it ends in TIMEOUT from any height (TDS:1658-1659). Examples are a Core-text defect, where no Core clause shows which side departed, and an injected Python result with no hand-off fix (item 5). All three Q1 options lock a mismatch, so this does not come from choosing (가).
     - Networks: a network where the comparison is not admitted is not an availability gap. There a BEAT is refused, not cut over, so a Heart on Class T Signet before its comparison arm can TIMEOUT (item 10). Before RM v15, a Class T BEAT whose INITIAL-only chain cannot reach its tip because of the 4,096-block limit is treated the same way. Where the comparison is admitted, a missing Python result alone still never ends the Heart. If QA-U3 ever lifted the host's mainnet refusal without admitting the comparison there, mainnet would fail toward TIMEOUT, not toward Rust-only signing: the safe direction, which QA-U3's Reviewer check covers.
     - Class T before RM v15: every link replays from H0−1 (item 2), so the one-block link and a current follower hold only from v15. On a chain with 10-minute blocks the follower falls behind as tip − H0 grows, AL-9 fires in routine operation, and a BEAT waits until E. Past H0 + 4,095 (about 28 days after H0 at the 10-minute mean, ESTIMATE) a Class T BEAT can be neither compared nor cut over, so a Class T Heart that must BEAT after that height ends in TIMEOUT at `A + 2,017` unless v15 lands first or a BEAT broadcast before then is mined. A long Class T run can therefore TIMEOUT (R19-21). Class E assumes v15.
     - For a later BEAT, E (`A + 1,152`) comes 63 blocks after the draft READY bound (AL-2 at `A + 1,089`, FBO:203). A Python outage across L1 therefore shows no READY and raises AL-2 before the cutover. The path still fits: at most 464 of the 784 blocks of `B_total`, and 320 of the 864 blocks left at E (FBO §7.11; UNMEASURED).
     - The Reference operator role is the Founder's, on the Claude reading of FD-CT-53 at B22:70. It adds one dated confirmation in the Signer's form before the REGISTER broadcast; without it the REGISTER broadcast is not authorized (`ACTION_BLOCK`). It only adds a refusal, and it is approved with the FBO bytes at A3 (FBO:130; FBO §7.11; R19-8).
   - **For the Class E notice (Claude plan ask, with R18-2):** A asks the Founder to accept these together with §14.18's residuals (R18-2), as section 11 Q2 did for the earlier residual lists (:972; B45:75). The ruling names no acceptance step, and CRD:51 covers only ARDR (R19-18).
     - Blocks after the decision tip that hold a REGISTER or BEAT are compared by the follower only after the broadcast. TDS 0.1 item 11 asks for agreement at every launch-critical height, so before broadcast this tail is not covered; the follower's result is appended as executed evidence (MBA:329-332).
     - The comparison checks replay state, not the candidate's own effect. The S03/S04 corpus, the offline signer's semantic checks and the TDS 6.8 node checks cover that.
     - A relabelled Python result cannot be detected from the files alone (`crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/prestate.rs:37-42`). Independence rests on session separation, leg records and Reviewer reruns.
     - A misreading common to both implementations is not caught.
     - Until RM v15 the chain is INITIAL-only and fails closed at 4,096 blocks (formats :230), and the comparison is admitted only on the Class T networks of item 10.
     - §14.18's W rows above `compared_through` rest on the Rust replay alone and carry the Rust-only label (item 3, which narrows ruling §6 for them). A comparison outage alone never gives W-DATA.
     - The gate governs only the project's own broadcast, its record and its public label. Bytes already broadcast can be relayed by any peer, so the gate cannot stop them from being mined at a tip that has no MATCH chain.

10. **Class T campaigns and networks** (Claude plan; the #692 ruling names no network but `main`).
    - The gate is never switched off for a campaign or a network.
    - The comparison is admitted on a network only where the host's real-block check passes (`check_real_block_network`, `crates/bitagi-h0-receipt/src/real_block.rs:61-71` at `c5195fd1`). Today that is `regtest` only. `signet` passes only after P5 item A9 (a) and the H0−1 Signet unit, as for step 6 (step-6 record :1193-1213), and B's check accepts it no earlier than RM's comparator does. `main` stays refused (`NETWORK_NOT_CLASS_T`, exit 6) and is admitted only with the other mainnet refusals in 044a-3 (QA-U3). Any other value is refused.
    - Where the comparison is not admitted, the gate fails closed. An availability refusal leaves no record (item 4), so the core cannot tell such a network from a Python outage. The host therefore refuses every `CutoverReplay` Sign and Broadcast there, and a REGISTER there waits as in item 3. BEAT-3b, which turns BEAT-3a's interim refusal for the host BEAT act (`crates/bitagi-launch-host/src/act.rs:808-811`; item 3) into the gate, adds this refusal and tests it on a `PrivateSignet` descriptor. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, #258 issuecomment-6733960591, Q-2): "leaves no record" now reads: leaves no stop and no ledger record, and no `ReplayComparisonUnavailable` journal record either, because the host writes that record only where the comparison is admitted (item 4 dated line). So the core cannot cut over here, and the host refusal of every `CutoverReplay` Sign and Broadcast stays as a second guard.*
    - (Claude plan, A's settlement of 2026-11-04) Before RM v15 the chain is INITIAL-only (item 2). A Class T BEAT whose INITIAL-only chain cannot reach its tip because of the 4,096-block limit (formats :230) is treated like a network where the comparison is not admitted: no cutover, fail closed. The same host refusal covers it, and BEAT-3b tests it at the limit and one block past it.
    - XD-1 runs (section 5.1) take the H0−1 path only. They never reach the gate.
    - ARDR items 2 and 4, L0 seat (:536, :1023, :1054). The H0−1 comparison runs as today (`ARDR_RUN_V1.md:1821-1826`; this bullet cites ARDR_RUN_V1 at main `3031f432`). After block G every L0 verdict comes from a check that runs before the gate: the lock, the outcome, the offline release and the stale gate (`machine.rs:171`, `:1369-1392` at `c5195fd1`). So no comparison runs at tip ≥ H0. The expected labels (`ARDR_RUN_V1.md:1881-1884`, `:2296-2300`; :1068) and the slice bound (:1252) do not change. A changed label fails the run (`L0_LABEL_MISMATCH`, `ITEM4_LABEL_MISMATCH`), so these runs also check where the gate sits. In ARDR item 2, a mismatch on an excluded gate is only logged (`ARDR_RUN_V1.md:1875-1877`).
    - Section 5.3 retry (:705-720, two-node Regtest). It reaches the gate, so once the code under test has B-692b it runs with RM's comparator. Step 2 signs only on a MATCH chain through its signing tip, and step 3 broadcasts only on one through its broadcast tip (#692 ruling §9, C8 and C9). "Must be signable" (:707-709; B1:58) is read as (a Claude reading of FD-CT-03's retry allowance, not a Founder decision): signable once the chain is fresh. While the comparison is late the retry waits (item 3), which is the REGISTER gate's accepted cost (#692 ruling §2: time, a gate opportunity and fees; item 8's ESTIMATE). A run with no comparator result waits, raises nothing and does not pass. A run made before B-692b merges is made again after it (R19-16).
    - ARDR item 3, the project's first BEAT (:539). If it uses the launch host's BEAT path, it is gated once BEAT-3b is in, the comparator runs on Regtest, and its link time counts in the ARDR item-3 slices (:1252). Before RM v15, a cohort whose first BEAT lies past its launch host's H0 + 4,095 fails closed (the settlement above). A settles this in the ARDR item-3 unit (ITEM3 CONFIRMATORY is not built, `ARDR_RUN_V1.md:1657`).
    - P5-002 on Signet (P5 item A9, `CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:391`). Under A9 (b) the launch host makes no REGISTER or BEAT, so the gate is not reached. Under A9 (a) the first BEAT needs the comparison admitted on Signet. Without it no BEAT is signed on Signet, and the run stops as an explained interruption, as an open Reorg67 lock on Signet does (:1133; `CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:513-517`). A adds the comparison's Signet arm to P5-002 (a)'s wait list at the A9 decision on 11-06 (`CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:449`). The FBO 7.9 samples follow from this (FBO §7.11).
    - In every campaign the Python side is RM's pinned container, run as a black box under a leg record (step-6 record §10-§11). Builder code never reads reference code.

Either FD-CT-105 is recorded, and its record id replaces "FD-CT-105 candidate" in the headings, headers and status lines of §14.19 and FBO §7.11 and in FBO §7.11's Not covered line, in the lines marked "Founder, FD-CT-105", and in the notes that name the cutover (045b:880, :881, :882, :1166, :1167, :1168 and :1262; FBO:69, :70, :80, :81, :95, :119, :201, :203, :206, :215, :218 and :221), while the other notes then read "Claude plan, §14.19"; or the lines marked "Founder, FD-CT-105" (the BEAT cutover in item 3, the segmented re-replay in item 5 and item 9's Q1 list) are taken out before the freeze. In their place goes a dated line: until a recorded decision, a BEAT has no cutover and is gated as a REGISTER is, at every height, and a mismatch releases only on a sequential re-replay from INITIAL (TDS:1656). A Python outage alone could then end the Heart, so A asks the Founder again before Class E. In that second branch the same change rewords to "no cutover until a recorded decision", or takes out, every Claude-plan line that rests on the cutover or on the Q1 acceptance, so that WB9 ends only on a MATCH chain, including: the Scope lines; item 2's "gets no cutover" and "a BEAT reaches E"; in item 3 the E, window, authority and re-broadcast bullets and the W-row reason; item 4's "a BEAT waits until E"; item 6's S2 "Its size then" heading; AL-10's E clause in item 7; item 9's precision list; item 10's `CutoverReplay` refusals; item 11's no-exit bullet; WB9's name, Start, End and Exit cells (then: at every height); R19-5's `CutoverReplay` cells, R19-6's E cells and R19-19's recompute figures; in FBO §7.11 the header, E, the availability rule's E (the confirmation then runs through the first BEAT's inclusion), the AL-10 condition's `CutoverReplay` clause and its action, the Bound and tests E cells, READY, Re-broadcast, Covered, Not covered and the 7.6 recompute lines; and the notes listed in the first branch. The FBO 7.5 Covered line and the 7.6 item 6 recompute are redone without the cutover. The rest of §14.19 and FBO §7.11 stays either way.

**This fails if** a bound in item 8 fails on the rig (item 8's last bullet then applies), or if a STATE link can be accepted without the host matching its start bytes to the stored verified state at that height. The ruling sets no date for RM v15. If v15 is late, the gate keeps those limits and A tells the Founder; nothing is relaxed to make up the time.

### 14.20 P5 item A9: scope (b) (2026-11-06, Claude decision; #258 issuecomment-6675019247)

- **Decision.** A decided P5 item A9 on 11-06 (`CT-P5_PHASE_PLAN_KO.md:391`). The scope is (b), a step down from the plan's default (a). A6 stays (a): one registration, after this freeze (`:388`).
  - The registered P5-002 run is the two Signet nodes, the replay and the watcher only. It covers S13's 86,400 s of continuous observation and S14's Node A/B agreement at genesis, H0−1 and H0, plus any reorg fork points and public report cutoffs.
  - S14's project REGISTER/BEAT candidate and accepted heights (`docs/tasks/P5-002_PRIVATE_SIGNET_ENDURANCE.md:19`) are recorded as not covered. They are not counted from Regtest CI either.
  - There is no Signet H0−1 Rust/Python comparison: the (b) rule of `REORG_STEP6_RECORD_V1.md` §13.6 and §14 applies, `signet` stays `MANIFEST_NETWORK`, and 1D3 Q17 lands on Regtest only.
- **Why not (a).** At main `0fa972f7`, (a) waited on nine units that had not opened, and they could not reach `main` before the P5-005 registration (11-13–11-16), which freezes P5-002's commands (`P5-002_PRIVATE_SIGNET_ENDURANCE.md:12`):
  - Signet transport (`RegtestTransport` refuses other networks);
  - the H0−1 Signet unit with RM's formats §1.1 edition (`check_network` refuses `signet`; `initial_prestate` refuses a non-Regtest genesis);
  - B-692b P2a, P2b and P3;
  - 555-a;
  - BEAT-3a-2, which refuses non-Regtest BEAT steps;
  - BEAT-3b.
- **Effects on this file.** The cited lines keep their text.
  - §14.8 `:1133` (the Signet Reorg67 lock line) has no object in P5-002, because no launch host runs there. It stays for a later Signet launch run.
  - (C2) `:1223`: the witness "the Class T rehearsal (P5-002 scope (a))" does not exist under (b). The other witnesses stand: the EXPLORATORY XD-1 runs and the L0 seat's item-2 and item-4 runs.
  - (H7) `:1225`: P5-002 makes no launch-host decisions on Signet under (b). The per-reorg claim check on Class T Signet applies wherever a launch host runs there.
  - §14.19 item 10 `:1805`: under (b) the gate is not reached, as that line already says.
  - R19-14 (`:1842`) is closed by this decision: the comparison's Signet arm is on no wait list. R19-21 (`:1849`) loses P5-002, which makes no BEAT under (b).
- **Registration (P5-005).** The registration carries the (b) scope line and the not-covered-heights line. A later Signet launch run that covers those heights comes after the nine units and needs its own registration. A brings its plan when BEAT-3b merges. Until then the P5 exit judgment (P5-004) records those S14 heights as open.

### 14.21 Freeze values, frozen files and budget records (2026-11-09, A; amends sections 0, 1.2, 1.3, 6 and 10, rows A5, A8, A9, F3, F10 and F13 of section 14.Z, FEE §2, §4, §6 and §8, and FBO §7.9)

- **Freeze-time node read.** The Founder read Node A at its console on 2026-11-09 (tip 975,182): `estimatesmartfee 2 CONSERVATIVE` 2,004 sat/kvB, `estimatesmartfee 6 CONSERVATIVE` 689, `getblockstats` 75th percentiles 2, 1, 3, 5, 2 and 6 sat/vB at 975,177–975,182, `incrementalfee` and `relayfee` 100 sat/kvB. Node B was not read; its read joins before the P5-005 registration as a confirmation line, and a larger or different value stops the registration until A re-decides (A8 :1182, A9 :1183).
- **Values.** `b` = 6,000 sat/kvB for REGISTER rung 0 and for a BEAT (the percentile input; operator floor 1,000, emergency floor 1,000). `r_inc` = 100 sat/kvB. `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb` = 1,464,000 (A5 :1179). `v` = 213 vB (A10 :1184). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification FZV-5): FEE V1 :34 means 1,000 × the largest per-block 75th `feerate_percentiles` value over the last 6 blocks, on each node, the larger of the two (A8 :1182; `crates/bitagi-wallet/src/fee_policy.rs:1037-1040`, `percentile_rate.max(rate)`, at main `59bc1e1d`). That overrides column 2's "per-transaction" wording; any V2 fixes the wording itself.*
- **REGISTER ladder (section 1.3) at `b` = 6,000, `v` = 213, `r_inc` = 100.** The BIP125 step is 22 sat and moves no rung. All eight rungs are at or below 30,000 sat (FD-CT-102); the cap would cut the ladder only for `b` above 8,243 sat/kvB.

  | rung `k` | target `r_k` (sat/kvB) | fee `f_k` (sat) | feerate `f_k` × 1,000 / 213 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 0 | 6,000 | 1,278 | 6,000 |
  | 1 | 9,000 | 1,917 | 9,000 |
  | 2 | 13,500 | 2,876 | 13,502 |
  | 3 | 20,250 | 4,314 | 20,253 |
  | 4 | 30,375 | 6,470 | 30,375 |
  | 5 | 45,563 | 9,705 | 45,563 |
  | 6 | 68,344 | 14,558 | 68,347 |
  | 7 | 102,516 | 21,836 | 102,516 *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification N3): this column rounds down. Rung 7's exact feerate is 102,516.43, and A5 :1179 and FEE :53 round up to 102,517 (the column's ceilings are 6,000; 9,000; 13,503; 20,254; 30,376; 45,564; 68,348; 102,517). A cap of 102,516 would refuse rung 7 under the product's exact check (21,836,000 > 21,835,908; `crates/bitagi-wallet/src/campaign_budget.rs:232-237`); `max_feerate_sat_per_kvb` 1,464,000 is unchanged.* |

- **BEAT shapes (FEE §8, SSoT:1798's BEAT size distribution).** A BEAT carries the current Anchor and exactly one fee input, key-path signed: 220 vB (weight 878) without change and 263 vB (weight 1,050) with one P2TR change output. These are the exact key-path weights of the product's shapes (`crates/bitagi-wallet/tests/fee_policy.rs:414-415`, `crates/bitagi-wallet/tests/beat_caps.rs:857-865`); no BEAT has a real-node record yet, since the BEAT act is held. The reserve prices every BEAT at 263 vB. The product now accepts more fee inputs (`crates/bitagi-wallet/src/beat.rs` `MAX_BEAT_INPUTS`; the signer allows 64); a BEAT with more than one fee input is outside this policy, and B proposes the unit that builds only one or refuses more (A #258 issuecomment-6743136008 item 3). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification N4): the tests assert 263 vB and 1,050 WU (`crates/bitagi-wallet/tests/fee_policy.rs:64`, `:414-415`); `beat_caps.rs:857-865` is the `beat_vsize()` helper with no literal, and 220 vB appears only in the T2b doc comment (`crates/bitagi-wallet/tests/beat_caps.rs:869-870`, "2,002 sat over 220 vB"). 878 WU is A's computation from the weight rule (`fee_policy.rs:418-419`): 1,050 − 172 WU for one 43-byte P2TR output, and ⌈878 / 4⌉ = 220 vB. No test asserts the no-change weight yet.* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C20): FEE :113-114's "an estimate" and :119's "measured vsize" read as this 263 vB, the planner's key-path rule (labelled `UNSIGNED_TX_TAPROOT_KEYPATH_ESTIMATE` in `b6000fz`'s result), equal to the product shape's weight (`fee_policy.rs:414-415`). No real-node BEAT exists yet. `b6000fz` gives BEAT replacements 3,000,090 sat, at least the 3,000,000 of FEE :119.*
- **Replacement in L1 (FBO :284, R2 Part 2 C7).** None. A BEAT not included by `A + 1,152` is replaced from L2 on under FEE §5. From `A + 1,152` the deadline `A + 2,016` is 864 blocks away, with a replacement allowed each block, and `max_beat_replacements` (100 for 60 BEATs) is not spent on normal-window variance.
- **Frozen files (sections 0 and 1.2).** Each hash subject is a separate file beside its draft in `docs/claude-track/preregistration/`; the drafts stay as the history.
  - `FEE_POLICY_V1_EN.md` is `FEE_POLICY_DRAFT_EN.md` at this commit with line 1's "(draft)" and line 3's DRAFT header replaced, and each "filled at freeze" or "PENDING Q1" cell given its value on the same line (:33-36, :53-55, :134, :155). Nothing else changes, so every `:N` of the draft is the same line in the frozen file. *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification FZV-11): besides giving cells their values, the frozen lines fold their dated notes into the cell text, and two cells are reworded: :53 has "at the freeze-time `b`" for "under the 044c-1 fee observations" (A5 :1179; the :100 and :1024 notes read "the 044c-1 fee observations" as the Q17 `getblockstats` read, which V1 :53 now names as the source of `p_high`), and :55 drops "(A's reading of the decision)" and credits 500,000 to FD-CT-102, which A1 :1175 records as kept "as drafted" (A's reading). :34 takes its Settled note's percentile definition into the cell (A8 :1182). "Nothing else changes" means no line moves and no other line changes.*
  - `FIRST_BEAT_OPERATIONS_V1_EN.md` is `FIRST_BEAT_OPERATIONS_DRAFT_EN.md` with lines 1 and 3 replaced the same way; it has no "filled at freeze" cell. Its UNMEASURED terms, M and the alarm bounds stay UNMEASURED assumptions, which the Founder approves as such at A3 (FBO :130). *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification C17): A3 was asked and answered on 2026-11-09 for the Class E record `e9cef7c2…`, which carries this FBO hash (`first_beat_operations_sha256`); that record is superseded by `82b5d263915470f1426c79fec56a898b7a6b9ed759373c625dbc1c4410ff5f4a`, which carries the same FBO hash, and the Founder re-approved it on 2026-11-09 (:942; #258 issuecomment-6745650687).* *Dated line 2026-11-09 (A, freeze verification FZV-1): in `FIRST_BEAT_OPERATIONS_V1_EN.md`, "FEE" (FBO :134, which names `FEE_POLICY_DRAFT_EN.md`) means `FEE_POLICY_V1_EN.md` (`5cd1057a…`), whose line numbers equal the draft's (:1892). So FBO's FEE citations, among them FEE:33 and FEE:33-36 (FBO :163, :164, :243, :268), read the frozen values; they are the exception to FBO :127's `1637b151` (:30 note). If FBO is ever rebuilt, its :134 names the V1 file.*
  - `DISPOSITION_POLICY_V1_EN.md` is 045a lines :383-:533 (from "## 3." through the end of §3.4, Option B, FD-CT-18), byte for byte, each line with its LF, at this commit (045a is unchanged since `b3dd7ba5`). - **ARDR policy (F10, F3, F13).** `b` = 6,000 for every experiment, `H0` = 1,000, `gates_per_run` ITEM1 200, POOL 4, ITEM2 1, ITEM4 1, ITEM3 17 per cohort, counts as :1250, the ITEM2 L0 seat 1, `p0_rule` `CEIL_2_5_B`, 24 attempts, `CONTINUE_WITHIN_LIMITS`, `budget_sha256` as above; the thresholds' one source is this file, and the method policy's `thresholds` is a copy that the tools check. B writes the policy bytes (B-FZ-pol, A #258 issuecomment-6743136008) with the seed `PENDING_FREEZE`. **Seed rule:** seed = SHA-256(ASCII `BITAGI_CT_ARDR_SEED_V1` ‖ 0x00 ‖ P ‖ H), where P is the 32-byte SHA-256 of the policy file with the seed `PENDING_FREEZE` and H is the 32 bytes of the `getblockhash` display hex of Bitcoin mainnet height H_s. A names H_s, the Node A tip plus 12, in the commit that adds P; the Founder reads H_s's hash on Node A; A writes the seed, the only change. P and this rule are fixed before block H_s exists, so the seed cannot be chosen. *2026-11-09 (A, B-FZ-pol): P = `39cdeb89d30f40782a77b83a0ee26dd5cb6470c4c09cf811db7313d75d094fd9`, the SHA-256 of `docs/claude-track/preregistration/ARDR_POLICY_V1_CLASS_T_REGTEST_FIRST.json` as B committed it (`c1baa8db`, 786 bytes). A separate A writer produced the same ITEM1, ITEM2 and POOL entries and the same method bytes. H_s = 975,205: Node A's last read tip, 975,182, plus 23. It is named in this commit, the first A commit after P on this branch.
